# P02M0103 - The graphics rendering foundation

Status: OPEN. It closes when every item below is done, and not before.

**THE FOUR QUESTIONS THIS FILE WAS WAITING ON WERE ANSWERED (2026-09-21), AND THE ANSWERS ARE HERE
BEFORE THE QUESTIONS SO A READER SEES WHAT IS DECIDED RATHER THAN WHAT WAS ASKED.**

    1.  image-convert's ceiling    Try to reach it, but NOT at the cost of what the backend
                                     produces. If it cannot be reached, leave it.
    1b. four-lane encode           REFUSED. "It must be universal for every platform" - the three
                                     ports stay one program, which is the property question 1b said
                                     the decision was really about.
    2.  MANAGE on yourself         ALLOWED. `SYS_PROCESS_SELF` is in, and loading code into
                                     yourself is refused on that handle - see the item for why the
                                     capability model draws the line there rather than at the whole
                                     of MANAGE.
    3.  f-ext and e                DO THEM.

AND ONE AND 1b TOGETHER DECIDE ONE. The lever named under question 1 is the output encode, and there
were exactly two ways to pull it: change the pixels, or compute the three channels at once. The first
is refused by "not at the cost of functionality" and the second by "it must be universal". What is
left is work that changes no pixel and no instruction set, and the arithmetic under this item already
says how far that reaches: the per-pixel FLOOR for any covered pixel is 33 ns, this scene covers the
frame TWICE, and 2 x 307,200 x 33 ns is 20.3 ms against a 16.7 ms budget. So sixty frames a second is
out of reach at this content with a sampler and a shader that cost NOTHING AT ALL - which is not a
tuning verdict but an arithmetic one, and it is why "if it cannot be reached, leave it" is the branch
this item takes. What the ceiling should instead be is a statement about what this system promises
and is recorded where the ceilings are.

**WHAT THIS MILESTONE WAS WAITING FOR, AS QUESTIONS SOMEBODY CAN ANSWER (2026-09-19).**

1. **Is `image-convert`'s ceiling sixty frames a second at this content?** The scene became two on
   the owner's answer to this item's earlier question, and both halves inherited 16.7 ms from the
   scene they came out of. `image-resample` at 16.7 is not in doubt - UI imagery at UI sizes. The
   half that draws two FULL FRAMES through a transfer function and a matrix is the one the original
   question was about, and it is still open.
   **AND IT HAS A MEASUREMENT UNDER IT NOW (2026-09-20) RATHER THAN AN ESTIMATE.** The per-pixel
   FLOOR of this backend is 33 ns: one full-screen opaque solid rectangle, one command, the backdrop
   decode already skipped, is 10.125 ms over 307,200 pixels. A 16.7 ms ceiling is 54 ns per pixel for
   everything. `image-convert` covers the frame twice over - 72.5 ms for its full-screen mipmapped
   draw and 54.7 ms for its full-screen YUV one, each measured alone - so it cannot reach 16.7
   whatever its sampler costs. The lever is named in `docs/PERF.md`: the floor is the OUTPUT ENCODE,
   three square roots and three table lerps per pixel, and removing them changes the pixels the
   conformance registry compares. That makes it a decision and not a tuning pass.
1b. **AND ONE OPTION UNDER QUESTION 1 THAT THIS FILE HAD NOT NAMED (established 2026-09-20).** The
   floor's own note says the lever is the OUTPUT ENCODE - three square roots and three table lerps
   per pixel - and that removing them changes the pixels the conformance registry compares, which
   makes it a decision. There is a second way to attack the same lever that does NOT change a single
   pixel: compute the three channels TOGETHER. A four-lane single-precision square root is one
   instruction on every target this system runs on, it is correctly rounded exactly as the scalar one
   is, and the lerp either side of it is multiply-add arithmetic whose lane-wise form is
   bit-identical as long as nothing contracts it into an FMA. So the encode could get cheaper with
   the conformance registry unchanged.

   WHAT IT COSTS IS NOT PERFORMANCE, IT IS A PROPERTY THIS TREE CURRENTLY HAS. There is no SIMD
   anywhere in it - not one architecture intrinsic in the kernel or in userspace - and the square
   root beside the encode carries a comment about going out of its way to make "the three targets
   agree by construction". A vector path is either written three times or written once for x86_64
   with the scalar path kept behind it, and either way it is the first place where the three ports
   stop being the same program. THAT is the decision, and it belongs to whoever owns that property
   rather than to an implementer who noticed a number. It is recorded here so the question under it
   is the real one: not "can the floor be met" but "is a per-architecture rendering path what this
   system wants".

2. **May a process hold a MANAGE capability to itself?** That is the whole of what parallel tile
   execution needs - the kernel already takes a caller-chosen entry and stack top, so there is no
   thread runtime to write - and it is the only structural route left to the 3D floor.
3. **Is `f-ext` activated, and is `e` approved?** Nine of the thirteen open items are Scene3D
   Extended, which this file says has no real consumer asking for it; the tenth is a list whose own
   item stays open until the part is approved.

Nine of thirteen are one unapproved part, and the rest is the two floors and the aggregate gate over
them. An implementer picking a ceiling to make a number look better is what this file refuses in as
many words, and it refuses it just as firmly when the implementer split the scene.

WHERE IT STANDS (2026-09-19): 137 ITEMS DONE, 13 OPEN, AND NOT ONE OF THE 13 IS BLOCKED ON MISSING
IMPLEMENTATION EFFORT ALONE. Counted by part, because the count on its own says nothing:

    part      open   what they are
    c            1   the enforceable 2D performance floor
    d            1   part d's aggregate Done gate, which asks for the conformance suite on ALL THREE
                       architectures and for `c`'s benchmark at its ceilings. THE PORTS ARE IN
                       (2026-09-19) and the three of them report the same counts, so what this row

    e            1   Render3D Core Profile 1, whose LIST exists in the machine-readable registry.

    f-ext        9   Scene3D Extended. ACTIVATED 2026-09-21, and every prerequisite it names is

    i            1   the 3D performance floor

**AND THAT TABLE'S READING CHANGED ON 2026-09-21.** Ten of the thirteen were work waiting for an
ACTIVATION this document did not grant itself, and the activation arrived: the owner was asked
whether `f-ext` is activated and `e` approved, and answered that both should be done. So those ten

AND EVERY PREREQUISITE `f-ext` NAMES IS MET, WHICH WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED. `P02M0103f` and
`P02M0103g` carry no open item, and `s-3d-ext` - which freezes the Extended EQUATIONS, LIMITS,
TOLERANCES and LIFECYCLE CHOICES - is done: the document exists, it is GENERATED from
`scene3d_extended.rs` rather than written beside it, and it carries its own specification hash. The

WHAT THAT DOES NOT MEAN IS THAT EVERY ITEM BELOW IS NOW ORDINARY WORK. The part's own header says it
carries a CLOSED ENUMERATED feature list, and it does not: adding one moves the frozen document's
hash, which makes the first thing this part owes an amendment to a normative specification rather
than an implementation. See the header below, where it is set out with what it blocks.

AND THE TWO PERFORMANCE FLOORS ARE MEASURED, RECORDED AND NOT MET. Both have their numbers and their
decomposition beside them and in `docs/PERF.md`; in one line each:
  - THE 2D FLOOR asks for FIVE scenes under two ceilings and has one. It was four until 2026-09-19,
    when the project owner answered this item's own question and `image-stress` became
    `image-resample` and `image-convert`. `UI-basic` is met (11.8 ms against 16.7). `vector-stress`
    is AT ITS LINE and not under it - a gradient hoist recorded under part `c` moved its median by
    about 0.35 ms, which is half a percent and smaller than the scene's own sensitivity to what else
    runs in the process: six runs after the suite gained its fifth scene read 67.1 to 67.7, all over.
    I recorded it as met on three earlier runs and that was wrong; the correction is under `c`. `image-stress` is
    11.4x over, down from 20.7x, and the item already says the fixture question - whether a video
    scene's ceiling is 60 Hz at this content, or whether the scene is two scenes - is "a decision for
    the project owner and not something an implementer may quietly adjust" - AND THEY ANSWERED IT ON
    2026-09-19: it is two scenes. `image-resample` is 80.6 ms against 16.7 and `image-convert` is
    127.1, which is 4.8x and 7.6x where the sum was 11.4x. `UI-effects` is 2.28x
    over, down from 2.8x, and what remains in it is a DIRECT GAUSSIAN whose every constant factor has
    been taken out: going faster means a box-blur approximation, and the profile specifies a
    Gaussian, which is a change to `Render2D Core Profile 1` rather than to this backend.
  - THE 3D FLOOR is 33 ms and the frame is 934 ms, and 2026-09-21 turned "keep optimising" into a
    decidable question. The handle that blocked parallel tile execution now exists - `SYS_PROCESS_SELF`
    - and the arithmetic of what parallel tiles can buy was computed from the measured decomposition
    before building it: geometry is 0.9 ms and everything else is 933.0, so the parallel fraction is
    99.9 per cent and the frame is `0.9 + 933.0 / workers`. THIRTY WORKERS is the first count under
    the floor, optimistically - perfect scaling, balanced tiles, no synchronisation, no bandwidth
    saturation. Said per fragment it is sharper: 2,109 ns a fragment now against 73 ns allowed, and
    the lit stage alone is 1,448 ns of it at 35 ns per IR statement. One core reaching 73 ns would
    need under 2 ns a statement, which is a JIT and not a tuned interpreter. SO THE FLOOR HAS TWO
    ROUTES - about thirty cores, or a compiled fragment stage - and both are decisions rather than
    effort.

WHAT AN OWNER COULD DO WITH THIS, IN THE ORDER THAT UNBLOCKS MOST: approve `P02M0103e`'s profile box
and activate `f-ext` (10 items), answer the `image-stress` fixture question and the Gaussian question
(1 item), and own a thread runtime or accept that the 3D floor is not reachable on one core (1 item).
The thirteenth closes when the other three do and the emulated ports have run.

P02M0096a AND P02M0097", and both of those are complete - so as written it authorised a ten-part
subsystem to start immediately. That is not what the roadmap says: `docs/todo/TODO.md` records that
server, general real-hardware, desktop and AI directions remain VISION rather than active product
milestones, and `docs/CONCEPT_EN.md` orders server before desktop and puts the desktop platform in
Phase 4. A dependency on two finished milestones is not an activation gate; this line is.

WHICH PARTS MAY START EARLIER, AND ON WHOSE APPROVAL. The whole is Phase 4, but three pieces are
genuine cross-phase foundations that other milestones already depend on, and each is separately
approvable:

    P02M0103s (the normative specifications)   may be written at any time; it produces documents
    P02M0103a-common (image and colour model)  what `P02M0136` and every renderer need; no display
    P02M0103b + c (Profile 1 and soft2d)       the 2D foundation a compositor is built ON, and
                                                 the renderer `P02M0136` proves text against

AND `a-wsi` IS NOT ONE OF THEM, WHICH THIS LIST CLAIMED AND THE ROADMAP NEVER SAID (corrected
2026-09-04). It was added here with a note that `docs/todo/TODO.md` "carries the same correction";
that file's P02M0103 line permits exactly `s`, `a-common` and `b`+`c` as cross-phase foundations and
does not name `a-wsi`. Two documents cannot give different answers about whether WSI implementation
may begin before the desktop phase, and the roadmap is the one that decides scope - so the entry is
withdrawn rather than the roadmap widened to match a claim made here. `a-wsi` remains Phase 4 like
the rest, and `d`, `g` and `i` remain gated on it.

Everything from `P02M0103e` onward - render-math, Render3D, scene3d, soft3d, the 3D conformance suite
and the interactive 3D application - is Phase 4 and is not approved by approving the 2D foundation.

AND THE SCOPE IS INVERTED RELATIVE TO WHAT PHASE 4 ASKS FOR, which is recorded here because it
decides what gets built first rather than merely how it is labelled. Phase 4's requirement is a
GUI/compositor, a window manager and shell, and accelerated graphics on the reference virtual
platform. This file DEFERS all three - the compositor, multi-application presentation, the widget and
accessibility stack, every Mesa/OpenGL/Vulkan path, virgl/Venus and virtio-gpu 3D execution - and in
their place makes a complete vector-effects engine, a custom programmable shader IR, a full software
3D pipeline and an engine-class retained scene layer MANDATORY before completion, with no mandatory
profile feature permitted to return `Unsupported`. (Animation, skinning, morph targets, PBR/IBL,
shadows, HDR and postprocessing have since been moved out of that mandatory set into `f-ext`.)
It is possible to spend years completing that and still not have the thing Phase 4 was defined to
deliver.

So the ORDER is fixed here, and the part boundaries now express it: the compositor-facing 2D
foundation (`s-common`, `a-common`, `s-2d`, `b`, `c`) is delivered and exercised FIRST, and
`a-wsi`/presentation and compositor integration do not GATE it - they hang off `s-wsi` and
`a-common` and are delivered beside `b` and `c`, on their own Done gate.
(The word was "follow", and three other places in this file read that as three different graphs -
the matrix kept one undivided `a` row, the build-order paragraph still said `s -> a -> b`, and the
`a` section said `a-wsi` runs beside `b` and `c`. "Beside, gating nothing" is the one that matches
the freeze section and the part headers. Corrected 2026-09-01.)
AND "GATING NOTHING" IS ABOUT THE TRACKS' API WORK, NOT ABOUT EVERY PART IN THEM (corrected
2026-09-02). `a-wsi` gates neither track's specifications or API work - which is what lets `b`, `c`,
`e` and `f` be finished with no window system - and it DOES gate the three parts that end at a
live surface: `d`, `g` and `i`. `h` also waits for `a-wsi` transitively through `g`; a headless
conformance suite cannot complete before the backend whose integration it tests. The 2026-09-01 correction put that edge in the matrix, said it had
put it in the build-order paragraph as well, and had not; the two then described different schedules.
All four places now carry the same sentence, and the canonical-review list at the end of this file
carries it too. `a` is divided into `a-common`
and `a-wsi` with a Done gate each, because the order above was impossible while one heading carried
both. The 3D API stays EXPERIMENTAL - it may be designed and
implemented, and it may not be declared stable - until it is co-designed with a concrete accelerated
backend or validated by a second implementation, because this file's own rule says a shape with
fewer than two real consumers is a guess and `render3d` has exactly one, `soft3d`, with every
accelerated implementation deferred. Scene-engine features are part `f-ext`, a separately activated
part with its own `Scene3D Extended Profile 1` and its own gates, driven by a real consumer rather
than sitting in Profile 1's mandatory set - and they are absent from every core work item,
conformance list, demo scene and aggregate Done clause, which is what makes "optional" mean
something.
And the title is narrowed with it, in the heading rather than only in this paragraph: this is the
graphics RENDERING FOUNDATION. Text is `P02M0136`, pointer and touch are elsewhere, and the
compositor and application platform are not here - so "the platform desktop and mobile applications
are built on" overstated what completing this file produces. `docs/todo/TODO.md` carries the same
name and the same correction.

The reconciliation record at the end maps the earlier plan corrections to their authoritative
sections. The correction checkboxes record decisions in this plan, not implemented graphics or frozen
specifications. The unchecked work below writes and freezes the normative profiles before any backend
implements them; there is one current contract for each item.

**REWRITTEN 2026-08-15 to be a single statement of the work.** It carried its outside reviews inline
for two rounds, and the third review's sharpest finding was about the document rather than the
design: the main text still said "24- or 32-bit depth" and "textures remain optional future work"
while a review section two hundred lines below said "pick one depth format" and "textures are not
optional" - both as open checkboxes, with nothing to say which was newer. The reviews are gone;
their conclusions are the items below and their reasoning is stated where it changes a decision.
Everything in this file is what to build.

A fourth pass closed items that required a value to be CHOSEN without choosing it. "State the
handedness", "a small cap", "pick a depth format" are not normative. Where such an item appears below
it now carries the number: `MAX_DAMAGE_RECTS = 16`, 8 subpixel bits, the clip-plane inequalities,
`MAX_CLIPPED_VERTICES = 9`, column-major `M * v` right-handed with `[0, 1]` clip depth and CCW front
faces.

A fifth pass drew the line those numbers need. "Two implementations must produce the same pixels" is
true of the INTEGER half of a rasteriser and is not achievable for the floating-point half - operand
order, FMA contraction, the libm in use and the optimiser all move the last bit. So the contract is
split and each item says which side it is on - and the split is PER DIMENSION, because 2D and 3D
coverage are not the same problem. In 3D, coverage is discrete sample-point classification against
quantised edges and IS bit-exact. In 2D, coverage is an antialiased area estimate, and this file
deliberately lets a backend choose its coverage algorithm - so 2D coverage is TOLERANCE-BASED while
2D path topology, clip classification and quantised geometry stay deterministic. Requiring a
particular coverage number from an unspecified algorithm was a contradiction between the split and
the antialiasing item. BIT-EXACT: geometry classification, clipping topology,
viewport quantisation, 3D coverage (sample-point classification), shared-edge ownership, and depth
clear, storage and comparison. 2D COVERAGE IS NOT IN THAT LIST and the word appeared unqualified
here for two passes while the paragraph above it said the opposite (corrected 2026-08-30).
TOLERANCE-BASED: interpolated floating attributes, filtering, shading and the final RGB conversion.

MARKED BY CONTRACT, NOT BY STATUS LINE (corrected 2026-08-30). The previous version of this matrix
read the milestones' `COMPLETE` labels and declared the WSI's prerequisites met except for one. A
status line is a claim about a milestone; what this file needs is a behaviour, and three of them are
qualified below in the current tree, irrespective of status labels:

    P02M0164   THE CATALOGUE MECHANISM AND A PARTIAL DISPLAY MIGRATION ARE IMPLEMENTED
                 (reviewed 2026-09-08). DisplayService receives `CATALOGUE`, subscribes to display
                 providers, opens a publication, releases scanout on driver-channel close and adopts
                 a later publication while its slot is empty. It also reports withdrawal frames.
                 The old claim that it still receives only a fixed `GPU` bootstrap handle is stale.
                 What remains for `a-wsi` is the full consumer contract: track provider identity and
                 generation, detach the matching withdrawn provider, retain/select alternatives,
                 fail over or reopen after channel loss, recover the subscription, and validate the
                 typed versioned display-device contract. The existing one-slot consumer ignores
                 another live provider while occupied and does not implement that complete policy.
                 P02M0099 and `a-wsi` continue to co-own this work: whichever first adds a second
                 display provider or WSI performs the remaining migration; the other consumes it.
                 There is no circular prerequisite on the migration this part itself owns. Reuse
                 the landed catalogue seam, and prove the remaining identity/failover behavior here.
    P02M0167   LIVE SELECTION/TAG PROOF PASSED; PRODUCER-SAFE ACQUISITION REMAINS OPEN
                 (reviewed 2026-09-08). Selection-specific kernels and loaders are staged under
                 build locks; ISO paths are content-keyed, assembly is serialized and cache hits
                 verify recorded output digests. USB/system-disk templates now have content-keyed
                 generations and run-private writable copies. Those repairs discharge their own
                 races; they do not snapshot every medium producer. `mkimage.sh` still reads the
                 shared init/volume packages, bootstrap files and manifest while the kernel build
                 lock is no longer held. An assembly lock and post-assembly key comparison detect
                 movement rather than stabilizing those inputs, including a same-content rewrite.
                 P02M0170's M1/M2 own that complete acquisition boundary and its producer/acquisition
                 mutation proof. UNTIL THAT BOUNDARY AND PROOF ARE COMPLETE, ACCEPTANCE RUNS ONE GUEST
                 ARCHITECTURE AT A TIME WITH NO OVERLAPPING SAME-ARCHITECTURE SELECTION OR
                 MEDIUM-INPUT PRODUCTION. Serial guest selection alone does not exclude a package,
                 bootstrap or manifest producer from rewriting an input during assembly.

                 The scheduler's registered host gate exercises the actual executor, including
                 prerequisite order, failed-descendant suppression, shared prerequisites, status
                 precedence, seeded cost and guest-slot accounting. Catalogue narrowing is also
                 repaired: `candidate::catalogue_losses` compares every runnable variant's
                 `(check, architecture, environment, configuration)` identity per covered component.
                 Activation calls `evidence_failures` before materialisation, so removing a variant
                 while keeping its check ID and `covers` still invokes the evidence requirements.
                 The real-registry regression
                 `a_candidate_that_narrows_only_the_registry_is_still_a_narrowing` constructs a
                 `host_configuration_unrunnable` narrowing and passes in this review. No catalogue
                 repair belongs in this milestone.

                 The actual live same-architecture selection/tag gate PASSED after the private-file
                 admission fix, as recorded by P02M0167's combined verification closure and
                 `/tmp/libersystem-final-concurrency-recheck.log`. Two x86_64 suites with disjoint
                 selections and `dma`/`domain` tags passed with distinct run logs; the production
                 `check-concurrent-selection.sh` checks each guest ran its own selected IDs and none
                 of the other's. That executed proof is discharged, not still owed or inferred from
                 host tests. It does not prove input stability during producer publication or
                 acquisition, staged-digest binding, or P02M0170's hostile replacement cases. The
                 remaining serial restriction above is for that unsatisfied producer/acquisition
                 boundary, not for absence of this completed live selection/tag check.
    P02M0141   UNMET, and unambiguously: its own status line says PLANNED, its Definition of Done
                 says no previously non-restartable service can be restarted safely, and
                 DisplayService is still `restart = "escalate"`.

The remaining prerequisite claims are narrow. P02M0153's non-coherent/DMA import contract is not
required by this CPU-only v1; such imports remain outside its admitted image set. P02M0163 supplies
the current discovery/bind model, not general hotplug discovery. P02M0166 supplies removal and graph
transitions, including refusal of enable during any planned stop; the prior dependency-stop hole is
repaired. P02M0162 supplies the teardown/rebind mechanism, but its M5 target-window measurement is
still handed to consumers: the 300/400/4000 constants were round counts and are not accepted timing
evidence merely because they are now consumed as ticks. Before accepting driver-loss/reacquisition
on a target, record its measured window and run the relevant recovery gate. This does not add a
second deadline mechanism or block writing the graphics specifications.

P02M0165 supplies the stop-and-drain implementation; its current evidence is qualified rather than
inferred from its status (reviewed 2026-09-08). `stop_all` drives nodes with bindings or pending
teardowns through `settle_shutdown_node`, sharing the normal event queue and Pending ledger. Each
pass reads ready process and claim confirmations before advancing or classifying expiry. A process
is ready when `wait(process, now.max(1)) == 0`; `wait_any` is only a bounded wake, so its zero-based
index is not discarded. The overall shutdown bound covers the existing stop allowance and teardown
fallback; unconfirmed outcomes land in quarantine. The earlier `ready > 0` defect is repaired.

The host regression covers shutdown selection with a binding, an earlier stop and a pending teardown.
The development-only `device_manager::tests::pending_shutdown_outcomes` additionally drives the
production settlement function with a sole ready handle and a non-ready timeout and checks the
confirmed/quarantined distinction. `dev-gpu-restart.py` requires its success marker. These are
implemented oracles; this plan review does not claim a fresh guest or mutation-test pass. `a-wsi`
acceptance must run the registered driver-restart gate against the tree it consumes and must not
infer teardown confirmation from a `STOPPED` message alone.

Withdrawal also has two different ownership cases. `driver_binding::apply_withdrawal` orders close
before announce, with host recorder tests for ordering and completeness. An unopened offered endpoint
is still catalogue-owned: the development-only `unopened_provider_withdrawal` test calls the actual
`Catalogue::withdraw_binding`, checks the real peer closes, checks no stale late-subscription replay,
and checks an old-generation withdrawal cannot close a replacement. It also decodes the withdrawal
frame. The driver-restart gate requires this oracle's marker; claiming that production close has no
test is stale. Once `open` transfers the offered endpoint, the catalogue no longer owns that handle,
and closing its entry cannot revoke the consumer's endpoint. Driver peer-close remains the data-path
loss signal. WSI must process both catalogue withdrawal metadata and peer-close idempotently, release
a withdrawn connection itself, and reopen a live matching provider by identity. It must progress on
peer-close even if an announcement is delayed or absent. Its own migration gate proves observable
withdrawal, detach and reacquisition; the existing tests do not prove that future consumer behavior.

`docs/todo/TODO.md` ticks P02M0141 as complete while its own file says PLANNED, which is a
discrepancy this file must not rely on in either direction. The WSI's restart and rebind semantics
are blocked on P02M0141 actually closing, and until it does this file may not describe them as
available. They are NOT additionally blocked on P02M0164 (corrected 2026-08-31): its consumer
migration is `a-wsi`'s own deliverable, so naming it here as a second block made the part wait for
work it performs itself.

AND ONE THING IS OWNED BY NOBODY, down from two (2026-08-30). The completion primitive is DECIDED -
ordinary channel pairs, TWO of them per SURFACE (corrected 2026-09-03: this said "per present", which
was the arithmetic before the endpoint became per-surface, see the present-queue item) - so it needs
no new kernel object, no ABI number and no owner beyond this file; pass 10's asynchronous submission
model shares that same decision.

What remains unowned is the SELF-THREAD RUNTIME, shared with `P02M0135`. It is no longer a
prerequisite of anything mandatory here: multithreading has been removed from `c`'s and `g`'s Done
conditions, because a completion condition that requires an unowned mechanism cannot be met and its
presence made both parts unschedulable. Tiled single-threaded rasterisers are conforming; the worker
pools are a later performance item that consumes the runtime when somebody numbers it.

## Who consumes this API, and who instantiates the backend

Added 2026-08-30, architecture premise corrected 2026-09-08. This file promises public `render2d`
and `render3d` APIs "for ordinary applications written by other people" and must name their access
boundary. `docs/CONCEPT_EN.md` defines native typed capability IPC/ABI as the default application
contract; WebAssembly/WASI components are the first recommended host on top of it, not the
application ABI itself. `docs/DYNAMIC_LINKING.md` separately limits the `.lslib` Rust ABI to an
internal system-image build optimisation, not a cross-release third-party ABI. The native IPC
contract therefore exists, but it does not by itself expose these in-process rendering APIs to
external applications. That graphics-specific boundary must be specified separately; an
in-process `DrawList` does not create it.

The answer this file takes, stated so it stops being ambiguous: **these are INTERNAL SAME-IMAGE RUST
APIs in v1.** Their consumers are in-tree applications and toolkits built from the same tree, and
that is what "public" means here - public within the image, versioned with it, not an ABI anyone
outside the release links against. A native SDK boundary or a Wasm-host graphics adapter, each with
its own version, resource, copy, lifetime and capability semantics, is a SEPARATELY APPROVED
milestone and is the only thing that makes the third-party claim true. Until one exists this file
does not make that claim.

AND SOMETHING HAS TO INSTANTIATE THE BACKEND. This file says applications never name `soft2d` or
`soft3d` and that only a backend depends on them, while deferring the OS graphics device and factory
vocabulary and naming no crate or service that selects and creates the software backend - so an
in-tree application must either name the backend directly, breaking the layering, or invent a
factory. v1 permits COMPILE-TIME SELECTION: one small composition crate owns the choice, every
consumer takes the trait, and the crate is the single place `soft2d` is named. A runtime
device/factory vocabulary with manifest and provider edges arrives with the first second backend and
not before, because that is the milestone that can test it.

AND THE 3D PROFILE IS NOT DECLARED STABLE AGAINST ONE BACKEND. The command, resource and submission model
is validated against a concrete SECOND backend - a GPU backend, or a deliberately different-shaped
second implementation - before "Profile 1 is stable" is said of it, by this file's own rule that a
shape with fewer than two real consumers is a guess.

A sixth pass changed what this milestone is FOR. Every pass before it improved the inside of a
document whose purpose was "enough graphics to prove a present queue and then draw a software cube".
The real purpose is a platform for a desktop environment, a mobile shell and ordinary applications
written by other people. That pass introduced `render2d` above `soft2d`, because `soft2d` was both
the public API and the CPU implementation of it, and it un-deferred paths, antialiasing, transforms,
clipping, layers, compositing and text - which are not advanced features a desktop wants eventually
but what an icon, a button, a scroll viewport and a line of text are MADE OF.

**A seventh pass finished that correction, because the sixth applied it to one dimension and not the
other, and because "complete" was still an adjective rather than a gate.** Two changes:

  - **`render3d` was missing, and its absence was the same mistake the sixth pass had just fixed.**
    The layering read `render-scene -> soft3d` and was described as the equivalent of
    `render2d -> soft2d`. It is not. `render2d` is a DRAWING AND COMMAND API; `render-scene` is a
    retained DATA MODEL. The 3D counterpart of `render2d` is a resource, pipeline, pass and command
    API - and with it missing, an application wanting a CAD viewport, a custom engine, a
    postprocessing chain, render-to-texture or a non-standard material had nowhere to go except the
    scene graph or the CPU rasteriser. The stack is now `scene3d -> render3d -> soft3d`, and a future
    GPU backend implements `render3d` rather than a whole scene graph.
  - **"Complete" is now two finite PROFILES rather than a word.** `Render2D Core Profile 1` and
    `Render3D Core Profile 1` each enumerate a closed list of features that the public API expresses,
    the software backend implements ENTIRELY, and a conformance suite covers. Inside a profile a
    backend may NOT answer `Unsupported`; that variant exists only for post-profile extensions. This
    is what converts "a complete software library" from an intention into something a gate can refuse.

That pass also removed three phrases that quietly capped the design: `conic gradient in the type
space even if the first backend refuses it`, `non-separable blend modes are deferred by name`, and -
the largest - `soft3d implements a subset and returns a typed Unsupported for the rest`, together
with the scene-model item that required hierarchy, passes, offscreen targets, instancing and skinning
only to be ADMISSIBLE rather than implemented. A library whose backend may decline its own API is a
specification for a demo.

**An eighth pass closed the contracts INSIDE those layers, and corrected a guarantee the seventh pass
had quietly broken.** The architecture did not move; four direct contradictions and one whole class of
under-specification did. The contradictions: the compositing line said "12 Porter-Duff operators" over
a list of thirteen (`Plus` is additive and is not one of them); the 3D profile named five primitive
topologies and the backend then promised "all six"; `sRGB8A8` sat in the format list while
`ColorSpace` already carried the transfer function, so two places could disagree about whether bytes
are encoded; and the 2D part required loading a real scalable font while the milestone that parses
font files, `P02M0136`, depends on this one - a dependency cycle in which each milestone waits for the
other or writes a throwaway parser twice.

The broken guarantee is the one worth naming on its own. The fifth pass promised BIT-EXACT geometry
classification and clipping topology, and that was achievable when the vertex pipeline was
fixed-function. The seventh pass added a PROGRAMMABLE vertex stage, whose output position is computed
by floating-point shader code that the same split puts on the tolerance side - so the input to a
bit-exact stage stopped being bit-exact, and two architectures could classify a vertex at
`z = +/-1e-8` differently and BOTH conform. A guarantee that its own inputs cannot support is worse
than no guarantee. The fix is to make the seam explicit: the clip-space POSITION output has strict
floating-point semantics of its own - fixed operand order, no FMA contraction, canonical NaN, stated
rounding - and is then QUANTISED into a canonical clip representation before any classification runs.
Everything downstream of that quantisation is bit-exact; every other varying stays tolerance-based.

The under-specified class is everything a profile entry names without defining: what a tone-mapping
operator or a dither actually is, what a blend factor set contains, where MSAA samples sit, what a
shader IR may express, what a resource descriptor holds. Those are listed below with values, because
a profile whose entries cannot be implemented twice the same way is a list of intentions.

**On scale, stated plainly rather than discovered later:** the profiles below are the work of a
compact 2D vector engine and a compact software 3D pipeline with programmable shading. That is a
subsystem, not an ordinary milestone, and the parts exist so it can be built and gated in
order rather than attempted at once. The build order is `s-common -> a-common`, then
`s-2d -> b -> c -> d` for the 2D track, and for the 3D one a BRANCH rather than a chain:
`s-3d -> e`, then `e -> f` and `e -> g` in parallel, both meeting at `h`, and `h -> i`. `h` additionally consumes `c` for the 2D/3D interop
scenes, and `i` inherits that backend for its HUD. `d` gates nothing in 3D and `e` can start as soon
as `a-common` and `s-3d` are done.
THE 3D TRACK WAS WRITTEN HERE AS `e -> f -> g -> h -> i` AND THAT WAS A LIST OF LETTERS, NOT A GRAPH
(resolved 2026-09-02). `f` is `scene3d`, a convenience layer that GENERATES command lists for the
API `e` freezes; `g` is `soft3d`, a backend that IMPLEMENTS that API. A backend waiting for a layer
built on top of it is backwards, `f`'s own header says it depends on `e`, and the canonical review at
the end of this file had already frozen the branched form. Three places said two different things
about whether `g` waits for `f`; they now all say it does not. `a-wsi` hangs
off `s-wsi` and `a-common` and blocks NEITHER TRACK'S API WORK: it is delivered beside `b` and `c`
rather than before them.
IT DOES GATE THE THREE PARTS THAT PRESENT, and this paragraph said "neither track" without that
qualification (corrected 2026-09-02 - the correction was claimed on 2026-09-01, was written and lost
before the file was saved, and the matrix and this paragraph therefore described two different
schedules for a day). `d`, `g` and `i` each wait for `a-wsi` IN ADDITION to the predecessor their
track's chain above gives them. The distinction is the whole reason the split exists: `b`, `c`, `e`
and `f` produce and conform APIs that draw into images and present nothing, and can be finished with
no window system at all; `d`, `g` and `i` each end at a live surface on a screen.
AND `h` IS NOT IN THE FIRST GROUP, WHICH IS AN ORDERING ERROR AND NOT A WORDING ONE (corrected
2026-09-04). What `h` DOES present is nothing - it is a conformance suite - but the matrix gives it
`g` AND `f` as hard prerequisites, `g` waits for `a-wsi`, and the matrix states in as many words that
the relation is TRANSITIVE. So `h` cannot complete without a window system, and listing it among the
parts that can was the two statements describing different schedules again, which is the defect the
2026-09-02 correction was made for. A conformance suite needs the implementation it tests; that
implementation presents; so the suite inherits the gate. What a part presents does not decide when it
may finish - its prerequisites do. The matrix is
where the complete edge set is recorded and is authoritative where a chain here is read against it.
(This said `s -> a -> b` and `s -> a -> e`, written when `a` was one part and `s` one freeze. Read
after the split it made all of `a-wsi` - presentation, the compositor seam, the whole WSI profile -
precede two APIs that present nothing, which is the opposite of the order the header fixes. Three
places said three different things about where `a-wsi` sits; they now say this one. Corrected
2026-09-01.) `s` comes first in both because it is the normative specifications, and a
backend written before its specification is the specification. What is NOT negotiable in either track is that no part is called done while its
backend can decline a command its profile requires.

Three things stay DEFERRED, all by the same rule this tree applies elsewhere - a shape with fewer
than two real consumers is a guess written down:

  - the graphics adapter/device/context/queue vocabulary, which had one consumer and it was the null
    backend written to exercise it. That is the OS-level device model, and it is distinct from
    `render3d`, which is a userspace rendering model with two planned implementations;
  - the generic timeline/fence capability. What presentation needs is a completion mechanism for
    presentation, which is specified in `a`; a system-wide timeline is worth extracting when GPU,
    video decode or DMA also want one;
  - the resource accounting for device-local bytes, contexts, queues, command bytes and in-flight
    submissions, none of which exist once the above are deferred.

The C/C++/TLS/Mesa substrate lives in `P02M0135` and the text shaping stack in `P02M0136`. Neither
gates anything here: `render2d` consumes an already-shaped `GlyphRun`, and the Rust renderers never
needed a C++ substrate.

The architectural split is deliberate: OpenGL/OpenGL ES/Vulkan are userspace API libraries; WSI,
presentable images, synchronisation, governed memory and access to a capability-scoped graphics
device are OS contracts; virgl/Venus/native-device protocols belong to a backend/driver. The software
renderers exercise the same window-system, resource-lifetime, resize and frame-pacing contracts but
remain ordinary CPU clients of DisplayService. They are not a fake OpenGL implementation and no
provisional `gl*`/`vk*` API is introduced merely to draw a demo.

The layering, once, so no item below has to restate it:

    application / UI toolkit / game / CAD / editor
         |                                    |
         |                                 scene3d          retained hierarchy, cameras,
         |                                    |             lights, materials, culling,
         |                                    |             animation, pass graph
      render2d                            render3d          resources, pipelines, passes,
    Canvas, DrawList, paths,                  |             shaders, command lists
    paints, clips, layers,                    |
    filters, glyph runs                       |
         |                                    |
      soft2d ... future gpu2d           soft3d ... future gpu3d
         |                                    |
         +------------ graphics-core ---------+
              images, colour, sampling, compositing
                            |
                   Surface / present queue
                            |
                      DisplayService
                            |
                      display driver

## P02M0103s - The normative specifications, frozen before any backend exists

**IT IS FIVE FREEZE POINTS, AND THEY ARE DECLARABLE** (2026-08-30, corrected to four 2026-09-01 and
to five 2026-09-04 - see `s-3d-ext` below, which `f-ext` needed and which nothing froze).
`s-common`, `s-2d`, `s-3d`, `s-wsi` and `s-3d-ext` have the freeze criteria below and individual
Done clauses. The first four govern the core; the fifth governs only the separately activated
Extended profile. None is declared frozen merely because its plan is complete.

**AND `s-wsi` WAS MISSING, WHICH LEFT THE WSI THE ONE NORMATIVE DOCUMENT NOTHING FROZE** (added
2026-09-01). This section's own rule is that a normative specification is frozen before the part that
implements it. `WSI_PROFILE_1.md` is a normative specification item of this section, and the still-open
WSI rows assign material contract choices to it - memory and cache ordering for shared surfaces, the
atomic surface-configuration rule, and pre-compositor multi-surface semantics - while `a-wsi`'s
prerequisites named `a-common`, P02M0141, P02M0165, P02M0166 and the completion object and no freeze
at all. Its Done clause asked only that those contracts be "documented", which is a weaker thing than
frozen and is exactly what a freeze gate exists to make checkable: an implementation could begin, and
the document could still change under it.

    s-common   `docs/GRAPHICS.md` and `docs/graphics/IMAGE_COLOR_PROFILE_1.md`: the layer names, the
                 image and colour model, the format and alpha sets, the coordinate spaces and the
                 canonical linear intermediate. FROZEN when both documents are written and reviewed
                 and the machine-readable format/alpha/colour registry they describe exists.
                 Releases `a-common`.
    s-2d       `docs/graphics/RENDER2D_PROFILE_1.md` and the `Render2D Core Profile 1` registry
                 entry. FROZEN when every mandatory command, paint, clip, layer, blend mode, filter
                 and image mode is listed with its expected behaviour and its conformance
                 requirement. Releases `b`.
    s-3d       `docs/graphics/RENDER3D_PROFILE_1.md`, `SHADER_IR_1.md`, `SCENE3D_PROFILE_1.md` (the
                 CORE scene profile) and their registry entries. Releases `e`.
    s-wsi      `docs/graphics/WSI_PROFILE_1.md`, and it is frozen only when the choices the WSI rows
                 leave open have ONE answer each: the scale-factor representation and the exact
                 logical-to-physical rounding rules, the surface event set, the present state
                 machine, damage semantics, the completion contract, the MEMORY AND CACHE ORDERING
                 for a surface shared between a client and the compositor, the ATOMIC
                 surface-configuration rule (which properties change together and what a partial
                 application means), and the pre-compositor MULTI-SURFACE semantics. Releases
                 `a-wsi`.
                 The five shared requirements are those in the per-part Done list, including
                 numerical minima and tolerances. No implementation starts against a draft.

    s-3d-ext   `docs/graphics/SCENE3D_EXTENDED_1.md` and the `Scene3D Extended Profile 1`
                 registry. Freezes the Extended feature equations, limits, tolerances and lifecycle
                 choices on the same five requirements as the other freezes. Releases only
                 `f-ext`; no core freeze or Done condition depends on it.

A freeze is declared only when the document and registry exist and all five requirements in the
per-part Done list pass. Document presence or matching feature names alone does not freeze unresolved
constants or tolerances. Corrections to a frozen profile follow the semantic-version/hash rules below;
the dependent part may start only against that declared profile.

A ninth pass found a defect that is about this document rather than about the design, and it is the
same shape as the one the third pass found: the file had grown a large number of sentences of the
form "ONE named tone-mapping operator", "the exact 2x and 4x sample positions", "a stated number of
anisotropic taps", "either strict semantics or quantise through one rule - choose one and say which",
"sampling while rendering is refused or defined". Every one of those correctly identifies something a
specification must decide. **None of them decides it.** A milestone that enumerates the open
questions reads like a specification and gates like a wish, and two implementations reading it still
produce different pixels - which is the exact failure the fourth pass was written to end.

The answer is NOT to inflate this file with matrices, kernels and sample coordinates. It is to admit
what this file is - a ROADMAP and a definition of done - and to put the actual values in normative
documents that are written and FROZEN BEFORE the backends they govern. `docs/GRAPHICS_2D.md` and
`docs/GRAPHICS_3D.md` currently appear as the last item of the last part, which is backwards: a
specification published after the thing it specifies is a description.

- [x] Write `docs/graphics/IMAGE_COLOR_PROFILE_1.md` and freeze it before `P02M0103a` builds anything
  on it. It carries what the colour item only promises: the Bradford adaptation matrices, the
  reference white luminance, the named tone-mapping operator WITH its equation and parameters, the
  gamut-mapping equation, the exact PQ and HLG transfer functions and inverses, the ordered-dither
  matrix and its phase, and the half-float rounding, subnormal, NaN and infinity policy. It also
  specifies the valid `PixelStorage x ImageSemantics x operations` matrix, allocation/padding rules,
  and the three separate byte-span quantities defined in `a-common`.

  Its YUV chapter fixes NV12/I420/P010 plane count and order, per-plane extent and pitch, odd-extent
  rules, P010 bit placement and byte order, BT.601/709/2020 coefficients, full/limited nominal ranges,
  chroma siting and reconstruction filter, crop alignment and applicable PQ/HLG metadata. Plane
  reconstruction and the YUV matrix produce encoded RGB before transfer decoding, primary conversion
  and premultiplication; freeze that order and its numeric rules rather than applying the RGB-only
  sampling recipe directly to YUV bytes. Missing or short planes and invalid layouts are typed
  refusals. All per-plane sizes use checked arithmetic and exported padding is canonical.

  DONE 2026-09-12, AND IT IS GENERATED RATHER THAN WRITTEN. The document carries the matrices, the
  transfer constants and the rounding rules that decide whether two implementations produce the same
  pixels, which makes it the worst thing in this tree to let drift from the code - so the REGISTRY is
  the source and the document is written from it, with a SHA-256 over its canonical form. A change to
  a constant is a change to the document in the same commit and a line in a diff.
  WHAT IS FROZEN: the twelve storage formats with their channel order, widths, encoding and byte
  count, and the canonical premultiplied linear `R16G16B16A16_FLOAT` intermediate named rather than
  implied; the alpha modes each format admits, which FALL OUT of what its channels are rather than
  being listed per format and getting out of step - an alpha-only format admits `straight` alone,
  because premultiplication is a relation between colour and alpha and there is no colour; the six
  image semantics and the operations each admits; the three primary sets and the eight colour spaces;
  sRGB with its linear segment; PQ's constants as the TWELVE-BIT FRACTIONS the standard states, not
  rounded decimals; HLG's three; the Bradford matrix and its inverse; diffuse white at 203 cd/m²;
  extended Reinhard on luminance as the one tone-mapping operator, with its parameter; hue-preserving
  desaturation by bisection in a FIXED sixteen steps as the gamut-mapping rule; the Bayer 8x8 dither
  matrix with its phase anchored to the TARGET's origin; the rounding at every boundary including
  half-float subnormals, NaN and infinity; the three YUV layouts with plane order, per-plane pitch,
  bit placement and odd-extent rule; the three matrices, both ranges at both depths, chroma siting
  and reconstruction; THE ORDER OF OPERATIONS, which is the part an RGB recipe gets wrong; the
  allocation and padding rules; the HDR metadata each transfer function needs; the guaranteed minima
  and the conformance tolerance for every comparison, so "passes conformance" has a boundary rather
  than a judgement; the initialisation contract, where the uninitialised case is a DISCLOSURE rather
  than an aesthetic problem; and the three byte spans.
  A CORRECTION MADE THE SAME DAY: the first version froze the wrong three spans - the minimum row,
  the pitch and the visible bytes. The three `a-common` names are `minimum_visible_bytes`,
  `backend_access_span` and `allocation_len`, and they are three different QUESTIONS: what a borrowed
  CPU view needs, what the selected display or DMA backend may touch including the final row's pitch
  padding, and what the allocation actually is. The row quantities are stated beside them as what the
  spans are computed FROM. An implementation storing one number answers all three with it and is
  wrong about two.
  THE FIXTURES CHECK THE VALUES RATHER THAN THEIR PRESENCE, which is what a transcribed constant
  needs: sRGB's two segments must MEET at their thresholds, PQ's `c1 = c3 - c2 + 1` identity must
  hold, HLG's `b` and `c` must be the derivations of `a`, the Bradford pair must actually be
  inverses, the dither matrix must be a permutation of its own range, the ten-bit YUV ranges must be
  the eight-bit ones scaled, and the matrix must be applied BEFORE the transfer function is decoded.
  15 fixtures in the profile crate, 15 passed; `check-graphics-profile` regenerates and compares.
  WHAT `s-common` STILL NEEDS BEFORE IT IS FROZEN: `docs/GRAPHICS.md`, the other half of this freeze
  point.

- [x] Write `docs/graphics/WSI_PROFILE_1.md`: the scale-factor representation and the exact
  logical-to-physical rounding rules, the surface event set, the present state machine, damage
  semantics, and the completion contract. Include the atomic `SurfaceConfiguration` lifecycle,
  coherent CPU-memory restriction and release/acquire ordering, and the pre-compositor visibility
  rule defined in `a-wsi`. Freeze them before that implementation begins.

  DONE 2026-09-12, GENERATED FROM ONE REGISTRY AND HASHED. The scale is a RATIO with the edge rule
  computed from it in integers - a fractional scale two sides round differently is the one-pixel seam
  nobody can reproduce - the authoritative extent is named, damage rounds outward and says it is
  conservative rather than a promise of non-overlap, and the inverse input mapping is frozen beside
  it because they are one decision. The configuration is TWELVE fields in one atomic snapshot with
  the five that invalidate an image generation marked, so a client never applies a new scale with an
  old extent. Six surface events, four image states and EIGHT transitions - including the abandon
  edge a client that acquires and then decides not to draw needs, and one resize answer per state.
  `acquire_next` is NONBLOCKING and says why: one dispatch loop serves every client, so blocking
  inside the handler stops the loop that would deliver the release. Four present outcomes, because
  "every present is displayed" cannot hold for a background client that must also not be blocked
  forever. The word "displayed" is split into THREE facts with the third marked NOT observable on the
  current backend, which is what stops a flush acknowledgement being reported as a presentation
  timestamp. Sixteen damage rectangles with an answer to every question a caller asks at the cap, and
  the rule underneath them: every presented image contains a COMPLETE valid frame and damage is a
  hint. Two completion channel pairs with their rights split. Two fixtures hold the machine closed
  and the numbers frozen; the document is generated and hashed like every other profile.

- [x] Write `docs/graphics/RENDER2D_PROFILE_1.md`: the equation for every Porter-Duff operator and
  every blend mode including the non-separable four with their luminance and saturation models and
  gamut clipping; the flattening tolerance as a NUMBER, the maximum subdivision depth as a number,
  and the projective `w` epsilon as a number with the horizon rule chosen; the boolean-operation
  answers rather than the boolean-operation questions; the per-node filter contract; the LCD gamma;
  and `Render2DLimits` with its guaranteed minima. Freeze the prepared-list compatibility and
  mutable-content rules, and the bounded reusable-builder contract defined in `b`.

  DONE 2026-09-12, GENERATED FROM THREE REGISTRIES. The feature LIST already said what the profile
  carries; this says what each entry MEANS numerically, which is the half two implementations can
  agree on in name and differ on in pixels.
  THE FACTORS ARE THE OPERATOR. All thirteen are written as their `Fa`/`Fb` pair under one equation,
  which is what makes them checkable against one another rather than thirteen paragraphs - and a
  fixture holds every pair DISTINCT, because two operators with the same factors are one operator
  under two names. `Plus` is not among Porter and Duff's twelve and is there anyway: additive light is
  what a glow and an emissive overlay are, and an implementation without it grows a private one.
  THE TWELVE SEPARABLE BLEND MODES with their equations, and the two with a DIVISION state their
  endpoints rather than leaving them to whatever the division produces. `SoftLight`'s `D(Cb)` is a
  piecewise function of the BACKDROP with its threshold on the backdrop, which is the part that is
  got wrong.
  THE FOUR NON-SEPARABLE ONES CARRY THEIR WHOLE COLOUR MODEL: `Lum`, `Sat`, `SetLum`, `SetSat` and -
  the step the mode's name does not imply - `ClipColor`, which PRESERVES LUMINANCE and reduces chroma
  rather than clamping each channel, and which every implementation that omits it gets wrong on
  saturated colours. `Lum`'s coefficients are the compositing specification's own fixed triple and
  stay fixed in every colour space: using the destination's luminance would make `Luminosity` give a
  different result for the same two colours depending on which space they were tagged with.
  THE GEOMETRY NUMBERS ARE NUMBERS: a flattening tolerance of a quarter of a PHYSICAL pixel - physical
  because flattening in logical pixels makes a curve twice as coarse on a two-times display, visible
  as facets on exactly the screens that show them best - a maximum subdivision depth of sixteen with a
  STATED outcome at the limit that is not a refusal, a projective `w` epsilon, and the horizon rule:
  CLIP IN HOMOGENEOUS SPACE BEFORE DIVIDING, because dividing first produces a vertex at ten million
  pixels and a rasteriser that spends a second on one triangle. The point-coincidence epsilon is a
  SEPARATE and smaller number, because merging vertices a quarter of a pixel apart collapses thin
  features that were meant to be there.
  THE BOOLEAN ANSWERS RATHER THAN THE BOOLEAN QUESTIONS, all eight the plan asks: self-intersections
  resolved first, an open subpath closed rather than refused, operands resolved under their own fill
  rules so the two never have to agree, the result POLYGONISED and saying so, one tolerance, a
  canonical ordering and winding stated in the device's y-down space, degenerate edges dropped with an
  empty result rather than a refusal, and determinism as a property of the input bytes alone.
  THE LCD NUMBERS the word "LCD" does not carry: the five-tap FIR and the gamma coverage is blended
  through - blending it linearly makes light-on-dark text look bolder than dark-on-light, which is the
  artefact reported as "the font renders too thin" and is a gamma question rather than a font one.
  AND THE CONTRACTS A RECORDING API HAS THAT A DRAWING API DOES NOT: the eight dependencies a prepared
  list is bound to, each with the reason a change invalidates it, so `is_compatible` is a list to
  check rather than a judgement and a test can change one ALONE; a mismatch as a TYPED requirement
  rather than a silent re-prepare, because a caller getting a full preparation sixty times a second
  has a performance bug it cannot see; CONTENT IS NOT STRUCTURE, so a new video frame refreshes one
  cache and re-flattens nothing; the reusable builder allocating nothing within its reservation and
  refusing BEFORE any replay begins; and the per-node filter contract, whose bounds map is why a blur
  over a small dirty region does not cost a full-screen blur.
  28 fixtures in the profile crate, 28 passed.

## P02M0103a - Common graphics platform

The common image model and WSI are separate sub-parts. Their individual prerequisites and consumers
are the matrix above; neither requires a GPU rendering backend.

**AND IT IS TWO PARTS FOR ORDERING PURPOSES, `a-common` AND `a-wsi`** (2026-08-30). The header of this
file says the compositor-facing 2D foundation is delivered first and WSI follows it, and that was
impossible as the parts stood: WSI, surfaces, presentation, lifecycle and accounting all live in `a`,
and `b` depends on the whole of `a` - so the 2D renderer was blocked on WSI, on P02M0141, and on a
completion primitive nobody owns. A stated order that the part boundaries contradict is not an order.

    a-common   the image and colour model, `graphics-core`, the shared conversion, sampling and
               compositing, the typed error, the resource vocabulary. Nothing here needs a display,
               a driver, a service restart or a completion object.
    a-wsi      surfaces, the present queue, presentation timing, the typed display-device interface,
               the client-Domain accounting and the lifecycle. This is what needs P02M0141, the
               completion primitive and the provider reacquisition path.

`b` depends on `a-common` and its own `s-2d` freeze, and not on `a-wsi`. `a-wsi` runs beside
`b` and `c` and is required before any part that presents.

**AND THEY ARE ACTUAL SUBSECTIONS WITH THEIR OWN DONE GATES** (2026-08-30). The previous round called
the split "a dependency statement" and left every item in one interleaved list with one combined Done
condition - which gave no objective point at which `a-common` could be declared complete and release
`b` while `a-wsi` was still blocked on restart and reacquisition work. An ordering that cannot be
declared is not an ordering. The items below are divided under the two headings, each item belongs to
exactly one of them, and the Definition of Done at the end of this file carries a separate condition
for each.

This is the split the previous round rejected as "the parts already are the split". That answer was
right about `s`-through-`i` and wrong here: `a` was carrying two things with different prerequisites,
and one of them was blocking the other for no reason but the heading they shared.

### P02M0103a-common - the image, colour and conversion model

Depends on `s-common`. Needs no display, no driver, no service restart and no completion object.
This is the half `b` waits for.

- [x] Write `docs/GRAPHICS.md` first, and name the layers in it, because "framebuffer" currently
  means five things: the UEFI boot surface in `bootproto`, the virtio-gpu resource's DMA backing,
  the DisplayService scanout, an application's surface, and `pix::Target`. FRAMEBUFFER is the legacy
  linear boot surface only; SCANOUT is what the display controller is showing; IMAGE is any 2D
  region of pixels; SURFACE is a presentable application object; RENDER TARGET is an image a
  renderer draws into. Freeze the ownership diagram above and the future integration routes -
  OpenGL/ES through EGL and a Mesa state tracker, Vulkan through a loader and ICD with Venus as the
  likely transport, DisplayService as the WSI and presentation owner, the display driver as device
  transport - and record which boundary validates every untrusted length, opcode and resource
  reference. Do not invent a common GL/Vulkan command language or expose virtqueue descriptors to
  applications.

  DONE 2026-09-12, AND GENERATED. Its core is a NAMING decision, and a naming decision written in
  prose drifts the first time somebody adds a sixth meaning - so the five names, the ownership edges,
  the integration routes and the validation boundaries are a registry, the document is written from
  it, and a SHA-256 over the canonical form makes a change to any of them a line in a diff.
  THE FIVE NAMES: `FRAMEBUFFER` is the LEGACY LINEAR BOOT SURFACE and nothing else - it was the word
  that meant everything and it is now the narrow one; `SCANOUT` is what the display controller is
  showing; `IMAGE` is any 2D region of pixels with a layout and a meaning; `SURFACE` is a presentable
  application object; `RENDER TARGET` is an image a renderer is drawing into. Each carries its OWNER,
  which is the question "whose bug is it" reduced to a field.
  THE OWNERSHIP IS AN ENUMERATION RATHER THAN A DIAGRAM. A diagram is checked by whoever reads it; a
  list of edges is checked by a fixture, which holds the graph ACYCLIC - a cycle is the failure that
  turns a stack into a knot, and it is the one a picture never shows. Every edge states what crosses
  it, because an edge with no payload is a dependency nobody has thought about.
  THE INTEGRATION ROUTES ARE FROZEN BEFORE ANY OF THEM EXISTS, because a provisional `gl*` or `vk*`
  API introduced to draw a demo is the API the tree then has. OpenGL and OpenGL ES arrive through EGL
  onto a Mesa state tracker; Vulkan through the Khronos loader and an ICD with Venus the likely
  transport; presentation through DisplayService; the device through the display driver. Each route
  names what it REFUSES, because the absence is the decision - and the two the plan names by name, a
  common GL/Vulkan command language invented here and virtqueue descriptors reaching applications,
  are refused in the registry where a fixture can see them.
  AND EVERY UNTRUSTED LENGTH, OPCODE AND RESOURCE REFERENCE HAS A NAMED BOUNDARY. The point of naming
  one is that the layer below may then ASSUME: a stack where every layer re-checks is one where the
  check that matters is the one nobody wrote because everybody assumed somebody else had, and a stack
  where none does is the other failure. Six boundaries, including the one a driver is most likely to
  skip - what a DEVICE writes back is untrusted input too, and a reply is not trustworthy merely
  because it came from hardware.
  22 fixtures in the profile crate, 22 passed.

- [x] Define the canonical single-plane image model separately from future GPU descriptors:
  DONE 2026-09-12, IN `graphics-core` - and the nine items above it are one crate rather than nine,
  because a layout without a format set, an alpha rule, a colour model and a meaning is a struct
  nobody can validate.
  THE PROFILE IS THE LIST AND THE CRATE IS THE CODE, and neither restates the other. Every format,
  colour constant, byte-span rule and operation table comes from the frozen registry, and a fixture
  holds the enumeration and the registry to describing the same twelve formats in the same order with
  the same widths and the same alpha rules. A second copy of a list is a second answer.
  THE THREE BYTE SPANS ARE IMPLEMENTED AS THREE. `minimum_visible_bytes` EXCLUDES the last row's
  padding, so a buffer that is exactly big enough for the image it holds is accepted - a validator
  demanding `pitch * height` refuses it. `backend_access_span` takes whether the backend reads that
  padding as an ARGUMENT rather than assuming: assuming it always does makes every presentable image
  own bytes it does not need, and assuming it never does is the out-of-bounds read. An `OwnedImage`
  allocates the backend span, because an allocation one row-padding short is an out-of-bounds read by
  HARDWARE, which no bounds check in this process can catch.
  TWO COORDINATE SPACES AND TWO TYPES. The drawing space is `f32` and SIGNED, because an unsigned
  parameter makes "half a pixel to the left of the origin" unrepresentable and because geometry has
  to clip off the left edge as naturally as off the right; the pixel space is what damage, a scissor,
  a surface extent and everything on the wire is in. Both HALF-OPEN, so adjacent rectangles tile - a
  closed rectangle is why two adjacent damage regions redraw a shared column twice, which is
  invisible until it flickers. A NaN extent is EMPTY, and saying so needs the comparison written out:
  every comparison with NaN is false, so a plain `<= 0.0` makes a rectangle nobody can place count as
  something to draw.
  THE COLOUR MATRICES ARE DERIVED AND NOT TABULATED. A tabulated matrix is a fourth place the
  primaries live and the first one somebody updates without the others; the derivation is arithmetic
  over the chromaticities the profile already froze. The fixture checks it by the property every
  correct derivation has - the primaries must reproduce their own white point - and by a round trip,
  which is what catches a TRANSPOSED matrix, since a transposition is still invertible.
  THE CONSTRUCTOR IS THE POINT. What `ImageView` replaces is an application computing a length and
  building an aliasing mutable slice out of a raw pointer, which is unsound whenever the length is
  wrong and is exactly as easy to write when it is. A view that cannot be constructed from a short
  buffer cannot be used over one.
  AND AN OWNED IMAGE IS ZEROED, which is a security property rather than a courtesy: a buffer recycled
  between Domains holds whatever the last one put in it, and an image whose bytes were never written
  shows that - a disclosure that reads as a flicker and is therefore never reported. Export ZEROES the
  padding rather than trusting it to have stayed zero, because a drawing routine that wrote a whole
  pitch is not a bug and its padding is still not content; two exports of one image are then the same
  bytes, which is what makes it hashable and a golden comparison meaningful.
  9 fixtures in `graphics-core`, 9 passed. Its allocation is fallible, because infallible allocation
  in userspace ends the process - and an image is exactly the allocation an application is most likely
  to ask for too much of.


      struct ImageLayout {
          extent: Extent2D, pitch: u32, storage: PixelStorage,
          origin: RowOrigin, semantics: ImageSemantics,
      }
      enum ImageSemantics {
          Color { color_space: ColorSpace, alpha_mode: AlphaMode },
          Data, NormalMap { convention: NormalMapConvention }, Depth,
          Mask { interpretation: MaskInterpretation },
      }

  Alpha compositing exists only in `Color`; non-color semantics cannot carry an ignored alpha or
  color-space field. `IMAGE_COLOR_PROFILE_1.md` defines the valid storage/semantics/operation
  matrix, including mask interpretation, and checked constructors enforce it.

  Every offset and size uses checked arithmetic. `minimum_visible_bytes()` is exactly
  `(height - 1) * pitch + minimum_row_bytes`, for a nonempty validated extent. A borrowed CPU view
  needs only that span, so a legal final row without padding is accepted. `backend_access_span()`
  separately states every byte the selected display or DMA backend may touch, including final-row
  pitch padding if it accesses that. `allocation_len()` is the actual owned allocation length.
  A presentable or DMA image must own at least its declared backend span; a driver may never touch
  beyond that span. Padding is not pixel content. Allocation size is not a second stored answer to
  the CPU view's computed minimum.

  A terminal raster and a decoded PNG need none of mip levels, array layers, sample counts, tiling,
  usage or memory domain - those belong to a `GpuImageDesc` that a later backend milestone adds ON
  TOP of this layout, sharing its format and extent. Building the ordinary image model out of the GPU
  one replaces four over-specific representations with one over-general one.

- [x] Use TWO coordinate spaces and say which is which, because a platform that only has integer
  pixel coordinates cannot do HiDPI, fractional scaling, zoom, rotation or smooth animation - and
  those are not desktop luxuries, they are what a phone does on every orientation change. The PUBLIC
  drawing coordinate is `f32` and SIGNED: geometry has to be positionable between pixels and has to
  clip off the left and top edges as naturally as off the right and bottom, and an unsigned integer
  parameter makes "half a pixel to the left of the origin" unrepresentable. Integer coordinates stay
  INSIDE the rasterising backend, where quantisation is defined. Both kinds are HALF-OPEN,
  `[x, x + width) x [y, y + height)`, so adjacent rectangles tile without overlapping and an empty
  rectangle is width or height zero. Presentable surfaces have a fixed `TopLeft` row origin in v1,
  with `RowOrigin` on the layout for images that are not presentable.

  Give the two spaces two TYPES, because one `Rect` cannot serve both and the version that tried was
  wrong at one end or the other: a damage rectangle crossing an IDL boundary must be whole physical
  pixels, and a drawing rectangle must be fractional and signed. So `PointF`/`RectF` (`f32`) for
  geometry, transforms, paths and drawing bounds; `PixelOffset` (`i32`) and `PixelRect` (`u32`) for
  damage, post-rasterisation scissor, surface extents and everything on the wire. `liber:graphics@1`
  carries `pixel-rect` and never the float one - a wire format with an `f32` rectangle whose meaning
  depends on a transform stack the receiver cannot see is not a wire format.

- [x] Carry the FORMAT SET both profiles need, not only the ones a scanout accepts. Eight-bit RGB is
  what a display takes; it is not what a mask, a glyph cache, a filter intermediate, a wide-gamut
  composite or an HDR target is made of, and adding those formats after the compositing code exists
  means rewriting the compositing code. The mandatory set:

      A8_UNORM            coverage masks, glyph masks, alpha-only layers
      R8_UNORM            single-channel data images
      R8G8_UNORM
      B8G8R8X8_UNORM      scanout
      R8G8B8X8_UNORM      scanout (GOP PIXEL_RGB)
      B8G8R8A8_UNORM
      R8G8B8A8_UNORM      encoding is never a second storage format - see below
      R10G10B10A2_UNORM   wide-gamut scanout
      R16G16B16A16_UNORM
      R16G16B16A16_FLOAT  the canonical intermediate
      R32_UINT           integer data and object-ID images
      R32G32B32A32_FLOAT  full-float CPU-visible images (RGBA32F in the 3D shorthand)

  `graphics-core::PixelFormat` owns all single-plane CPU-visible storage described by `ImageLayout`.
  `render3d::DepthStencilFormat` owns specialized depth/stencil attachment storage, and
  `render3d::TextureFormat` wraps the common formats and specialized set with an explicit per-format
  capability table. It must not redeclare an unrelated private list for the same storage.

  And name the canonical INTERMEDIATE explicitly, because the choice is invisible until the artefacts
  appear: layers, filter intermediates and offscreen composition are premultiplied LINEAR
  `R16G16B16A16_FLOAT`. Repeated compositing through 8-bit sRGB intermediates bands and loses
  precision, and a blur over an 8-bit intermediate is where it shows first.

  A second arm handles what firmware hands over. `src/uefi/src/gop.rs` accepts `PIXEL_RGB`,
  `PIXEL_BGR` and `PIXEL_BIT_MASK` with validated contiguous disjoint channel masks whose ELEMENT
  SIZE comes from the masks, so 16- and 24-bit modes are live; `bootproto::Framebuffer` carries that
  as `bpp` plus six shift/size fields and `term::Geometry`/`term::Raster` carry it again. Narrowing
  the model to fixed formats would reintroduce a defect this tree already fixed - the comment at
  `gop.rs:72` records that a literal `32` there gave a firmware-reported 24- or 16-bit layout "a
  diagonal smear rather than a picture". So:

      enum PixelStorage {
          Known(PixelFormat),                 // the list above
          PackedRgbUnorm(PackedRgbLayout),    // bytes_per_pixel + per-channel shift/bits + reserved
      }

  `PackedRgbUnorm` exists for BOOT AND SCANOUT ADAPTERS - the GOP hand-off and the terminal - and is
  not a texture format, not a presentable application surface format and not something a renderer
  samples.

- [x] Say which alpha combinations are VALID and which are merely unimplemented, and fix the
  byte-level rules two implementations otherwise answer differently. `AlphaMode` is `Opaque`,
  `Straight`, `Premultiplied`; an `X8` format is `Opaque` only, so `Straight` and `Premultiplied` on
  one are a type error and not an unsupported case; an `A8` format carries alpha and nothing else.
  Under `Opaque` an alpha channel reads and writes 1.0 in its storage representation (the maximum
  channel integer for UNORM, 1.0 for float), rather than being carried through unexamined.
  Under `Premultiplied`, normalized bounded color storage may treat a channel above alpha as clamped
  to alpha. Floating linear HDR/wide-gamut intermediates retain valid negative or above-alpha values
  until explicit output conversion; the alpha rule must not erase the color model's allowed range.
  Freeze zero-alpha canonicalization and non-finite handling in the storage/semantics matrix.
  Channel order is defined by the format
  NAME and never by host endianness; float values convert to integer by round-half-away-from-zero
  after clamping; NaN converts to zero and infinities clamp rather than wrapping or panicking; and
  the `X` byte of an `X8` format and reserved bits of a packed layout are ignored on read and
  written with all their bits set (`0xff` for X8), so a producer cannot leak stale bytes into them
  and a consumer cannot start depending on uninitialized content.

- [x] Colour management is a MODEL, not two transfer functions. `Linear`/`Srgb` over implied sRGB
  primaries is right for a first scanout and wrong for a phone, and retrofitting a colour space into
  a compositor that assumed one is a rewrite of every blend. `ColorSpace` carries primaries, white
  point and transfer function, and computes conversion matrices; the mandatory set is sRGB, Display
  P3, Rec. 2020 and their linear variants, plus the PQ and HLG transfer functions. ICC profile
  parsing is a separate later module; what this item forbids is a public model that makes one
  impossible.

  Naming the spaces is the easy half. "Supports tone mapping and dithering" is not a contract - two
  implementations can satisfy that sentence and produce visibly different images - so Profile 1 picks
  the algorithms: BRADFORD chromatic adaptation between white points, with the matrices written down;
  a stated reference white luminance and whether HDR values are absolute cd/m² or a named relative
  model; linear values permitted to go negative and above one INSIDE the pipeline, clamped only at
  output; ONE named tone-mapping operator with its parameters; the exact PQ and HLG transfer
  formulae and their inverse; and a stated gamut-mapping rule for out-of-gamut colours.

  Dithering picks ONE and it is ORDERED, with a fixed matrix and a fixed phase relative to the target
  origin. "Ordered or error-diffusion" was another unchosen alternative, and here the alternative is
  not merely vague - error diffusion carries state ACROSS pixels, which makes a tile-parallel
  renderer's output depend on tile decomposition. A backend built to render tiles independently and
  deterministically cannot use it without giving up both properties. Half-float behaviour is stated
  with the rest: NaN, infinity, subnormals and the rounding mode used when converting into
  `R16G16B16A16_FLOAT`.

- [x] Say what an image MEANS, not only how it is stored, because a colour-managed pipeline that
  cannot tell a colour from a measurement will transform the measurement. A normal map, a roughness
  map, a coverage mask and a depth buffer are all just bytes, and running any of them through an sRGB
  decode corrupts them silently - the artefact looks like a lighting bug, which is where the days go.
  Every image carries the `ImageSemantics` defined above, and every conversion path refuses to apply
  a transfer function to anything but `Color`. Mask interpretation and normal-map convention are
  explicit and validated against storage.

- [x] Provide `ImageView<'a>` and `ImageViewMut<'a>` - a borrowed slice plus a layout - whose
  constructors refuse a pitch below the minimum row bytes, a length below `minimum_visible_bytes()`, a
