  zero extent or an unknown format. `surface::Mapping` hands out one of these (or runs a closure
  over one) and `Mapping::addr()` is DELETED rather than left beside it: today
  `imgview.rs` turns that `u64` into a target with
  `core::slice::from_raw_parts_mut(surface.addr() as *mut u8, target_len)`, an application computing
  a length and building an aliasing mutable slice from a raw pointer.

- [x] Provide OWNED images and offscreen render targets, not only borrowed views. An application has
  to be able to create an image and draw into it without a Surface and without DisplayService, or
  every one of these needs a special case: component caches, thumbnails, chart and map tiles, an
  image editor, shadow maps, render-to-texture, a 3D frame a 2D pass composites, a 2D texture a 3D
  pass samples, screenshots and every host test of every drawing routine. `OwnedImage` carries a
  layout and its bytes, is charged to the creating Domain, and hands out `ImageView`/`ImageViewMut`.

  It is a 2D, SINGLE-SAMPLE, single-plane image with any valid `ImageSemantics`, so an A8 mask,
  integer picking image or normal-map cache has an owner as well as a color image. Color-only entry
  points validate `Color` semantics. It remains distinct from multisample storage, specialized
  depth/stencil attachments, cube/array/3D textures and mip chains, which belong to `render3d`'s
  resources. `ImageLayout` has no sample count. The interop path is explicit:

      3D MSAA / HDR attachments  --resolve + colour conversion-->  OwnedImage  -->  render2d

  Saying "a 3D attachment is an `OwnedImage`" would have made `soft3d`'s own requirement to validate
  a target's SAMPLE COUNT unsatisfiable against a type that cannot express one.

- [x] Zero newly allocated `OwnedImage` storage and present-image backing before exposure. A buffer
  recycled between Domains is cleared. Pixel writes and export canonicalize X/reserved channels
  according to the format rules; row padding is never interpreted as pixels or returned uninitialized
  by readback or screenshots. External buffers still undergo layout/span validation. First-frame
  `Full` damage does not substitute for initialization: applications need not overwrite padding.

- [x] Converge the pixel-plane types that already exist rather than adding a fifth. `pix::Target`
  carries `data/width/height/pitch/bytes_per_pixel` plus six channel shift/size fields; `pix::Image`
  carries `data/width/height/pitch`; `term::Raster` and `term::Geometry` are a third; and
  `surface::Mapping` rebuilds a `Framebuffer` with the shifts hard-coded to 16/8/0. Each becomes the
  new layout and view, or is deleted. `bootproto::Framebuffer` stays a boot-protocol type with a
  one-way adapter into the image model - a boot hand-off and a general image are different layers.

  The dependency graph, decided rather than offered as an alternative. `graphics-core` owns
  `Extent2D`, `Point2D`/`Vector2D`, `Rect`, `RoundedRect`, `Transform2D`, `Color`, `ColorSpace`,
  `PixelFormat`, `AlphaMode`, `ImageLayout`, `ImageView`/`ImageViewMut`, `OwnedImage`, `Sampler`,
  `DamageRegion`, checked pixel conversion, compositing and sampling. `pix` DEPENDS on it and stays
  an image utility library - owned `RgbaImage`, animation `Frame`/`Animation`/`Compositor`, crop,
  scale, blit - while its own `Image`, `Target` and `Rect` become aliases or are deleted. `render2d`
  and `render3d` depend on `graphics-core`; `soft2d` depends on `render2d`, `soft3d` on `render3d`;
  `scene3d` depends on `render3d` and never on `soft3d`. Nothing depends on `pix` in the other
  direction, and nothing but a backend depends on `soft2d` or `soft3d`.

  That graph settles the RUST side and leaves the WIRE side to grow a duplicate. These values cross
  the application display interface, the display-device interface and their generated crates, and
  `liber:display@1` already declares its own `enum pixel-format { b8g8r8x8 }`. So there is a
  value-only IDL package, `liber:graphics@1`, owning the wire-safe types - `extent-2d`, `offset-2d`,
  `rect`, `pixel-format`, `color-space`, `alpha-mode`, `row-origin`, `damage-region`, `image-layout`
  - which every graphics interface imports instead of redeclaring. `graphics-proto` is the generated
  wire identity. `graphics-core` does NOT re-export those types and add methods to them - Rust does
  not permit a crate to write an inherent `impl` for a type it does not own, and re-exporting does
  not transfer ownership, so as written this item does not compile (corrected 2026-08-30). Treating
  the unvalidated wire DTO as the public core type would also delete the boundary that proves a
  hostile descriptor was checked, and would tie the in-process layout to the wire schema this file
  wants versioned independently.
  THE WIRE PACKAGE AND THE CONVERSION LANDED 2026-09-13; THE RUST-SIDE CONVERGENCE HAS NOT.
  `liber:graphics@1` exists as `src/idl/graphics.lsidl` and `graphics-proto`: a package with NO
  interface in it, carrying `extent-2d`, `offset-2d`, `rect`, `pixel-format`, `alpha-mode`,
  `color-space`, `row-origin`, `image-layout` and `damage-region`. Its closed sets are the PROFILE's,
  in the profile's own order - twelve formats, three alpha modes, eight colour spaces - because a
  wire enumeration admitting a thirteenth format would be a format no profile describes.
  `liber:display@1`'S OWN `pixel-format` IS DELETED, in the same change that introduced the package
  rather than left beside it: it was an enumeration of ONE member, and a second interface needing one
  would have declared a second enumeration with the same name and a different meaning.
  `graphics-core::wire` IS THE ONE DOOR. `TryFrom<&wire::ImageLayout> for ImageLayout` runs the
  existing checked constructor rather than re-validating - a second copy of the rule is the copy that
  admits what the other refuses - and `to_wire` is the direction that CANNOT fail for colour and
  answers `None` for depth, data, normal maps and masks, which the wire record has no way to carry.
  Five tests hold the boundary: a pitch that does not cover a row, a zero extent, an alpha mode the
  format has no channel for, a round trip that preserves every field, and the honest `None`.
  TWO GENERATOR CHANGES CAME WITH IT, both forced by the first cross-package record this tree has:
  the rendering helpers became `pub` rather than `pub(crate)` (a record holding another package's
  type calls that type's renderer, across what is a crate boundary), and an external import is
  emitted as `pub use` (a consumer reading such a record has to be able to NAME the type through the
  package that handed it over, and the alternative is every consumer depending on every package its
  records transitively mention).
  THE RUST-SIDE CONVERGENCE LANDED 2026-09-13, AND THE FOUR PIXEL PLANES ARE ONE. `pix::Image` and
  `pix::Target` hold a `graphics_core::ImageView`/`ImageViewMut` and nothing else. `term::Geometry` is
  DELETED; `term::Raster` is a raw device address plus an `ImageLayout`. `surface::Mapping` carries an
  `ImageLayout` where it used to rebuild an ABI `Framebuffer` with its channel shifts written out as
  16, 8 and 0. The call sites converted with them: `bmp`, `png`, `display_service`, `imgview`,
  ConsoleService, the kernel's boot console, the kernel's own test and `pix`'s tests.

  THREE DECISIONS MOVED INTO THE SHARED MODEL, each because more than one place had been making it:
  `PackedRgbLayout::from_masks` (the six numbers a mode line carries, refused as one thing rather than
  six), `ImageLayout::scanout` (the three constants a scanout surface does not CHOOSE - first row at
  the top, opaque, sRGB) and `PixelFormat::packed_masks` (a NAMED format as channel masks, which is
  what a writer that packs needs when a display server reports `B8G8R8X8` by name). The last replaced
  a fallback that answered `B8G8R8A8`'s masks for EVERY named format: correct for the two BGR
  formats, silently red/blue-swapped for the two RGB ones, and meaningless for the wide and float
  ones, which a packed writer now declines to write at all rather than filling with noise.

  AND THE PRIVATE PACKERS WENT WITH THEM. `term` packed pixels with its own shift arithmetic and
  validated a mode line with its own five checks; both are the shared model's now - `write_packed`
  and `ImageLayout::new` - and what `term` still owns is the one thing the model deliberately does not
  express: a device mapping addressed by a raw address rather than by a slice, written with volatile
  stores. `term` and the kernel's console therefore depend on `graphics-core`, which the kernel's
  graph already carried through `pix`.

  EVIDENCE: `graphics-core` 26 host tests, `term` 68, `pix` 10, all green; three of them are new and
  hold the decisions above, including the mirror pair a hard-coded mask table got wrong.
  `./build.sh --arch x86_64` is clean, `check-graphics-profile` passes, and the guest suite passes 25
  tests over `boot,image,imgview,display,console` - which includes the harness that runs the REAL
  DisplayService and ConsoleService against a real terminal.

  TWO DEFECTS THIS ITEM'S OWN GUEST RUN FOUND, both of them the kind a host suite cannot see.
  A NAMED SCANOUT FORMAT WAS NOT A RASTER. `term::Raster::new` accepted only the MASK arm of
  `PixelStorage`, and a display server hands over a NAMED format - so the boot console still drew
  (firmware describes masks) while every userspace VT was blank: the surface mapped, the service came
  online, and no terminal was ever built. It is one line - ask the storage for its masks rather than
  matching on the arm - and it is exactly what `PixelFormat::packed_masks` was added for. A term test
  now builds a raster from both arms and requires them to pack a pixel identically.
  AND A WIRE VALUE MOVED UNDER TWO HAND-WRITTEN HARNESSES. Replacing `liber:display@1`'s one-member
  `pixel-format` with the shared twelve-member one renumbered `b8g8r8x8` from 0 to 3. DisplayService
  and its clients name the value and were unaffected; two harnesses in `src/kernel/tests.rs` stand in
  for DisplayService and wrote the ordinal by hand as a literal `0`, which now means `a8-unorm` - so
  the client refused the surface it was offered and the test waited three minutes for a frame nobody
  was going to send. Both now name the value through `graphics-proto`, which the kernel already had in
  its graph, so the next renumbering is a compile error rather than a timeout.
  So there are TWO representations and one conversion. `graphics-proto` owns the generated wire DTOs.
  `graphics-core` owns VALIDATED NEWTYPES with checked constructors, `minimum_visible_bytes()`, views over
  real slices, and the conversion, sampling and compositing routines; every trust boundary crosses
  from the DTO to the newtype through a mandatory `TryFrom` whose error is the typed refusal. Every
  renderer accepts only the validated core form and never the DTO. The CANONICAL representation for
  hashing and for the semantic registries is the wire DTO, because that is the one whose byte layout
  a version is declared against. `liber:display@1`'s own `pixel-format` is deleted in the same change that
  introduces the package, not left beside it.

- [x] Add the MULTI-PLANE image model, which is the largest functional hole left in the 2D half: every
  mandatory format above is RGB or RGBA, so a video player, a camera preview and a hardware decoder
  must convert every frame to RGBA before `render2d` will draw it - a full-frame conversion per frame
  on the CPU, for the one workload where that cost is least affordable. It is a SEPARATE type rather
  than a widening of `ImageLayout`, so a single-plane image never grows fields it cannot use:
  `MultiPlaneImage` with its planes, a planar format, a YUV colour model, range and chroma siting.
  Profile 1 carries `NV12`, `I420` and `P010`, BT.601/BT.709/BT.2020 conversion, full and limited
  range and explicit chroma siting, and `render2d` draws one through the SAME shared sampling and
  conversion pipeline as everything else rather than through a private video path.

- [x] Migrate `pix` rather than leaving it beside the model. `pix::RgbaImage` as it stands is
  `width`/`height`/`pitch`/`pixels` with no format, alpha mode, colour space or meaning, so keeping it
  unchanged leaves a back door through which an image with no colour metadata enters the new system -
  and the item above converges the pixel-plane types precisely to close doors like that. Either
  `RgbaImage` becomes `OwnedImage`, or it wraps one and is always RGBA8 with an explicit alpha mode
  and meaning. The animation `Frame` and `Compositor` go the same way.

### P02M0103a-wsi - surfaces, presentation and the display-device interface

Depends on `s-wsi` (the frozen WSI profile - added 2026-09-01, see the freeze section: this part
implements a normative document and may not start against a draft of it), on `a-common`, on P02M0141
(service restart), P02M0165 (stop and drain), P02M0166 (removal and graph state), and on the
completion object above. Runs beside `b` and `c`; required before any
part that presents.

P02M0164's catalogue mechanism is available. DisplayService already subscribes and reacquires a
replacement after driver-channel loss; this part completes the identity-aware withdrawal, alternative
provider selection, subscription recovery and typed device protocol described in the prerequisite
assessment. P02M0099 assigns the remaining migration jointly to this part and the first second-display-
provider item, whichever reaches it first. Consume what has landed and implement only what remains;
the WSI reacquisition/failover gate proves the complete contract either way.

- [x] Carry OUTPUT COLOUR metadata on the surface, because a colour-space NAME is not enough to tone
  map with - a conversion that does not know the destination's luminance is guessing at the one
  number that decides how the image looks. The surface reports its colour space plus SDR white
  luminance, minimum and maximum luminance and maximum frame-average luminance, and the HDR-to-SDR
  and HDR-to-HDR paths in the colour item above consume those rather than assuming a display.
  IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-13, in both halves: the surface reports and the colour path consumes.
  THE REPORT is `output-colour` on `surface-info` - the space, SDR white, minimum, maximum and
  maximum frame-average luminance - so it travels WITH the surface rather than being a second call a
  client would have to make before it can draw its first frame.
  EVERY LUMINANCE IS OPTIONAL AND `none` IS NOT ZERO, which is the part worth arguing about. Nothing
  in this system asks a panel what it can do: DDC needs a bus no driver here can reach, which is the
  EDID item's blocked half in `P02M0099`. So DisplayService reports the space - which follows from the
  format every scanout here uses - and `none` for the four luminances, rather than inventing numbers.
  `Image and Colour Profile 1` says absent HDR metadata is a refusal to assume rather than a default
  to invent, and a consumer handed `none` uses the profile's own STATED reference white point: one
  assumption, written down in one place, instead of one made per conversion.
  THE CONSUMPTION is `graphics_core::pixel::OutputLuminance` and `Encoder::new_for_output`. The tone
  map's white point was the profile's constant for every destination; it is now `max / sdr-white`
  when the display reported both and the numbers can be believed. A description that cannot be true -
  a zero white, a peak below diffuse white, a non-finite number, or half a description - falls back to
  the constant rather than computing a curve from nonsense.
  AND IT REACHES THE RENDERER. `render2d`'s `TargetDescription` carries the luminance and `soft2d`
  hands it to the encoder that writes each tile back into the target. It is deliberately NOT part of
  the prepared key: the tone curve is applied when a tile is ENCODED, and preparation flattens
  geometry and builds shaders - a display that changes what it can show changes the pixels, not the
  preparation.
  EVIDENCE: a `graphics-core` test that the same bright pixel encodes differently for a
  thousand-nit display and for one that said nothing, that the unknown case is exactly the profile's
  constant, and that six impossible descriptions fall back rather than compute; `graphics-core` 27,
  `render2d` 21 and `soft2d` 21 host tests green; the guest passes 25 over
  `boot,image,display,console,imgview`.

- [x] Break the ONE-SURFACE-PER-CONNECTION assumption. `liber:display@1` says it in its own words -
  "One display connection owns at most one surface" - with `acquire`/`present`/`release`/
  `input-focus` all at connection level and a synchronous `present`. Every desktop and mobile
  application is more than one surface: a main window, a dialog, a popup, a menu, a tooltip, a video
  surface, offscreen content. Replacing the synchronous present with a queue and leaving the object
  model at one surface would rebuild the same wall one layer up. The model is:

      DisplayConnection
          create_surface(...) -> Surface capability
      Surface
          its own present queue, its own event stream,
          its own extent, scale, transform and colour metadata

  A compositor is still a later milestone; what this item forbids is an object model a compositor
  would have to break. Foreground input focus is a property of a surface rather than of a connection,
  so P02M0091's focus capability binds to the surface that actually has it.

  Before a compositor exists, at most ONE surface is visible and scanout-bound. Other surfaces keep
  independent resources, generations and queues while hidden/occluded; acquiring from them returns
  `NotVisible`. Frames accepted before a visibility change still settle in FIFO order with the
  appropriate discarded outcome. Focus belongs to the visible surface. There is no popup position,
  z-order or simultaneous composition contract in v1; those arrive with the compositor. Tests switch
  the visible surface and prove independent lifetimes instead of claiming to compose a popup.

- [x] Separate LOGICAL size, PHYSICAL extent and SCALE FACTOR, and put all three in the surface
  contract. `liber:display@1` today has one extent and calls it "preferred logical size", which is
  the physical one wearing the other's name - there is no scale anywhere in the interface. A phone
  surface is 400 x 800 logical at scale 3.0 and 1200 x 2400 physical, and an application that cannot
  see those as three numbers either renders at the wrong size or renders blurred. Applications lay
  out in logical units, `render2d` transforms by the scale, and the backend rasterises physical
  pixels. Configuration arrives as one `SurfaceConfiguration` snapshot carrying serial, generation,
  logical and physical extent, scale, transform, output, presentable pixel format, color information,
  subpixel layout, visibility and focus. A client never applies a new scale with an old extent.
  The lifecycle is
  `configure(snapshot) -> rebuild -> ack_configure(serial) -> first present Full`; stale or unknown
  serials are refused. Each present identifies its configuration serial and image generation, and
  both must match the acknowledged current configuration at acceptance. Changes to extent,
  scale, orientation or presentable format invalidate the old image generation. Other configuration
  changes carry a new serial and are applied atomically without an unnecessary image allocation.
  Close requests and presentation completions remain separate events.

  The scale factor is a RATIO on the wire, `{ numerator: u32, denominator: u32 }`, not a bare `f32`:
  a fractional scale that two sides round differently produces an off-by-one extent, and that is the
  bug that shows as a one-pixel seam nobody can reproduce. With it, state which of logical and
  physical is authoritative and freeze one logical-to-physical edge rule used by both neighbors of
  a shared edge. Damage bounds round outward (left/top down, right/bottom up); that conservative
  bound is not a promise of non-overlap between independently rounded geometry. Freeze inverse input
  mapping and orientation together with this rule. A scale change bumps the image generation just
  like resize, through the atomic configuration lifecycle above.
  DONE 2026-09-14. `surface-configuration` carries all twelve fields the profile freezes, as ONE
  snapshot: a client never applies a new scale with an old extent because they arrive together.
  THE SCALE IS `{ numerator, denominator }` and never an `f32` on the wire, so both neighbours of a
  shared edge compute the same integer from the same two integers. The PHYSICAL extent is
  authoritative and the logical one is derived; at a scale of one they are the same number and they
  are still two fields, so the day a fractional scale arrives nothing above changes shape.
  The lifecycle is `configure -> rebuild -> ack_configure(serial) -> first present Full`, and a
  stale or unknown serial is REFUSED. A present names its configuration serial AND its image
  generation and both must match at acceptance. A change to the extent bumps the generation, marks
  every image stale, settles every frame in flight as `replaced-by-resize` - a frame in flight is not
  un-submitted - and rebuilds; a change that does not touch the extent carries a new serial alone.

- [x] Replace the current display contract with the present queue, with NO compatibility path for
  the single-surface API. Nothing is versioned before the first release and no external software
  depends on it. A presentable surface has a pixel format immutable within its generation, an explicit extent, a resize
  generation and a NEGOTIATED image count - the service advertises minimum and maximum, the client
  requests, the service answers with the actual, and no number is written into the interface.
  DONE 2026-09-14, WITH NO COMPATIBILITY PATH. `acquire`/`present`/`release` at connection level are
  gone rather than deprecated; `./gen.sh --accept-breaking` is what the pre-release break is spelled
  with, and every client in the tree was moved in the same change.
  The image count is negotiated: `image-limits` advertises two to three, the client asks, the service
  clamps and `queue` reports what it gave - so no number is in the ABI. The images themselves are
  fetched ONE AT A TIME by `image(index)`, because a record carrying a LIST of capabilities has a
  count no schema can bound and an encode that stopped part way could not hand back the handles it
  had already taken. That refusal came from the IDL generator rather than from review.
  `present_mode` is an ENUM with `Fifo` defined now, so `Mailbox` and `Immediate` have a place to be
  added rather than a meaning to be argued about. Advertise `min_images = 2` and `max_images = 3` in
  v1 - negotiated stays negotiated, but a service free to answer "one" turns the
  double-or-triple-buffering gate into a test of nothing. Specify the image state machine as states:
  `Available -> acquire -> Acquired -> present -> PendingPresent -> released -> Available`, with
  `Stale` after a generation change and a rule that a stale image is never presented into a new
  generation. Define FIFO ordering, maximum frames in flight, backpressure, `OUT_OF_DATE` handling,
  what process death returns, and console restoration.

  Three things that state machine is missing. An `Acquired -> abandon -> Available` edge, because a
  client that acquires an image and then decides not to draw must have a way back that is not a
  present. The resize transitions in full: `Available` in an old generation becomes `Stale`;
  `Acquired` in an old generation becomes `Stale` and the client discards it rather than presenting
  it; `PendingPresent` in an old generation completes or is discarded and then becomes `Stale`. And
  the Rust shape that makes the machine unrepresentable-when-wrong rather than merely documented:
  `acquire_next` returns an owned `AcquiredImage`, drawing borrows it, and `present` CONSUMES it, so
  after presenting there is no safe path to the pixels until the queue hands them back.

  `Fifo` as written promises something the occlusion rule takes away - "every present is displayed"
  cannot hold for a background client that must also not be blocked forever. Split the promise: FIFO
  is the ordering of ACCEPTED presents, by present-call order and not acquire order; while visible,
  every accepted frame reaches logical presentation; while occluded or in the background, new
  acquires answer `NotVisible`, and frames already accepted still complete IN ORDER but may
  complete as discarded. That needs a per-present outcome rather than a bare completion -
  `Displayed`, `DiscardedOccluded`, `ReplacedByResize`, `DriverLost`.

  **`acquire_next` DOES NOT BLOCK IN v1, AND THIS SAID IT COULD** (corrected 2026-08-30). LSIDL
  handlers are synchronous and DisplayService dispatches the GPU, the admin channel, the kill control
  and EVERY client from ONE `wait_any` loop. Blocking inside an acquire handler stops the only loop
  that could deliver the release, the resize, the peer death or the driver message that would unblock
  it - so a conforming implementation deadlocks the whole service under ordinary queue pressure. The
  v1 model is therefore NONBLOCKING: `acquire_next` answers `Again` when no image is available and
  `NotVisible` when the surface is occluded or backgrounded, and the client waits on an
  IMAGE-AVAILABLE event rather than inside the call. Deferred replies with a bounded pending-call
  table and an independent progress pump are the alternative, and they are a named extension with
  their own async-dispatch design - not something an implementer may assume from the word "block".

  **AND "DISPLAYED" IS THREE DIFFERENT FACTS.** The current virtio-gpu path acknowledges
  `TRANSFER_TO_HOST_2D` and `RESOURCE_FLUSH` and nothing else: there is no vblank, no page-flip
  timing and no evidence that a scanout happened, so a flush reply cannot supply a presentation
  timestamp and reporting one would be reporting a command acknowledgement as physical presentation.
  The three outcomes are named separately - ACCEPTED (the service took the frame), DRIVER-COMPLETED
  (the backend acknowledged the command), and PHYSICALLY DISPLAYED (scanout is observed) - and a
  backend that cannot observe the third says so. On the current backend the presentation timestamp is
  reported as UNAVAILABLE or explicitly ESTIMATED, and `Displayed` means driver-completed. A real
  timestamp requires a vblank/timing capability added to the typed display-device contract with its
  own tests, and that is a later item, not an assumption of this one.

- [x] Give the frame loop a TIMING contract rather than leaving every application to sleep on the
  monotonic clock: a preferred frame deadline, the output's refresh interval, the presentation
  timestamp of a completed present, and the `PresentOutcome` above. EVERY ONE OF THOSE IS OPTIONAL
  AND TYPED AS SUCH, because the current backend can supply none of them: the contract carries
  `Unavailable`, `Estimated(t)` and `Measured(t)` rather than a bare timestamp, and a client that
  requires `Measured` refuses on a backend that cannot produce it instead of pacing against a
  fabricated number. A compositor will later supply
  frame callbacks synchronised to scanout, and the contract has to have a place for them now -
  retrofitting a callback into a design whose only pacing primitive is `sleep` means every
  application that animates is rewritten.
  DONE 2026-09-14. `present-complete` carries the present's serial, its `present-outcome`, its
  `timestamp-evidence` and a `frame-timing` record, and EVERY TIMING FIELD IS OPTIONAL because the
  current backend can supply none of them.
  THE EVIDENCE IS TYPED - `unavailable`, `estimated(t)`, `measured(t)` - rather than a bare number,
  so a client that requires a measurement refuses on a backend that cannot produce one instead of
  pacing against a fabricated one. The virtio-gpu path acknowledges a transfer and a flush and
  nothing else: there is no vblank, no page-flip timing and no evidence a scanout happened, so this
  service reports `displayed` with `unavailable` evidence and says in as many words that the outcome
  and the evidence are two fields for exactly this reason.

- [x] Damage is a BOUNDED LIST of rectangles with an explicit whole-surface fallback, not one
  rectangle. Both sides of the driver protocol carry a single `(u32, u32, u32, u32)` today and the
  driver unions everything it receives (`union_rect` in `virtio_gpu.rs`), so a client updating two
  corners of a screen sends their bounding box - which is most of the screen.
  IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-13, on both sides, and the nine answers `WSI Profile 1` freezes are the
  contract rather than a description of one.
  THE INTERFACE CARRIES `damage-region`, which is `liber:graphics@1`'s own record - a `whole` variant
  and a list bounded at sixteen, the profile's `MAX_DAMAGE_RECTS`. `present` takes it instead of four
  numbers, which is a pre-release ABI break of `liber:display@1` taken with `--accept-breaking`.
  EACH ANSWER IS IMPLEMENTED WHERE IT CAN BE PROVED. An EMPTY list is "nothing changed": the present
  is ordered, it completes, it is counted, and nothing is transferred - a service that answered an
  error would make the profile's own answer a failure every client has to work around. The WHOLE
  surface is the variant, never a rectangle covering the extent. A rectangle outside the extent is a
  typed refusal and never a clamp, and a frame whose second rectangle is out of bounds presents NONE
  of it, because half a frame is a frame nobody asked for. More than the bound never reaches the
  service at all: the wire list is bounded and the decoder refuses a seventeenth rectangle, which is
  what makes "the caller's problem, solved by merging or by sending `whole`" true rather than hopeful.
  AND THE DRIVER STOPPED UNIONING EVERYTHING, which was the half that made the cost real.
  `drivers::gpu::DamageSet` collects what a service sends between flushes and merges two rectangles
  only when their bounding box is no larger than the two of them apart - true when they overlap or
  touch, false for two corners of a screen. A set at its cap merges the pair that wastes least rather
  than dropping a rectangle, because a dropped rectangle leaves the screen showing something that is
  no longer there.
  EVIDENCE: 3 host tests in `drivers::gpu` (two corners stay two transfers and their union is the
  whole screen; overlapping and touching rectangles become one; thirty-two rectangles hold the cap
  with every damaged pixel still covered), and in the guest, a two-corner present on a scaled surface
  that moves two source pixels rather than the four of its bounding box, an empty present that
  completes and transfers nothing, and an out-of-bounds rectangle that refuses the whole frame.

  "A small cap" is not an ABI. Fix `MAX_DAMAGE_RECTS = 16` and state the semantics: an EMPTY list
  means nothing changed and the present is still ordered and still completes; an explicit `Full`
  variant means the whole extent, and is not spelled as one rectangle covering it; a rectangle
  outside the extent is a TYPED INVALID rather than a silent clamp; exceeding the cap is the caller's
  problem to solve by merging or sending `Full`, not the service's to solve by dropping rectangles;
  overlapping rectangles are legal and may be normalised; and coordinates are always in the image
  space of the generation the present belongs to. The backend MAY merge rectangles when merging is
  cheaper than transferring them separately, but the unconditional union of everything is exactly
  what this item removes.

  And answer the question all of that skips: damage relative to WHAT, when consecutive frames are
  drawn into different images? With images A, B, A, a client that draws two rectangles into B has
  said nothing about the rest of B. The v1 rule is the simple one: **every presented image contains a
  COMPLETE valid frame.** Damage is a HINT describing which pixels changed against the previously
  presented frame; it is not permission to leave the rest of the image undefined. From that: the
  first present of a generation is `Full`; an empty damage list asserts the submitted frame is
  pixel-identical to the previous one, which lets DisplayService release the image without copying;
  and a client wanting to redraw only part of an image needs to know what that image already holds,
  which is a `buffer_age` extension a later milestone can add without changing this rule.

  Give the bounded merging one implementation - `DamageRegion::{None, Rects { rects: [Rect; 16],
  len: u8 }, Full}` in `graphics-core` with a `push_or_full()` helper that degrades to `Full` on the
  seventeenth rectangle. Otherwise every application writes its own bounded merge and the cap becomes
  sixteen different behaviours at the cap.

- [x] Implement presentation completion with ordinary CHANNEL PAIRS, two pairs per SURFACE,
  created once when the surface is admitted. No new kernel object, generic timeline, ABI number or
  signal right is introduced. `Event` is not the completion mechanism: it lacks the authority split
  and peer-close lifecycle required here.

      PRODUCER_READY   client: SEND only; service: RECEIVE | WAIT
      PRESENT_DONE     service: SEND only; client: RECEIVE | WAIT

  These are four endpoint objects, four handle slots and four inboxes per surface; attenuation makes
  exactly two inboxes capable of receiving messages. Each endpoint is created with depth
  `max_images`, so at three images at most six messages can be queued across both directions.
  Client endpoints carry neither DUPLICATE nor TRANSFER. Moving the safe completion token inside
  its process is allowed; the raw endpoint is never exposed alongside it.

  Completion authority and lifecycle are enforced at two boundaries:

  - An ownership-consuming per-present token implements signal-once in safe producer code. The
    token carries `PresentKey { surface_generation, image_index, present_serial }`; the serial is
    unique for that surface lifetime, is not reused when an image is reacquired, and exhaustion
    refuses another present rather than wrapping. The surface owns the reusable endpoint, so
    signaling consumes the token without closing the channel.
  - DisplayService reserves and acknowledges that key when accepting `present`, before the client
    publishes `PRODUCER_READY`. The receiver processes at most one outcome for the exact outstanding
    key. A stale generation, stale serial, unknown image or duplicate is a protocol violation that
    fails the surface and releases its pending records. Naming only the image index is insufficient:
    a delayed duplicate must not complete a later use of that same image.
  - The client sends producer-ready only after rendering has completed. DisplayService may use the
    image only after receiving that message; the client may reuse it only after the matching
    `PRESENT_DONE` carries its typed outcome and optional timing. Accepted presents settle in FIFO
    call order even if readiness arrives in another order.
  - Dropping a token without signaling reports a failed outcome for that key. Ordinary completion
    sends are bounded and nonblocking in the service loop. If a protocol failure prevents delivery,
    closing the endpoint fails the whole surface; neither a destructor nor an LSIDL handler may
    block the only progress loop.
  - Peer-close before destruction ends the surface, fails each outstanding present exactly once,
    releases its image references and wakes waiters. A successful frame never closes the endpoint.
    Closing the surface after the last outcome retires the pairs. Timeout/cancellation follows the
    same bounded cleanup; dropping the waiter endpoint cancels the whole surface, while dropping
    one token cancels that present. `WSI_PROFILE_1.md` fixes the deadline policy before implementation.

  The waiter needs RECEIVE | WAIT: the existing wait syscall refuses receive-only handles. The
  safe wrapper cannot constrain a hostile raw sender, so receiver validation is mandatory. A sender
  that fills an undrained depth-`max_images` queue gets `Full` on the next send and transfers no
  capabilities with that refused message. A duplicate admitted before the queue is full is handled
  as the protocol violation above, not mistaken for kernel-enforced signal-once.
  DONE 2026-09-14. Two ordinary channel pairs per surface, minted when the queue is built and handed
  over by `queue` with the client's ends ATTENUATED: PRODUCER_READY carries `SEND` alone and
  PRESENT_DONE carries `RECEIVE | WAIT`, and neither carries `DUPLICATE` or `TRANSFER` - a completion
  endpoint that could be handed to another process is proof about somebody else's frame.
  NO NEW KERNEL OBJECT, no generic timeline, no ABI number and no signal right. `Event` was not it:
  it lacks the authority split and the peer-close lifecycle this needs, which is why the pairs are
  ordinary channels.
  A PRESENT'S KEY IS ITS OWN SERIAL and not its image index: the serial is unique for the surface's
  lifetime and is not reused when an image is reacquired, so a delayed duplicate cannot complete a
  later use of the same image. Accepted presents settle in FIFO CALL ORDER, and every frame still in
  flight when a surface goes away settles exactly once rather than leaving a client waiting on a
  completion nobody owes it.
  The completion travels BOTH ways: the event stream carries the outcome, the evidence and the
  timing, and the PRESENT_DONE endpoint carries the release a frame loop waits on without a dispatch.

  The wake budget belongs to the SERVICE, not only to individual clients. Its set includes every
  actual fixed member (serve/admin roots, focus/kill controls, scanout, provider subscription and any
  added waitable control), one per live connection, one waitable process identity per live client
  task, and TWO per live surface: the surface channel and its `PRODUCER_READY` receiver. Enforce
  `fixed + connections + client_tasks + 2 * surfaces <= abi::MAX_WAIT_SET_MEMBERS` at admission;
  publish the available bound and return a typed refusal before accepting work that exceeds it.
  A WaitSet cannot contain another WaitSet. Clients similarly budget their fixed handles plus two
  members per surface (surface channel and `PRESENT_DONE`) against their own wait mechanism's limit.
  No wait list may be truncated and no runnable poller substitutes for a missing wake member.

  Freeze the memory contract in `WSI_PROFILE_1.md`: a successful channel send has release semantics,
  and the receive observing it has acquire semantics, in BOTH directions. Receiving `PRESENT_DONE`
  precedes the image becoming Available and the next acquire. v1 present images use coherent ordinary
  CPU-visible MemoryObjects only; non-coherent/DMA imports require the separate portable-sync
  contract and are not silently accepted. Verify shared-memory visibility and the syscall/compiler
  ordering on all three architectures; do not assume a correct state machine alone proves cache
  coherence. No new flush/invalidate API is part of this CPU-only v1.

  Tests cover signal/drop order, cancellation, token move then signal, compile-time duplicate-signal
  refusal, receive-without-wait denial, each peer-close direction, timeout and cleanup. Hostile
  clients fill a paused receiver's queue, send duplicate and stale keys (including reuse of the same
  image), and attach capabilities to invalid frames; assert exactly one outcome or terminal surface
  failure and return to the resource baseline. Admission tests open multiple clients and surfaces
  past the service ceiling, count the actual wait members and prove every admitted surface still
  receives progress. These tests distinguish source-level token ownership from receiver enforcement.

- [x] Replace the DisplayService <-> display-driver byte protocol with a typed LSIDL interface, and
  specify its lifecycle rather than only its calls. `display_service.rs` writes `b"PRESENT"` and four
  little-endian `u32`s into a 23-byte buffer; `virtio_gpu.rs` decodes it with `rd32_le(m, 7)` and a
  length check, with `FB`, `FBNEW`, `RESIZE`, `FLUSH`, `OK` and `ERR` beside it. Every other service
  boundary in this tree is LSIDL with typed capabilities. `liber:display-device@1` states: who owns
  the backing and for how long the capability is valid; how a backing binds to a generation; whether
  a resize changes the visible extent or delivers a new backing, and what happens to the old one;
  what present completion means; what driver death does to in-flight presents; how DisplayService
  finds a new backing after a driver restart; that the event stream is bounded; and that a backing
  from an old generation can never be presented into a new one.
  IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-13 as `liber:display-device@1`. `FB`, `FBNEW`, `RESIZE`, `FLUSH`, `PRESENT`,
  `OK` and `ERR` are gone, and with them the `Framebuffer` record that was memcpy'd out of a message
  at an offset and the four little-endian `u32`s decoded by hand on the other side.
  THE INTERFACE IS THREE CALLS: `scanout` hands over the backing, its layout, its visible extent and
  its generation; `present` takes the generation and a bounded damage list and answers when the
  device has acknowledged the transfer; `events` is the bounded stream a resize and a replacement
  arrive on.
  THE EIGHT LIFECYCLE QUESTIONS ARE ANSWERED IN THE PACKAGE'S OWN DOCUMENTATION, where two
  implementations of this wire can read them: the DRIVER owns the backing and the service's handle is
  valid until it is replaced or the connection is lost; the generation moves when the BACKING moves
  and not when the visible size does; a resize inside the existing backing keeps its pixels and a
  resize beyond it delivers a new one; present completion means the DEVICE ACKNOWLEDGED THE COMMAND
  and never that a scanout was observed; driver death fails the call in flight, of which there is
  exactly one; a restart is found through the provider catalogue rather than through a reconnect this
  interface cannot carry; the event stream is bounded and drops the OLDEST because both events are
  idempotent snapshots of the current state; and a present naming a generation that is no longer
  current is refused with `stale`.
  `stale` IS A NEW `liber:base@1` ERROR, added here because the distinction is what a caller ACTS on:
  an invalid request stays wrong however often it is sent, an `again` is the same request retried, and
  a stale one is well formed and was right until a moment ago - the caller re-reads and rebuilds. The
  one exhaustive match over that enumeration stopped the build until the new variant was classified,
  which is what it is for.
  AND THE INTERLEAVING IS GONE. A resize used to arrive on the same channel as a present's answer, so
  the service's flush loop had to recognise and handle a framebuffer replacement while waiting for an
  acknowledgement. Events have their own stream now: what comes back from a present is the present's
  answer and nothing else - and `serve` in the driver is a SAFE function, because the two
  `read_unaligned`/`copy_nonoverlapping` calls that decoded and encoded that record are gone.
  EVIDENCE: the guest passes 23 tests over `boot,display,console,imgview`, including the harness that
  runs the REAL DisplayService against a stand-in device, a resize event, a backing REPLACEMENT at a
  new generation, and a two-corner present that reaches the device as one call carrying two
  rectangles. Both kernel stand-ins speak the wire through the generated codec rather than by hand.
  THE BOX IS TICKED 2026-09-14: the implementation and its evidence were both recorded above and only
  the checkbox was left, which is the kind of gap a reader takes for unfinished work.

- [x] **Account through the KERNEL's Domain counters and OBSERVE through SystemGraph. ResourceManager
  is not extended** (decided 2026-08-30; the item is written around the decision rather than
  requiring and then rejecting the same integration). The kernel already charges MemoryObject and IPC
  bytes to the Domain that CREATED them, so the counter that enforces already exists - and moving
  creation out of DisplayService is what makes it charge the right side. It charges the SUPPLIER,
  which is ordinarily the client and is not guaranteed to be (see the sponsorship row below); this
  sentence used to say "with client-created images that Domain is the client's" and call the counter
  "already correct", which asserted in the opening what the same item withdrew two rows later
  (corrected 2026-08-31). This milestone adds no second byte budget, no graphics classification and
  no graphics-specific quota: a quota with no consumer is policy this file's own rule calls a guess.

  What this item owns is therefore narrower and fully specified:
  - SystemGraph reports bounded COUNTS and reset/fault state - surfaces, present images, queued
    presents, damage metadata entries and waiters - as observation, never as enforcement, and without
    exposing image contents;
  - every DisplayService-side structure that is not a MemoryObject (the present queue, damage lists,
    waiter records) has a stated per-connection BOUND, because those are not charged by the kernel
    and are what an adversarial client can multiply;
  - exhaustion of any of those bounds returns a typed error and releases the partial transaction; and
  - reclamation on client death is specified in the item below rather than asserted here.

  **THE ACCOUNTING CONTRACT IS SPONSORSHIP, AND THE INTRODUCTION SAYS SO** (corrected 2026-08-30).
  `SYS_MEMORY_OBJECT_CREATE` charges the SYSCALL CALLER's Domain, and DisplayService creates and
  retains every surface MemoryObject today - so those bytes are charged to DisplayService, not to
  the requesting application, and one client can exhaust the service's quota while its own budget
  still looks healthy. That is the defect this item fixes.

  WHICH DOMAIN PAYS IS NOT CLAIMED, AND THE SENTENCE ABOVE NO LONGER CLAIMS IT (corrected
  2026-08-31 - an earlier version said client-created images "necessarily" make the client's Domain
  the charged one and the enforcing counter therefore correct, and then withdrew exactly that two
  rows later, so one item asserted and retracted the same guarantee). The SUPPLIER of an image pays
  for it, and the supplier need not be the requester - `ObjectInfo` carries no charged Domain, so
  the service cannot tell the two apart. The property this item delivers is
  therefore stated exactly: DISPLAYSERVICE IS NOT CHARGED FOR ITS CLIENTS' IMAGES, and no Domain pays
  for storage it did not itself allocate. "Necessarily charged to the client's Domain, so the
  enforcing counter is correct" was the previous wording and it is withdrawn here as well as in the
  protocol row below, where it was already withdrawn - one item may not assert and withdraw the same
  claim.

  Pick one and write it down:
  (a) THE CLIENT CREATES AND IMPORTS - the application creates the MemoryObject in its own Domain and
  hands it in, with strict validation of size, rights and layout and a stated rule about which
  handles the service may retain; or
  (b) ALLOCATE-IN / CHARGE-TRANSFER - a capability-authorised operation that allocates against the
  CLIENT's Domain on the service's behalf, which requires a kernel mechanism that does not exist and
  is therefore itself a prerequisite.
  **DECIDED 2026-08-30: (a), CLIENT-CREATED AND IMPORTED, and here is the protocol** - the previous
  round chose it in one clause and left the API describing a service-managed pool, so implementing
  what was written would have preserved the accounting defect it was meant to fix.

    negotiation      unchanged: the service advertises `min_images`/`max_images`, the client
                       requests, the service answers with the ACTUAL count. What changes is who
                       allocates the images that count names.
    supply           after negotiation the SUPPLIER creates exactly that many MemoryObjects, each
                       of the extent, format and stride the service answered with, and imports them
                       in one `provide_images` call. The supplier is ordinarily the client itself;
                       "in ITS OWN Domain" was the previous wording and it stated as a requirement
                       something the service cannot check (corrected 2026-08-30).
    who pays         SPONSORSHIP IS THE CONTRACT, AND REQUESTER ATTRIBUTION IS NOT CLAIMED
                       (corrected 2026-08-30). `SYS_MEMORY_OBJECT_CREATE` charges the Domain that
                       CREATED the object and the charge stays with it when the capability is
                       transferred, so what this design guarantees is the property that matters:
                       DisplayService no longer pays for its clients' images, and NO DOMAIN PAYS FOR
                       MEMORY IT DID NOT ITSELF ALLOCATE. What it does NOT guarantee is that the
                       paying Domain is the requesting one: `ObjectInfo` carries koid, type, rights,
                       generation and size and NO charged Domain, so the service cannot tell a
                       client-created buffer from one another Domain created and handed over. A
                       third Domain sponsoring a client is therefore possible, and it is a
                       capability-system consequence rather than a hole: it requires that Domain to
                       allocate and transfer deliberately, which is what a capability is. The
                       previous version said attribution was enforceable "by construction" and
                       required the service to reject cross-Domain handles; the first was untrue and
                       the second was unimplementable, and both are withdrawn rather than left as a
                       gate nobody can build. Adding a kernel provenance field to `ObjectInfo` would
                       make requester attribution checkable and is a separate proposal against a
                       shared ABI this milestone does not own.
    validation       the service checks count, the full `backend_access_span()`, and rights, and
                       refuses the whole set rather than a subset - a partially supplied queue is a
                       queue that cannot be presented from.
    rights           the service needs READ and MAP and does not need WRITE; the client keeps its
                       own handles. The service may not TRANSFER an imported image onward, which is
                       what makes delegation a decision rather than an accident (below).
    acquire_next     unchanged in shape: it answers with an image the client already owns, by index
                       and generation, not with a handle it is being given.
    resize and
      generation     a generation change INVALIDATES the whole set. The service releases every
                       imported handle and answers `OUT_OF_DATE`; the client supplies a new set for
                       the new extent. No image ever crosses a generation, which is the rule the
                       state machine already states for `Stale`.
    reclamation      the service releases its imported handles when the surface is destroyed, when
                       the client's process dies, or on a generation change - whichever comes first.
                       The client's Domain gets the memory back when its own handles go, which is
                       what makes process death sufficient.
    delegation       DECIDED ONCE AND CLOSED (2026-08-30), AND ENFORCED STRUCTURALLY RATHER THAN
                       BY DETECTION (corrected 2026-08-30): the surface channel's client endpoint is
                       handed over WITHOUT `RIGHT_TRANSFER` and WITHOUT `RIGHT_DUPLICATE`, so moving
                       it to another process FAILS AT THE SYSCALL. The previous version said the
                       service "refuses a surface whose channel has moved process", which it cannot
                       do: a message identifies only the endpoint it arrived on, neither the message
                       nor `ObjectInfo` reports the holder, and watching the creator detects DEATH
                       and not a live transfer. An earlier version permitted the channel to move
                       while forbidding re-import, and then a later paragraph said the item must
                       still decide whether delegation is legal - three answers to one question.
                       One process identity owns
                       a surface's images, its resize authority and its cleanup, and a channel that
                       could move would leave "client death" with no unique process to name. A client
                       that wants another process to draw gives it the images through its own
                       protocol; the surface stays where it was created.

  RESOURCEMANAGER IS NOT EXTENDED, and the accounting item above is now written that way rather than
  requiring the integration this paragraph rejects - which is how one item came to instruct two
  incompatible implementations.
  And "killing a Domain reclaims surfaces, images, mappings and waiters" is not structural today:
  DisplayService's bound task is duplicated with `MANAGE|TRANSFER` rather than `WAIT`, and its wait
  set watches channels and not client processes, so a service-retained MemoryObject can outlive the
  process the plan says owns it. This item owns giving DisplayService a WAITABLE task identity and a
  bounded per-task wait set, and cleanup proved INDEPENDENTLY of channel peer lifetime and of driver
  progress. Delegation is not an open question here - the protocol above closes it, and closes it in
  the rights the endpoint is minted with rather than in a check the service cannot perform. The
  waitable identity does the OTHER half: the endpoint's rights keep the surface where it was created,
  and watching that process is how its death is noticed.

  **THE PART IS IMPLEMENTED** (2026-09-14), and the protocol above is what was built.

  CLIENT-CREATED AND IMPORTED. `surface.image` is gone and `surface.provide-image(index,
  @rights(read, map) image: handle<image-object>)` replaces it: the service negotiates the count and
  the pitch in `queue`, and the client creates exactly that many memory objects and hands them in one
  at a time. `image-object` is a resource declared `@kernel(memory-object)`, so the generated
  dispatch refuses a handle of any other kernel object and one that does not carry `read` and `map` -
  the service composes FROM these pixels and never into them, so it is never handed `write`. What is
  checked is the OBJECT'S OWN SIZE and not a length the client declared, because a supplier that said
  one number and handed over something smaller would be describing a buffer the service then reads
  past. A slot already filled for this generation is refused rather than swapped, a partially
  supplied queue answers `again` from `acquire-next`, and a generation change releases the whole set.
  DisplayService now allocates no client pixels at all.

  THE SURFACE STAYS WHERE IT WAS CREATED, ENFORCED AT THE SYSCALL. The surface channel's client
  endpoint is handed over carrying send, receive and wait and NOT `transfer` or `duplicate`, so
  moving it to another process fails in the kernel rather than being detected afterwards - which is a
  check no service can perform, because a message names the endpoint it arrived on and neither it nor
  `ObjectInfo` reports the holder. The producer endpoint carries `send` alone, the completion
  endpoint `receive` and `wait`, the event stream `receive` and `wait`, and the focus proof is the one
  capability that keeps `transfer`, because being handed to `input.subscribe-keys` is its purpose.

  THAT NEEDED A KERNEL PRIMITIVE AND IT IS THERE NOW. `SYS_CHANNEL_SEND_ATTENUATED` moves exactly one
  capability with a mask, and one is not enough for a reply that hands over a PAIR:
  `SYS_CHANNEL_SEND_CAPS_ATTENUATED` takes `[count, CapTransfer * count]` and applies a mask PER
  capability, under the same all-or-nothing transaction as the ordinary multi-capability send - a
  refused send leaves every one of the sender's handles open at the same value with its rights
  unchanged. Before it, the service had to add `transfer` back to every mask it wanted, because a
  capability without it cannot be moved at all, so the reply carrying it could not be sent.

  BOUNDS AND TYPED EXHAUSTION. Sixteen surfaces per connection, sixty-four across the service, three
  images to a queue, three accepted presents outstanding to a surface, sixteen damage rectangles to a
  present (the wire's bound, so more never reaches a handler). Exhausting one answers `exhausted` and
  releases what the attempt had taken: a refused present puts its image back to `Acquired` and gives
  back the serial it had reserved.

  WAITABLE TASK IDENTITY. `display-admin.bind` now declares `@rights(manage, wait)` and
  PermissionManager duplicates with both, and the service's one progress loop waits on every bound
  client's process alongside its channels. A process that ends has its surfaces released and its
  connection given back even though this side of the channel never moved - which is the half a
  channel cannot answer, since a channel stays open while anyone holds its peer.

  OBSERVATION THROUGH SYSTEMGRAPH, AND ON AN ENDPOINT THAT CAN DO NOTHING ELSE. A new
  `liber:display@1/display-stats` interface with one operation answers `display-resources`: live
  surfaces, queue slots, queued presents, retained damage entries and loop waiters, each beside the
  bound it is held to, plus the scanout reset count and the fault state. It is a SEPARATE root from
  `display-admin` on purpose - authority to read what a service holds must not be authority to bind a
  process to a display or take the screen - and SystemGraphService is handed that root and nothing
  else. `component` gained `resources: list<resource-count>`, which is the generic shape any service
  with structures the kernel charges nobody for can report through.

  EVIDENCE: the guest passes 68 tests over `channel,ipc,handle` including the new per-capability
  attenuating send, and 37 over `display,console,imgview,lico,input,mouse,permission-service,text`.
  The display suite now asserts the rights on every capability the service hands out, a partially
  supplied queue answering `again`, four separate typed refusals of a bad supply, the bounded counts
  before and after the clients come and go, and a bound client's process ending while this side of
  its connection never moves.

- [x] Add hostile-input and lifecycle tests before anything is built on this: malformed format,
  extent and pitch descriptors, overflowed image sizes, invalid generations, duplicate acquire and
  present, completion races, driver death, truncated messages, forged imports, a surface channel
  offered from a second process, per-connection bound exhaustion and cleanup during in-flight
  presentation. Fuzz every parser and every checked
  layout helper; prove that a malicious client cannot map another surface, retain foreground input
  or leave the console scanout blank.

  **DONE 2026-09-14.** `display_service_refuses_hostile_input` is the guest half: a zero on ONE axis
  of an extent and an extent past what the service will hold are typed refusals rather than guesses
  or clamps; a message too short to name a call and one with a trailing byte are both unanswered and
  the service is still there afterwards; a CHANNEL where an image belongs is refused by the schema's
  own type guard before the service is reached; a third acquire on a two-image queue answers `again`
  rather than blocking; an image nobody acquired, an image already consumed by a present and a frame
  drawn for an unacknowledged serial are three separate refusals and none of them reaches the device;
  a background surface is told `not-visible` and cannot mint the focus proof; a surface that HAD the
  screen and lost it is refused the proof, which is what stops a window that was once in front from
  reading the keyboard behind the one that is; the seventeenth surface on a connection is
  `exhausted` and the connection going away gives all sixteen back; a close that arrives while a
  frame is at the driver is answered AFTER the present rather than instead of it; and the driver
  going away makes every later present complete `driver-lost` with the service still serving and the
  console still on the screen.

  TWO OF THE LIST ARE ANSWERED STRUCTURALLY RATHER THAN BY A TEST, and are recorded here as that.
  A malicious client cannot map another surface because the service hands out no images at all any
  more - the pixels are the client's own memory objects, imported one way - so there is nothing to
  ask for. A surface channel offered from a second process fails at the syscall, because the
  endpoint arrives without `transfer`; the guest asserts the rights it arrives with.

  DRIVER DEATH WAS A DEFECT AND IS FIXED WITH IT. A present after the backend went away reported
  `displayed`, because `flush` returned `Ok(())` whenever there was no driver channel - which is
  also the boot framebuffer, a backend that works. The scanout now carries `lost` separately from
  "there is no driver", and a frame composed into a mapping nobody is reading completes
  `driver-lost`.

  FUZZING IS A DETERMINISTIC SWEEP, because that is what a build gate can run. `display-proto` gained
  a sweep over every generated record's `decode` and over all four generated dispatches, with both an
  empty and a non-empty handle list, across every op the package defines and several that are not
  ops; `graphics-core` gained one over `ImageLayout` for every format at the boundary extents and
  pitches, recomputing each span in `u128` so an answer smaller than the truth fails there rather
  than becoming a read past a buffer somewhere.

## P02M0103b - Render2D Core Profile 1

The public, backend-neutral 2D API. This part defines the PROFILE; `P02M0103c` implements all of it.
Depends on the `s-2d` FREEZE and on `P02M0103a-common`, and on no backend - NOT on `a-wsi`, which is
the correction that makes the stated delivery order possible: recording and validating a display list
needs the image and colour model and needs no display, no present queue and no completion pairs.
(The `s-2d` half was missing from this header while the freeze section called it a hard prerequisite,
so a reader starting here could begin `b` against an unfrozen profile. Added 2026-09-01.)

**THE PART IS IMPLEMENTED** (2026-09-12). The `render2d` crate carries the profile, the recording
`Canvas`, the immutable versioned `DrawList` with its typed resource table and canonical internal
encoding, the `prepare`/`render` backend split with its prepared-key invalidation, the one flattening
(adaptive, in device space, clipped against the `w = epsilon` plane in homogeneous space before the
divide), the boolean operations and the query surface, and damage computation - with the host tests
this section requires, all of them running without a backend.

- [x] Define `Render2D Core Profile 1` as a closed, enumerated list, and make "complete" mean this
  list. A conforming backend implements every entry; `Unsupported` is reserved for extensions added
  after Profile 1 and may never be returned for anything in it. The profile:

      geometry     paths (move/line/quad/cubic/close), non-zero and even-odd fill,
                   rect, rounded rect, circle, ellipse, arc, line, polyline, polygon
      strokes      width, butt/round/square caps, miter/bevel/round joins, miter limit,
                   dash pattern and offset
      antialiasing analytic coverage for fills, strokes and glyphs
      transforms   affine AND projective (3x3 with perspective terms)
      paints       solid, linear/radial/conic gradient, image pattern;
                   multi-stop, clamp/repeat/mirror spread, own transform, linear-light stops
      clipping     rect, rounded rect, path, nested, alpha mask, inverse
      layers       group opacity, blend mode, bounded nesting
      compositing  the 12 Porter-Duff operators plus additive Plus, and every separable
                   and non-separable blend mode below
      images       source/dest rect, arbitrary projective transform, nearest/bilinear/
                   bicubic, mipmapped minification, clamp/repeat/mirror, opacity,
                   colour-space conversion
      filters      a bounded FilterGraph, not three special cases
      text         glyph runs: outlines, grayscale masks, bitmap strikes, colour-layer
                   glyphs, embedded colour bitmaps, glyph transforms, subpixel positioning
      queries      path boolean ops, hit testing, tight and stroke bounds, length,
                   point and tangent at distance

  DONE. `src/user/libs/graphics/profile` holds it as `enum Render2DFeature` with 110 variants and
  `RENDER2D_CORE_PROFILE_1` over them: every line of the table above, at the granularity of
  "something a backend can fail to do" - `CapButt` and `CapRound` are separate entries because a
  rasteriser can get one right and the other wrong. The crate has NO dependencies, so a backend, a
  conformance suite and a documentation generator can all be checked against the same list, and it
  is host-testable, which a crate reaching the runtime would not be. Fixtures hold the list closed:
  no name twice, no group nobody is in, and the twelve Porter-Duff operators plus additive `Plus`
  counted rather than read.

- [x] Make BOTH profiles machine-readable, because a profile that exists only as a list in a Markdown
  file drifts away from the backend and the conformance suite the first time someone adds a feature
  in one place. The canonical form is code - `enum Render2DFeature`, `enum Render3DFeature`, and a
  `RENDER2D_CORE_PROFILE_1` / `RENDER3D_CORE_PROFILE_1` constant listing exactly what each contains -
  and the documentation table, the capability report, the backend checklist and the conformance
  matrix are all GENERATED from it, with a profile hash so a change is visible. A gate then checks
  three things no reviewer reliably catches: every mandatory feature has at least one conformance
  test, every profile feature has a backend handler, and no test claims a feature outside the
  profile. This is what keeps "complete" true a year after it is first true, and it is cheap only if
  it exists before the two backends do.

  Each feature names its OWNER - `GraphicsCore`, `Render2D`, `Render3D`, `Scene3D` or `Backend` -
  because the "every feature has a backend handler" check is wrong for features no backend
  implements. Path boolean operations, hit testing, bounds, length and point-at-distance are
  geometry-core functions that `soft2d` never sees; demanding a backend handler for them would force
  a stub whose only purpose is to satisfy a gate. The handler check applies to `Backend`-owned
  features; the conformance-coverage check applies to all of them.

  DONE, for both. `Render3DFeature` and `RENDER3D_CORE_PROFILE_1` are in the same crate with the
  same shape - 89 entries, one per sentence of the 3D list below - so one generator and one gate
  read both instead of each being written twice. `FeatureOwner` carries all five owners. From the
  two constants `src/tools/profile-doc` generates, under `docs/gen/render2d` and `docs/gen/render3d`:
  the documentation table, the backend checklist, the conformance matrix and the capability report,
  plus a `profile-1.canonical` the SHA-256 profile hash is taken over - so a feature added, removed
  or reordered, or a minimum lowered, changes a hash printed in the table.
  The three checks are `src/tools/check-graphics-profile.sh`. Claims are markers in the source -
  `@handles:` on the code that implements a feature, `@covers:` on the test that measures it - so a
  deleted handler takes its claim with it, which a registry beside the code would not. A name
  neither profile has is refused, and so is a handler for a feature no backend owns.
  NOT PERFORMED, and the gate says so rather than printing a pass: no backend and no conformance
  suite exist yet, so the handler and coverage halves range over nothing - 89 and 110 features
  awaiting a claim in 2D, 80 and 89 in 3D. Because a clean tree proves nothing about a scan that has
  stopped matching, the tool has a `--self-test` that builds a small tree - a real claim, a sentence
  that only mentions the marker, a name outside the profile, a handler for a feature no backend owns
  - and asserts each answer before the gate is trusted to approve anything.
  THE 3D LIST IS NOT 3D WORK STARTING. This item requires both profiles to be machine-readable and
  sits in the 2D part, so the enumeration is written here; nothing in `render-math`, `render3d`,
  `render-shader` or any 3D backend exists or is started by it, and the part below stays unapproved.

- [x] Bound `render2d` with a `Render2DLimits` value, which the 3D side has and this side does not:
  maximum commands, resources, path verbs, path points, subpaths, clip depth, layer depth, filter
  nodes, filter radius, glyphs per run, image extent, layer pixels, prepared scratch bytes, cache
  bytes and display-list bytes. A recording API without limits is an unbounded allocation driven by
  application data, which is the shape this tree bounds everywhere else. The guaranteed minima are in
  the profile document so that "supports Profile 1" cannot mean "accepts ten path points".

  DONE. `Render2DLimits` carries all fifteen bounds and `RENDER2D_PROFILE_1_MINIMA` is the
  guaranteed floor; `meets_profile_1()` refuses a declaration below it and NAMES the field that is
  short, which is the difference between raising the right number and comparing fifteen pairs by
  hand. The minima are hashed with the profile and generated into the profile document, so lowering
  one is a changed hash rather than an edit nobody reads.

- [x] DECIDED 2026-08-30, and the decision is the item below it. What `DrawList` transport actually
  has to do in v1, rather than promising the largest version of it. "Transportable to a compositor" is currently required, and it drags in a whole
  contract - capability transfer for every referenced resource, font, image and path lifetime across
  processes, schema compatibility, a validating parser as a security surface, cache invalidation -
  for a consumer that does not exist, while a real compositor mostly wants finished surfaces rather
  than every application's vector command stream. Profile 1 requires an IMMUTABLE, VERSIONED,
  CACHEABLE list replayable in-process, which is what the caching, damage analysis and backend
  independence above actually need. Cross-process transport becomes a named `render2d-wire` extension
  designed against a real consumer - and if it is kept mandatory instead, then canonical byte order
  and alignment, resource ID plus generation, capability ownership and rights, immutable snapshot
  semantics, unknown-command and version handling, resource hashing and prepared-list invalidation
  are all part of Profile 1 and none of them is written yet.

- [x] State what a stroke width MEANS under a transform, which decides what a scaled or projectively
  transformed stroke looks like and is currently unsaid: whether width, dash lengths and the miter
  limit are measured in user space before the transform or in device space after it, with an explicit
  `StrokeScaling::{WithTransform, NonScaling}` if both are wanted. Under a projective transform the
  two differ enough that a UI drawn with one and tested against the other is visibly wrong.

- [x] Introduce `render2d` as a separate library from any rasteriser, and make that the boundary a
  DONE 2026-09-12. The twelve items around this one are one crate, `render2d`, because a canvas
  without paints, paths, layers, filters, glyph runs, a list, a prepared key and an error type is a
  struct with no calls on it.
  WHAT A CANVAS PRODUCES IS A LIST AND NOT PIXELS, and that indirection pays for itself before the GPU
  backend it was also chosen for exists: the same list is cacheable per component, analysable for
  damage, replayable, and TESTABLE WITHOUT ANY BACKEND AT ALL - which is why recording, validation,
  transform algebra, limits and the encoding round trip are all host tests.
  THE STATE AT THE TIME OF THE CALL IS RECORDED WITH IT. A list whose commands referred to a mutable
  state object would draw differently depending on when it was replayed.
  `restore` WITHOUT A `save` IS AN ERROR AND NOT A NO-OP: treating it as one is how a component that
  restores once too often silently inherits its parent's clip, and the drawing that results is wrong
  somewhere else, in a component that did nothing. An UNCLOSED LAYER is refused rather than replayed,
  because its contents went into an offscreen nothing composites - a drawing that is simply missing,
  with nothing to say why.
  DEDUPLICATION IS NOT AN OPTIMISATION HERE. A component that draws the same rounded rectangle forty
  times records it once, which is what keeps the resource ceiling meaningful: a list storing forty
  copies would exceed it for a drawing that has one shape in it.
  STROKE SCALING IS `WithTransform` OR `NonScaling` and the default is stated. A shape scaled up should
  usually get a thicker outline; a hairline, a selection rectangle and a diagram's grid should stay one
  pixel at any zoom. An API with only the first makes the second a caller dividing by its own zoom,
  which is wrong under rotation and meaningless under perspective.
  THE TRANSFORM IS PROJECTIVE, because the affine version is the one that has to be replaced later - a
  card flip, a page turn and a map tilt are perspective. A point at or beyond the horizon has NO image
  and answers `None` rather than a very large number: dividing by a `w` near zero produces a vertex at
  ten million pixels and a rasteriser that spends a second on one triangle. A rotated rectangle's
  bounds take all FOUR corners, because a bound computed from two is smaller than the drawing - which
  is how a damage rectangle comes to clip the thing it was computed for.
  A PATH ANSWERS A HIT TEST, under both fill rules, flattened at the profile's ONE tolerance - which is
  why a point can never be inside for a hit test and outside for the fill that drew it. And an
  application that cannot ask implements its own geometry, which is the private-rasteriser failure one
  level up. Tight bounds are separate from loose ones because a control point is often well outside
  the curve, and a layer sized by the loose bound allocates an offscreen bigger than it needs, every
  frame.
  A FILTER GRAPH IS ACYCLIC BY CONSTRUCTION - a node may only read nodes before it - rather than by a
  check a later edit can defeat. Its bounds map runs BACKWARDS from the output, which is the direction
  the question runs: a caller knows what it wants drawn and needs to know what to read.
  THE VALIDATION BOUNDARY IS THE LIST, once, so a backend may then assume. Every handle is TYPED: a
  single integer index shared by paths, images, fonts and filters is an index a validator cannot check,
  because index seven is a valid path and a valid image and the drawing that confused them still
  replays.
  THE CANONICAL ENCODING IS AN INTERNAL BINARY FORM AND NOT A WIRE ABI - a cache key is a hash of
  bytes, so the list has a byte form whether or not anything sends it anywhere. A fixture requires
  every field it carries to CHANGE the digest, because a field the encoding loses is a field a cache
  hit loses.
  AND CONTENT IS NOT STRUCTURE: a replaced image invalidates a prepared list and a new video frame does
  not, while still producing a different drawing digest - so the preparation survives and the raster
  cache does not. Each of the prepared key's dependencies is changed ALONE in the fixtures, which is
  the test the enumeration exists to make possible.
  12 fixtures in `render2d`, 12 passed. The operator and blend-mode enumerations are held to the frozen
  registry by one of them, because an enumeration that drifted would offer a mode the specification
  does not define - discovered by a conformance run rather than by a compiler.

  future GPU backend replaces. An application, a UI toolkit and a game talk to `render2d`; they never
  name `soft2d`.

- [x] Give it a `Canvas` with saved state and a recorded `DrawList`. The immediate-mode surface is
  `save`, `restore`, `set_transform`, `concat_transform`, `set_clip`, `set_opacity`, `set_blend_mode`,
  `begin_layer`, `end_layer` plus the drawing calls - but what it PRODUCES is a bounded, validated
  display list rather than pixels. That indirection is what makes the same drawing replayable by a
  CPU backend now and a GPU backend later, cacheable per component, analysable for damage,
  and testable without any backend at all - and SERIALISABLE LATER without a redesign, which is the
  weaker and true version of the "transportable to a compositor" this line used to claim (corrected
  2026-08-30: that phrasing was the third of the three places the file promised a transport Profile 1
  does not have; see the `DrawList` item below). A canvas that writes
  straight into a slice is a CPU-only API however carefully it is written.

- [x] Specify the `DrawList` as Profile 1 requires it: IMMUTABLE, VERSIONED, CACHEABLE and
  REPLAYABLE IN-PROCESS. DECIDED 2026-08-30, and this item is what the decision replaced - it
  previously required a TRANSPORTABLE cross-process format, in the same part that decides against
  one, so the file carried two mandatory and mutually exclusive `DrawList` contracts and `P02M0136`
  could not build the shared font-resource seam against either.
  What Profile 1 keeps from the transportable form, because caching, damage analysis and backend
  independence need it: a versioned command schema; a resource TABLE with typed handles for paths,
  images, fonts and filters rather than inline references; stated resource lifetime and immutability;
  deduplication; bounded command bytes and bounded nesting; a defined validation boundary; and
  thread-safe replay and cancellation.
  What it drops, because a cross-process consumer does not exist and a compositor mostly wants
  finished surfaces rather than every application's vector command stream: canonical byte order and
  alignment, capability ownership and rights per referenced resource, cross-process snapshot
  semantics, and the validating parser as a security surface.
  A typed handle into a process-local resource table is NOT a raw Rust reference, and that is what
  makes the same list serialisable later without a redesign: cross-process transport becomes the
  named `render2d-wire` EXTENSION, designed against a real consumer, and adds byte order, rights and
  snapshot semantics on top of this schema rather than instead of it.

  AND PROFILE 1 STILL HAS A CANONICAL ENCODING, which is what two surviving requirements elsewhere in
  this file were actually asking for (decided 2026-08-30). The backend-free host tests asked for a
  "display-list serialisation round-trip" and part `b`'s Done asked the list to "round-trip through
  its transportable form", both of which read as the cross-process format this item just deferred.
  They are kept, renamed, and given a meaning that does not contradict it.
