  result's luminance is the backdrop's or the source's, by the profile's own coefficients.

  TWO SCENES ARE NOT PROFILE ENTRIES AND ARE RUN ALL THE SAME: wide-gamut composition and the
  quantisation at the end of a frame. A profile entry is something a backend can fail to do, and
  these are properties of the path every entry takes - so folding them into a feature would make that
  feature fail for two reasons, and leaving them out would mean the suite never drew a colour the
  target cannot hold. The gamut one probes WHITE, which is common to both gamuts and is where a wrong
  matrix lands beside the answer; the dither one puts a value a third of a step above a code over a
  region and requires BOTH neighbouring codes to appear and to average back to the value.

  AND `Unsupported` IS A FAILURE. The profile is a closed list, so a backend refusing a Profile 1
  drawing is a backend that does not conform; the run reports the two apart so a reader can tell
  "this is wrong" from "this is missing", and fails for either.

  WRITING IT FOUND FOUR THINGS, WHICH IS WHAT A CONFORMANCE SUITE IS FOR.
  THE SHAPE CONSTRUCTORS DID NOT EXIST. The profile lists rounded rect, circle, ellipse, arc, line,
  polyline and polygon and `PathBuilder` had `add_rect` alone, so seven entries were claimed by a
  profile nothing implemented. They are now in `render2d::shape`, with their conventions FROZEN in
  the profile's geometry module and generated into the spec document: which way a closed shape is
  wound, where a circle starts, that it is four cubics at a stated control ratio, how an arc's angles
  are measured and what it does about the current point, that an over-large corner radius scales all
  four corners by ONE factor, and that a negative radius is refused rather than clamped. None of that
  is decoration - a dashed circle whose first point is a quarter turn away has its dashes somewhere
  else.
  SIX FILTER NODES DID NOT EXIST. Convolution, the two morphologies, the displacement map, the crop
  and the tile were profile entries with no node behind them. Each is now in `FilterGraph` with a
  bounds map, a ceiling and a canonical encoding, evaluated in `soft2d`, and each one's MEANING is
  frozen in the profile's per-node table: a three by three kernel and not a general size, a
  RECTANGULAR structuring element and not a disc, `scale * (channel - 0.5)` so that a flat half-grey
  map is the identity.
  `tight_bounds` PUT A CUBIC'S EXTREMUM IN THE WRONG PLACE. The derivative's middle coefficient was
  doubled relative to the other two, so the peak of a symmetric curve came out at three quarters of
  the way along instead of at the half - a bound too small by a quarter, which is a layer allocated
  short and a damage rectangle that leaves a strip undrawn.
  A GLYPH RUN WAS NOT TRANSFORMED. The pen is in USER space and the glyph is drawn in DEVICE space,
  and the backend placed the form at the recorded coordinates - so text in a scrolled, scaled or
  rotated drawing stayed where it was recorded while the drawing moved. The pen is now mapped, and
  the subpixel phase is taken from the DEVICE position rather than the user-space one, which is the
  drift subpixel positioning exists to remove.

  THE GUEST GATE IS `kernel.applications.the_2d_profile_conforms_on_the_target`, tagged `slow`. It
  holds NO capability at all - every scene draws into memory it allocated itself and reads the pixels
  back - so a failure is about the profile rather than about a service that happened to be running,
  and it asserts the program's own count of failures, refusals and untested entries rather than only
  its verdict. On x86_64, aarch64 and riscv64 it reads `112 passed, 0 failed, 0 unsupported, 0
  untested` and `conforms` - the same verdict on all three, and the same one the host run gives,
  which is the whole of what "implemented on three architectures" can mean. The first version of the
  gate printed NOTHING, because a loop polling its end of a channel spins at the same priority as the
  program it is waiting for: the suite has to be given the processor.

- [x] Add one governed PIE named **2D Test SW**, canonical artifact `test2d-sw.lsexe`, only
  `display + input-keys` grants, Esc/q exits, Space pauses, R resets. It renders THROUGH `render2d` -
  an application-local rasteriser or a duplicate drawing loop is not accepted, and that includes
  generating any test image. This is the INTERACTIVE proof; the suite above is the exhaustive one,
  and neither substitutes for the other.

  DONE 2026-09-14. `bin/test2d-sw.lsexe`, granted `display` and `input-keys` in the permission
  manager's table and nothing else, drawn through `render2d` into the surface's own images by
  `soft2d`. Esc and q exit, Space pauses, R resets, the arrows scroll the clipped column.
  ITS SCENE NEEDS NO CAPABILITY TO BE ITS SCENE. The shapes are computed, the pattern image is
  generated into memory it allocated, and the glyph forms - including a COLOUR one - are the
  program's own, so a line of text with an emoji in it is drawn without a font catalogue or a volume.
  What that proves is `render2d`'s half of text: the shaping stack has its own conformance run and
  its own capability, and a demo holding one would be proving something else.
  THE DETERMINISTIC CONTROLS ARE LAUNCH ARGUMENTS AND NOT KEYS - `--frames`, `--phase-frames`,
  `--no-input`, `--no-second-surface` - because a key that changed the run is a key a person can
  press by accident. They came out of the launch CONTEXT and not out of its bytes, which is the first
  thing this got wrong: a program scanning the encoded record for its own flags finds none of them
  and runs with its defaults, which here was a demo that never ends.
  AND IT ASKS FOR THE SCREEN'S OWN SIZE. A surface with a logical size of its own is not reconfigured
  when the output changes, so the demo asking for 640 by 480 never saw the resize at all - which is
  the phase this whole scene exists around.

- [x] The scene demonstrates the platform an application would actually use: filled and stroked
  Bezier paths under both fill rules, antialiased edges at a shallow angle, a projectively
  transformed image, a multi-stop conic gradient, a rounded-rectangle clip with content scrolling
  under it, a nested and an inverse clip, a group-opacity layer containing two overlapping children,
  several non-`SourceOver` blend modes including a non-separable one, a backdrop-blurred panel, and a
  line of text including a colour glyph.

  DONE 2026-09-14, and every item of that list is in one drawing rather than in a row of swatches: a
  self-intersecting star filled under the EVEN-ODD rule and stroked over itself, a sliver four
  hundredths of a pixel per column off horizontal, a checkerboard under a perspective transform, a
  four-stop conic background, a rounded-rectangle clip with a column scrolling under it holding a
  nested clip and an inverse one, a group-opacity layer whose two children overlap, Multiply, Screen
  and LUMINOSITY patches, a backdrop-blurred panel over the scene, and an eleven-glyph line whose
  last glyph is a colour-layer one.
  THE SCENE IS LAID OUT FROM THE EXTENT IT IS GIVEN. Fixed pixel sizes put the moving objects past
  the edge of a small surface, and a damage rectangle that leaves the surface is a present the
  service REFUSES - which is a demo that stops with nothing to say.

- [x] It runs in PHASES, because "an animated background" and "two non-contiguous damage rectangles
  that reach the driver as two" contradict each other - a background that changes every frame changes
  every pixel, and its only honest damage is `Full`:

      1. full        everything drawn, damage = Full
      2. partial     background held still, objects move, damage = old bounds + new bounds
      3. multi-rect  exactly two distant regions change; the driver stand-in sees TWO rectangles
                     before any backend merging
      4. full-again  background parameters change, damage = Full
      5. resize      new generation, and its first frame is Full by the rule above
      6. scale       the scale factor changes with the logical size held constant; layout is
                     unchanged and every edge is resolved at the new physical resolution

  ALL SIX RUN (2026-09-15). The demo runs full, partial, multi-rect and full-again on its own frame
  counter, and the last two are entered by a REBUILD and by nothing else - because "the first frame
  of a new generation" is not a number of frames, it is a thing that happened to the surface. The
  gate drives a device resize under it and then a SCALE change over the display admin channel, and
  reads back which phase each one took it to.
  THE SCALE PHASE WAS IMPLEMENTED AND UNREACHABLE, and what was missing was in the SERVICE: the demo
  tells a scale change from a resize by comparing the configuration it rebuilt to - the same logical
  size with a different SCALE is the scale phase - and `DisplayService` answered every surface with
  `scale = 1:1` and had no source for another value. It has one now. `display-admin` gained
  `set-scale`, beside `set-visible` and for the same reason: a client that could set the scale would
  be deciding how much memory every OTHER client's images need, so it is an answer only something
  holding the whole screen may give.
  WHAT A SCALE CHANGE IS, stated once so the two rebuilds cannot be confused: every surface keeps the
  LOGICAL extent it has and its PHYSICAL extent becomes that times the ratio. A window is the same
  size on the desk and is resolved at more pixels. It is a new generation with a new image set,
  exactly like a resize, and the configuration is what tells them apart.
  AND THE TWO EXTENTS STOPPED BEING ONE NUMBER. `Surface` carried a single extent that was both, plus
  a `console` flag doing duty as "native-sized"; a surface now carries its logical extent, its
  physical one and whether it asked for the output's own size. That last separation was a defect on
  its own: `console` is the FIRST native surface, so a SECOND surface that asked for the output's
  size did not follow a resize at all - it kept an extent the output no longer had and was told about
  the change by a configuration whose numbers had not moved.
  THE RATIO IS BOUNDED AND REFUSED WHOLE. One eighth to eight, no zero on either side, and every
  surface's new extent is computed before any of them is touched - a scale one surface cannot express
  leaves the output at the scale it had, rather than a screen whose windows disagree about what a
  logical pixel is. The refusals are asserted against the real service, and the accepted case is
  asserted twice: as a configuration the service emits (logical held, physical doubled, a new
  generation) and as the phase the demo reports having entered.
  AND THE MIDDLE PHASE IS THE ONE THE WHOLE DAMAGE MODEL IS FOR: two distant regions reach the driver
  as TWO rectangles, checked at the device end of the path rather than at the application end of it.
  The gate reads the damage region out of the present the device sees and counts the shapes.

- [x] Prove the multi-surface model, since `P02M0103a-wsi` introduced it and an untested object model is
  a claim: open a SECOND surface and switch which surface is visible, presenting to each while it
  owns scanout. Hidden surfaces refuse new acquires with `NotVisible`; already accepted frames
  complete or discard in order. Resize or close one and verify the other's resources and generation
  survive. This proves independent lifetimes and queues, not simultaneous popup composition.

  DONE 2026-09-15, in `kernel.services.the_2d_demo_draws_a_real_scene_with_real_damage`. The demo
  opens a SECOND surface of its own after its phases and presents to it; the gate then creates a
  THIRD from another connection while the demo is still drawing, which takes the screen, and drops it
  again. Both halves of the switch are asserted: the demo reports `visibility=2` for the transitions
  it saw and `background=18` for the passes it spent idling, the gate proves NOTHING of the demo's
  reached the device while it was hidden, and the surface that took the screen runs the whole
  handshake - configuration, acknowledge, queue, four supplied images, acquire, present - with its
  frame reaching the DEVICE while it owns the scanout. Then the resize lands on the demo's surface,
  which rebuilds and carries on: the other surface's generation and resources survived the switch.
  EVERY DRAIN IN THAT SEQUENCE IS BOUNDED, and it has to be: the ordinary surface helpers call
  `run_until_idle`, which against a client pacing itself on a timer - and the demo is one, still
  running - never returns. The sequence pumps a tick at a time and answers the focus and device
  channels on every pass, because that is what the service is blocked on in between.
  THE `NotVisible` REFUSAL ITSELF is asserted where it is cheapest to see - the display service's own
  scenario, which acquires from a surface that is not the visible one and reads the typed answer -
  and what this gate adds is the same rule from the APPLICATION's side: a frame loop that is told it
  is in the background stops drawing rather than queueing frames nobody will see.

- [ ] It passes only when: the conformance suite passes on all three architectures with no profile
  entry unsupported; clean exit AND crash both restore the text console; a resize never presents a
  stale image; phase 3's two rectangles travel the whole path from application to driver as two; a
  live QEMU capture proves animation, antialiasing, blending, filtering and text; the package audit
  proves ET_DYN, provider identity and order, the exact grants and no statically duplicated drawing
  code; there is no steady-state allocation once the queue and resources exist; and a measured
  steady-state live frame budget at 640x480 and one HiDPI scale is recorded in `docs/PERF.md`.
  The accepted implementation also passes the shared HEADLESS benchmark from `c`, under its exact
  reference-host conditions, at or under each 640x480 scene's ceiling and frozen budget. Live
  presentation and HiDPI measurements are reported separately and do not redefine that floor.

  FOUR OF THESE HOLD ALREADY (2026-09-14), and the gate that shows them is
  `kernel.services.the_2d_demo_draws_a_real_scene_with_real_damage`: the conformance suite passes on
  all three architectures with nothing unsupported, phase 3's two rectangles reach the DRIVER as two
  (read out of the present the device sees), the resize is survived by a rebuild rather than by
  presenting an image drawn for the old generation, and the run ends cleanly with its own report.

  AND BOTH SUITES WERE RUN AGAIN ON ALL THREE PORTS AFTER THIS ROUND'S CHANGES (2026-09-19), WHICH
  IS THE ONE CLAIM A DECODED-SOURCE FIX INSIDE THE MIP CHAIN COULD HAVE BROKEN ON A TARGET THAT WAS
  NOT MEASURED. `--tags image,slow`: aarch64 34 tests in 2097 s, riscv64 34 in 2489 s, against an
  x86_64 run of the same suites the same day. The three ports do not merely all pass - THEY REPORT
  THE SAME NUMBERS:

      test2d-conformance   112 passed, 0 failed, 0 unsupported, 0 untested   conforms
      test3d-conformance   render3d 89, scene3d 71, 160 total, all zero      conforms

  `0 untested` on each registry separately is the half of `h` that is about COVERAGE rather than
  passing, and it is reported per registry rather than as one total precisely so that a suite which
  stopped exercising one of them could not hide inside the other's count. The 2D and 3D demos and
  `imgview` ran beside them on both emulated ports and passed.

  AND A TIMING NUMBER FROM THIS RUN IS NOT COMPARABLE TO ONE FROM ANOTHER SELECTION, which is worth
  writing down because it looked like a regression for an hour. `imgconv_cross_volume` costs 94 s in
  a `drivers,pci,slow,storage,usb,network,service` run on aarch64 and 1351 s in this `image,slow`
  one, with riscv64 agreeing at 1098 s. Nothing about the code differs: the first selection RUNS THE
  DRIVER TESTS, so `vol://system` is served by a bound block driver by the time the application
  tests reach it, and the second does not. A cross-run timing comparison is only a measurement when
  both runs selected the same work.
  AND IT RUNS ON ALL THREE PORTS (2026-09-15): the guest gate passes on x86_64, aarch64 and riscv64
  with the same counters - `visibility=2`, two rebuilds, `phase=5`, two presents from the second
  surface - and the demo's own draw time there is in `docs/PERF.md` beside what it means, because the
  two emulated ports are TCG and the number a reader will otherwise mistake for the port being slow
  is twenty to twenty-four times the x86_64 one.
  THE SCALE PHASE REACHES ALL THREE TOO, which is the half that would otherwise be an x86_64 claim:
  the same `set-scale` over the same admin channel, the same logical extent held, the same new
  generation, on each port.
  AND THE LIVE GATE WAS INTERMITTENT AND THE CAUSE WAS THE SCENE (2026-09-15). It failed with "the
  colour glyph contributes 0 pixels of its own palette", passed on a re-run, and failed again; the
  captures said what a log could not. One showed a clean orange ring with a dark centre, the next a
  flat magenta where it had been - the multi-rect phase's patch sits at the top-left corner, BLINKS
  between two sizes six frames apart, and at the larger one it covered the line of text completely.
  The scene was drawing over its own text half the time, and the gate only started catching it
  because a faster renderer changed which tick a capture lands on. The text moved below the patch and
  left of the star; the checker's band moved with it, because the two are one fact.
  AND TWO WEAKNESSES IN THE CHECKER WENT WITH IT. It asked every question of the FIRST frame, over a
  scene that animates across itself - so a capture with a mover parked on a sampled region read as a
  property that is not drawn; each question is now asked of all three and needs one to answer, which
  is not a weakening because three captures of a drawing that never drew a colour glyph contain no
  colour glyph. And a capture taken before the demo's first present shows the CONSOLE, whose boot log
  is white text on black - so "the line of text is being drawn" passed on a console screen while every
  colour check failed, and the gate blamed the glyph for its own timing. The gate waits for a frame
  with the scene's coloured background on it, and every frame is asked the same question.
  THREE CONSECUTIVE GREEN RUNS after that, where before it was one in two.

  AND THE LIVE HALF IS DONE TOO (2026-09-15): `./check.sh --gate qemu-2d-demo` boots a guest unless
  one is up, runs the demo, captures THREE timed frames off the real screen and checks pixels for the
  five things a log cannot say - that the picture MOVES, that the shallow edge carries partially
  covered pixels rather than a staircase, that the group-opacity overlap is a MIX of its children,
  that the projectively transformed image is filtered rather than nearest-sampled, and that the line
  of text holds both a white run and a colour glyph in its OWN palette. The checker fails all six of
  its checks on a capture of the console with no demo running, which is how a positive result is
  worth something.
  AND WHAT THE DEMO HELD IS RECLAIMED WHEN ITS PROCESS ENDS, which the gate reads back through the
  service's OBSERVATION root: zero surfaces and zero queue images once the demo is gone. The demo
  destroys nothing on its way out, so what is being measured there is the reclamation a CRASH gets.
  AND THERE IS NO STEADY-STATE ALLOCATION IN THE RECORDING EITHER (2026-09-15), which was the half
  that was missing. `restart` already kept the BUILDER's tables and their capacity; what it could not
  keep was the finished list, because `finish` cloned the commands and every resource vector into a
  fresh `DrawList` - a `Vec` per resource kind, sixty times a second, for ever. `finish_into` writes
  into a list the caller already holds, `clone_from` keeps the allocation and overwrites the
  elements, and the demo holds ONE list for its whole run. Both halves are needed: either alone
  leaves the loop allocating.
  AND A REFUSED RECORDING LEAVES AN EMPTY LIST, by one rule whichever check refused it - the
  canvas's balance checks, which refuse before writing anything, and the list's own validation, which
  refuses half way through. An empty list is legal and draws nothing; the alternatives are a list
  whose handles do not resolve, or the PREVIOUS frame's, which a caller that ignored the error would
  present again as though it were this frame's.
  THE FIXTURE ASSERTS CAPACITY AND NOT LENGTH, because an equal length is exactly what a reallocating
  implementation also produces: the same scene is recorded seventeen times and the command buffer's
  and the resource table's capacities may not move.
  THE PACKAGE AUDIT IS PART OF THE SAME GATE and runs before anything boots, because it answers a
  different question: what the ARTIFACT is. Position-independent on all three targets, declared
  providers that the built imports confirm, at least one imported symbol OWNED by each of `render2d`,
  `soft2d`, `graphics-app` and `surface` - which is what "no statically duplicated drawing code"
  means as a check, since a static copy owns nothing and imports nothing - and exactly the two grants.
  AND IT DOES NOT GROW WHILE IT DRAWS: the guest gate samples the demo's own charged memory six
  frames apart inside one generation, with the queue and every resource already made, and refuses an
  increase. That is the checkable half of "no steady-state allocation": a per-frame allocation that
  is freed again is invisible to the kernel's accounting, and a loop that charges another page every
  frame is exactly what this catches.
  THE FRAME BUDGET IS MEASURED AND RECORDED in `docs/PERF.md`: 88.3 ms mean to draw a 640x480 frame
  and 141.4 ms between presents, in a booted guest. The shape is the useful part - the draw is most
  of the interval, so the loop waits for the renderer and not for the display.
  AND IT IS THE RELEASE BUILD ON THE TARGET, which that note used to deny (corrected 2026-09-15). It
  said the debug profile because `./build.sh` builds the static services and drivers at `dev`, and the
  staged PIE applications do not come from there: every consumer object and every provider library is
  compiled `--release` into an image target directory that has no `debug` tree at all. So "the release
  measurement on the target" was already taken and was recorded as something else.
  AND THE HiDPI SCALE IS MEASURED TOO (2026-09-15), now that the service can report one: the same
  scene at the same logical size with its physical extent doubled costs 40.1 ms against 25.5 ms -
  FOUR times the pixels for 1.6 times the time, because recording the list and the per-tile setup do
  not grow with resolution and only the coverage and composite terms do. That ratio is what a HiDPI
  budget should be planned against, and it is measured rather than assumed. It is taken in the guest
  suite rather than the live boot for a reason worth stating: nothing in a booted image SETS a scale
  yet - the ratio is the system's to choose, and the only thing holding the admin channel is
  PermissionManager. That is a missing operator control, not a missing capability, and it belongs to
  whoever writes the compositor or the operator tool.
  AND THE THING THAT WAS IN THE WAY OF THE LIVE HALF IS FIXED (2026-09-14). The full boot-chain test
  hung after ConsoleService's first frame, and under it were two defects. The test's own drain was
  UNBOUNDED - `run_until_idle` returns when the run queue empties and the next deadline is beyond its
  own, which a system with ConsoleService pacing frames never reaches - so the collection loop was
  never entered. With that bounded, the real one appeared: `SystemGraphService` and the `Shell` never
  came online, because DisplayService's OBSERVATION root answered `resources()` and not the reserved
  CONNECT op, while ServiceManager mints an independent connection from it and BLOCKS on the reply.
  The root is a factory now, like every other root in this system. The whole x86_64 guest suite
  passes: 399 tests.

## P02M0103e - render-math and Render3D Core Profile 1

The public, backend-neutral 3D API - resources, pipelines, passes, shaders and command lists. This is
the layer whose absence the seventh pass was written to correct. Depends on the `s-3d` FREEZE and on `P02M0103a-common`, and NOT
on `a-wsi` (2026-08-30; the `s-3d` half added 2026-09-01, for the reason the `b` header gives): 
`render3d` is a non-presenting consumer - it renders into targets and returns - so it needs the
image, format and colour model and nothing that owns a display.

- [x] `render-math` is a CONTRACT and not a type list, and this file picks the values rather than
  requiring that someone pick them - "state the handedness" is not normative, a handedness is:
  angles in RADIANS everywhere, COLUMN-MAJOR storage, `M * v` as the transform, RIGHT-HANDED world
  and view space, clip-space depth in `[0, 1]`, counter-clockwise front faces. Constructors are named
  for what they encode - `perspective_rh_zo`, `orthographic_rh_zo` - so a caller cannot pick the
  wrong convention silently. Quaternions state their normalisation policy: constructors normalise,
  operations preserve unit length, and a zero-length quaternion is an error rather than a NaN
  generator. Inversion of a singular matrix returns a typed error and never a matrix of infinities,
  and every entry point states what it does with NaN and infinite inputs.

  Four of those choices only become unambiguous together, and leaving the last one out inverts what
  gets culled: `+Y` is up in world and view space, the camera looks along `-Z`, NDC `+1` is the TOP
  of the image, the surface row origin is `TopLeft`, and the viewport maps
  `y_window = (1 - (y_ndc * 0.5 + 0.5)) * height`. Front-face culling is evaluated BEFORE that Y
  inversion. A Y flip reverses apparent winding, so an implementation that culls after it culls the
  opposite faces while satisfying every other sentence in this file.

  DONE 2026-09-14. `src/user/libs/graphics/render-math` is the crate, `no_std`, no allocation, no
  `unsafe`, and every value above is picked in its module documentation and enforced by its API.
  THE NAMES CARRY THE CONVENTIONS AND THERE IS NO GENERIC CONSTRUCTOR: `perspective_rh_zo`,
  `orthographic_rh_zo` and `look_at_rh` are what exists; `perspective` does not, because a
  generically named constructor is how a caller picks the wrong handedness and finds out three layers
  later when the depth test is inverted.
  THE QUATERNION POLICY IS THREE SENTENCES AND THREE ASSERTIONS: constructors normalise (an axis of
  length five gives a unit quaternion), operations preserve unit length (a THOUSAND composed products
  are still unit, which is the drift that otherwise turns a rotation into a scale over a long run),
  and a zero-length quaternion is `Error::ZeroLength` rather than four NaNs that look like a rotation
  until something renders.
  EVERY ENTRY POINT STATES WHAT IT DOES WITH NaN AND INFINITY, and it is ONE rule rather than a
  sentence per function: anything returning a `Result` checks its inputs are finite and answers
  `NotFinite`; ordinary arithmetic propagates, because that is what IEEE 754 does and branching on
  every add to pretend otherwise would cost more than it buys; and `is_finite` is public on every
  type so a caller at a boundary this crate does not own can apply the same rule.
  INVERSION REFUSES BY NAME. The determinant is taken in `f64` - a projection matrix's cofactors span
  several orders of magnitude and one that cancels to zero in `f32` is a refusal nobody can act on -
  and a matrix whose inverse would hold an infinity is `Error::Singular` rather than that matrix.
  THE FOUR CHOICES THAT ONLY WORK TOGETHER HAVE THE FIXTURE THEY NEED. `facing` takes NDC positions
  and `window_from_ndc` is documented as coming after it, and one test asserts BOTH halves: a
  counter-clockwise triangle in NDC is the front, and the same three vertices are CLOCKWISE once the
  viewport has inverted Y. An implementation that culled after the inversion passes every other
  sentence in this item and fails that one.
  AND IT CARRIES ITS OWN `sqrt`, `sin`, `cos` AND `acos`, because `no_std` has none and taking a
  maths library here would put one in every consumer. Each is held against the host's `f64` version
  over the range it is used in, including at every quadrant boundary, which is where a range
  reduction is wrong if it is wrong anywhere.
  Eighteen fixtures, each holding a CONVENTION rather than an implementation: the values are
  hand-computed or computed in `f64` from the definition, so a rewrite that changes the arithmetic
  and keeps the contract passes.

- [x] Define `Render3D Core Profile 1` as a closed, enumerated list on the same terms as the 2D one:
  a conforming backend implements every entry, and `Unsupported` is reserved for post-profile
  extensions. THE LIST ITSELF NOW EXISTS, written under the 2D part's "make BOTH profiles
  machine-readable" item and not as an activation of this part: `Render3DFeature` and
  `RENDER3D_CORE_PROFILE_1` in `src/user/libs/graphics/profile`, with the generated table, backend
  checklist, conformance matrix, capability report and profile hash under `docs/gen/render3d`.

  DONE 2026-09-21, AND BOTH REASONS IT STAYED OPEN ARE GONE. The first was the approval, which
  arrived. The second was written when it was true - "the shader model, the strict-float rules and
  the rest of this part are untouched" - and the two items that carry them are now `[x]` above and
  below this one, the second of them closed on 2026-09-18 when `soft3d` and the conformance suite
  gave its third half somewhere to live.

  THE LIST WAS CHECKED AGAINST THE ENUMERATION BELOW GROUP BY GROUP RATHER THAN ASSUMED, because a
  list that exists is not the same as a list that says what this item asks for. 89 entries in nine
  groups, and the nine are the nine named below: resources 8, geometry 16, pipeline 10, passes 9,
  depth 15, msaa 6, sampling 15, formats 7, readback 3. Every phrase in the table has an entry and
  no entry is without a phrase - "MSAA resolve" is counted once, under `msaa` rather than twice
  under `passes`, and the depth formats likewise sit in `depth` rather than being repeated in
  `formats`, which is why those two group counts do not match a naive reading of the prose.

  AND BOTH CLAUSES OF THE CRITERION ARE ENFORCED RATHER THAN STATED. "A conforming backend
  implements every entry" is `Tally::complete`, which requires `untested` to be empty; "`Unsupported`
  is reserved for post-profile extensions" is the same function requiring `unsupported` to be zero,
  so a backend REFUSING something in Profile 1 is a backend that does not conform rather than one
  with a gap. `soft3d` answers all 89: the suite reports `render3d 89 passed, 0 failed, 0
  unsupported, 0 untested` on the host and in a booted guest on each target.

  The profile:

      resources    buffers, 2D textures, cube maps, 2D arrays, 3D textures, samplers,
                   mipmaps, render targets
      geometry     u16/u32 indices, multiple vertex streams, configurable attributes,
                   triangle list/strip/fan, line list/strip, point list, primitive
                   restart, indexed and non-indexed, instancing, base vertex,
                   base instance
      pipeline     programmable vertex and fragment stages, vertex layout, primitive
                   topology, rasteriser state, depth/stencil state, blend state per
                   attachment, colour write masks, viewport and scissor
      passes       render passes with load (clear/load/discard) and store (store/discard)
                   per attachment, multiple colour attachments, depth/stencil attachment,
                   MSAA resolve, offscreen render targets, render-to-texture
      depth        Depth16, Depth24, Depth32F, Depth24Stencil8, Depth32FStencil8;
                   all compare ops, depth write, depth bias; stencil compare, read/write
                   masks, fail/depth-fail/pass ops, separate front and back state
      msaa         1x, 2x, 4x; sample masks; alpha-to-coverage; resolve
      sampling     nearest/linear min and mag, nearest/linear mip, trilinear,
                   clamp/repeat/mirror/border, LOD bias and min/max LOD, depth-compare
                   sampling, anisotropic filtering to max_anisotropy 8
      formats      R8, RG8, RGBA8, RGB10A2, RGBA16F, RGBA32F, R32_UINT, the depth formats
                   (encoding is ColorSpace's, never a second sRGB storage format)
      readback     colour, depth and object-ID attachment readback

- [x] Introduce a PROGRAMMABLE shader model, `render-shader`, and treat it as the largest single item
  in this part - fixed-function Lambert and Blinn-Phong cannot be a complete renderer, because a
  procedural material, a picking pass, a scientific visualisation, a postprocess and every effect an
  application invents are all "a different fragment function". The model is a VALIDATED PORTABLE IR:
  vertex and fragment stages, typed uniforms, textures and samplers, varyings with `smooth`,
  `noperspective` and `flat` interpolation, bounded loops, no recursion, and checked memory access.
  `soft3d` interprets it first; a JIT or a translation to a GPU shader representation comes later
  without changing the API.

  Explicitly NOT Rust closures. A closure is the obvious shortcut and it is a dead end: a future GPU
  backend cannot take one, so the day the GPU backend arrives every shader in every application is
  rewritten. Ship a programmatic IR BUILDER so this milestone does not also have to write a GLSL or
  WGSL front end - a text shading language is a later, separate, optional piece.

  "A validated portable IR" is a description, and Profile 1 needs the enumeration. TYPES: `bool`,
  `i32`, `u32`, `f32`, their 2/3/4-component vectors, `mat2`/`mat3`/`mat4`, and arrays with a
  compile-time length. OPERATIONS: arithmetic, comparison, logical and bitwise, conversions, vector
  and matrix products, `dot`, `cross`, `normalize`, `min`, `max`, `clamp`, `mix`, `exp`, `log`,
  `pow` and the trigonometric set. CONTROL FLOW: `if`, `switch`, bounded `for` and `while`, `break`,
  `continue`, `return`, `discard`. VERTEX BUILT-INS: vertex and instance index, base vertex and base
  instance, the position output, point size. FRAGMENT BUILT-INS: fragment coordinate, front-facing,
  sample index, sample mask, and depth output. OUTPUTS: several colour attachments, an INTEGER
  attachment for object IDs, depth, and the sample mask.

  Two additions the profile's own requirements force. Interpolation needs `centroid` and `sample`
  beside `smooth`, `noperspective` and `flat` - without them MSAA shades at sample points that can
  lie outside the primitive and edges go wrong in a way that looks like a rasteriser bug. And
  DERIVATIVES - `dFdx`, `dFdy`, `fwidth` - are not optional here: the profile mandates implicit mip
  level selection and anisotropic footprints, and both are computed from derivatives. State how they
  are evaluated for a partially covered quad.

  Resource binding gets a real model rather than one `bind_resources` call: a `BindingLayout`
  declaring binding index, resource type, array length and stage visibility; `BindGroup`s built
  against a layout; and dynamic offsets. Shader IR and pipeline state otherwise have no shared
  contract about what binding 3 means.


  DONE 2026-09-14. `src/user/libs/graphics/render-shader` is the crate: `ir` is the enumeration, a
  programmatic `Builder` is what is shipped instead of a language, and `validate` is what refuses.
  THREE THINGS ARE UNREPRESENTABLE RATHER THAN CHECKED, which is stronger than validating them.
  RECURSION: there is no call instruction, so a stage is one body and a cycle cannot be written. AN
  UNSTRUCTURED JUMP: control flow is `If`, `Switch`, `Loop`, `Break`, `Continue`, `Return` and
  `Discard` over nested bodies, with no label and no `goto`, so there is no irreducible graph to
  analyse. AN UNBOUNDED LOOP: `Loop` CARRIES ITS TRIP COUNT AS A FIELD, so a loop without one does not
  type-check - a `while` is a `Loop` with a `Break` in it and the bound is still required, which is
  what makes a frame's worst case computable.
  THE BUILDER DOES NOT VALIDATE, on purpose. Construction and validation are separate because a
  builder that refused as it went could not express a forward reference, and a module is validated
  ONCE at load with the whole program in front of it - which is where the position dependency slice
  can be walked at all.
  WHAT VALIDATION ESTABLISHES RATHER THAN ASSUMES: single assignment, because a builder with a bug
  produces a module that reads an undefined value and a backend would read whichever register was
  there; the loop bound and the nesting depth against the device; a constant index inside its array;
  `break` and `continue` inside a loop and `discard` inside a fragment stage; a stage that writes what
  it exists to produce; and a stage that does not write the OTHER stage's outputs.
  Sixteen fixtures, and the ones that matter are the refusals: an IR that validates a correct module
  and nothing else has not been shown to validate anything.
- [x] Enforce StrictF32 on every data and control dependency contributing to position, including
  transcendental operations such as sin/cos/pow/sqrt/normalize, conversions and indices that select
  contributing values. `SHADER_IR_1.md` supplies the deterministic operation semantics, operand
  order, rounding, no-FMA policy and non-finite handling. A contributing operation with no strict
  profile definition is rejected by IR validation, not silently evaluated with host libm tolerance.
  Non-position values may use RelaxedF32. This applies to the complete dependency slice, not only
  the final matrix multiply.

  Quantize position into `ClipCoordQ` before classification. `RENDER3D_PROFILE_1.md` freezes its bit
  width, fractional bits, signed range, rounding, overflow policy and intersection arithmetic before
  `e` starts. Classification, clipping topology and quantized raster coverage consume that canonical
  value and are bit-exact; relaxed varyings remain tolerance-based. Cross-architecture cases place
  strict transcendental-derived vertices on both sides of clip boundaries.

  TWO OF THE THREE HALVES ARE BUILT (2026-09-14), AND THE THIRD NEEDS A RASTERISER.
  THE ENFORCEMENT IS DONE, in `render-shader::validate`. StrictF32 applies to every DATA AND CONTROL
  dependency of a position, and the CONTROL half is the one a naive implementation misses: a position
  SELECTED by `if (sin(t) > 0)` is as backend-dependent as one computed from `sin(t)`, because two
  backends take different branches at the boundary. So every enclosing branch's condition is on the
  slice of everything the branch stores, and a fixture asserts exactly that case - two positions
  neither of which touches the transcendental, refused because the BRANCH does.
  AND IT IS THE COMPLETE SLICE AND NOT THE FINAL MULTIPLY, with its own fixture four steps deep: a
  matrix built from a transcendental is what a real shader looks like, and a check that walked one
  step back from the position store would admit it. A memory INDEX is on the path too, because an
  index that differed by one between backends would read a different vertex.
  WHICH OPERATIONS ARE ALLOWED IS DERIVED FROM THE FROZEN ACCURACY TABLE rather than restated: a
  zero-ULP bound is what "a strict definition" means, so `sqrt` is allowed and `inversesqrt` at two
  ULP is not. An accuracy corrected in the profile corrects this without a second edit. THE REFUSAL
  IS AT MODULE LOAD and names the operation, its bound and how many steps from the position it was
  found - "this shader is not deterministic" is not something anybody can act on.
  AND A COMPOSITION IS ON THAT PATH TOO (2026-09-14). `normalize` and `length` are not single IEEE
  operations - a sum of products and a root - so two backends that compose them differently produce
  different positions, and the validator walked through both because they are not transcendentals.
  Each is now FROZEN AS A COMPOSITION of operations the profile already fixes: `length` is
  `sqrt(dot(v, v))` accumulated in component order with separate multiply and add, and `normalize` is
  `v / length(v)` and NOT a multiply by `inversesqrt`, which carries two ULP and would make every
  normalised position backend-dependent. A vector of zero or non-finite length normalises to itself.
  The validator refuses any composition with no frozen entry rather than walking through it, and a
  fixture enumerates the whole operation set so that one added without freezing it fails the gate on
  the day it is added and not on the day two architectures disagree about a picture.
  AND A FRAGMENT STAGE DOES NOT ACQUIRE STRICT REQUIREMENTS BY SHARING THE IR WITH A VERTEX ONE,
  which is its own fixture: a colour that differs by one ULP is not a wrong picture.
  `ClipCoordQ` AND THE CLIPPER ARE DONE, in `render3d::clip`. It is a NEWTYPE and not a `Vec4`,
  because what it carries is the promise that the value came out of the vertex stage and has not been
  divided - a divided position would pass every check and be wrong. The plane order is held against
  the frozen list name for name; the clip is homogeneous and at `f32` with the parameter computed and
  KEPT at `f32`; and the three answers the profile distinguishes are three: a non-finite component
  REFUSES the primitive, `w <= 0` everywhere CULLS it, and `w <= 0` somewhere clips against
  `w = CLIP_W_EPSILON` FIRST. A fan triangulation answers indices so the original primitive's
  provoking value cannot be taken from a vertex the clipper invented.
  WHAT IS LEFT IS THE THIRD HALF AND IT IS NOT THIS ITEM'S TO BUILD: "quantized raster coverage"
  needs a rasteriser, which is `soft3d`, and the cross-architecture cases that place
  transcendental-derived vertices on both sides of a clip boundary need the 3D conformance suite.
  Both are their own items in the parts below, and this box stays open until they exist.

  BOTH NOW EXIST (2026-09-18), AND THE CASE IS WRITTEN. `soft3d` rasterises from a fixed-point
  subpixel grid, so coverage is a decision about quantized numbers rather than about floats; and the
  conformance suite carries the case, inside `pipeline::ProgrammableVertexStage`, where the stage that
  computes the positions lives. It builds a triangle whose coordinates come out of `sqrt` - the
  correctly rounded root, at `sqrt(2) * 0.98994949 = 1.39999998`, which is past the right plane, and
  `sqrt(0.5) * 1.4 = 0.98994946`, which is not - so ONE VERTEX IS OUTSIDE AND TWO ARE INSIDE and the
  cut is where the root put it. It asserts that the clipper actually ran, that the surviving edge sits
  ON the boundary rather than short of it, and that the same scene twice is the same frame BIT FOR
  BIT, because a coverage decision has no tolerance.
  IT REACHES `normalize` AS WELL AS `sqrt`, because the composition is the harder half: a backend that
  spelled it as a multiply by `inversesqrt` carries two ULP and would put the vertex on the other side
  of the plane, and a case built only from `sqrt` would never have touched it.
  AND THE REFUSAL IS CHECKED ON THE PATH A BACKEND TAKES: the same stage with `sin` - four ULP in the
  frozen table - is refused before a frame runs, which is the profile's own stated rule rather than a
  validator a backend might not call.

  AND THE THREE PORTS HAVE NOW RUN IT (2026-09-18). The suite conforms on x86_64, aarch64 and
  riscv64, which means the same `sqrt`-derived vertex landed on the same side of the same clip plane
  on three different floating-point implementations, and the same `normalize` composition put it
  there. That is what the strict-float rule is FOR, and it is the first time it has been evaluated as
  a claim about architectures rather than as a rule a validator enforces.

  SO THIS BOX CLOSES, AND ON EXACTLY THE TWO THINGS IT SAID IT WAS WAITING FOR. "Quantized raster
  coverage" needed a rasteriser: `soft3d` rasterises from a fixed-point subpixel grid, so coverage is
  a decision about quantized numbers and not about floats, and its own tests pin the shared-edge case
  that makes that visible. The "cross-architecture cases that place transcendental-derived vertices
  on both sides of clip boundaries" needed the conformance suite: it exists, it carries that case,
  and it has now been evaluated on all three ports.

  WHAT IS NOT CLAIMED BY CLOSING IT: the suite runs ONE such case, not a family of them. It is the
  case the item names - a vertex a strict transcendental puts either side of a plane - and a second
  backend or a wider set would be `h`'s to add, not this item's to have anticipated.

- [x] Provide the command model: `CommandList`, `RenderPass`, `RenderTargetSet`, `GraphicsPipeline`,
  `ShaderProgram`, `VertexLayout`, `Buffer`, `Texture`, `Sampler`, `PipelineState`, with
  `begin_render_pass`, `end_render_pass`, `bind_pipeline`, `bind_vertex_buffer`, `bind_index_buffer`,
  `bind_resources`, `set_viewport`, `set_scissor`, `draw`, `draw_indexed`, `draw_instanced`. This is
  a USERSPACE rendering model and is not the OS-level adapter/device/queue vocabulary that stays
  deferred - the difference is that this one has two planned implementations and that one has none.

  DONE 2026-09-14 in `render3d::command`. THE VALIDATION IS AT THE CALL AND NOT AT SUBMISSION, which
  is the whole point: a list that records anything and validates later has moved every error message
  away from the code that caused it, and one that validates at the call can say "this draw has no
  pipeline bound" while the caller is still in the function that bound one. A draw outside a pass, a
  pass inside a pass, a pass ended that was never begun, a list finished with a pass still open, a
  command recorded into a finished list and a list finished twice are each refused where they happen.
  EVERY BINDING IS PER PASS, with its own fixture: a pipeline bound in one pass is NOT bound in the
  next, because the attachments changed and its compatibility with them has to be established again -
  carrying the binding across would make that check silent.
  THE BASE VERTEX IS SIGNED AND THE RANGE IS CHECKED THROUGH IT. `first_index + count - 1 +
  base_vertex` is the largest index a draw can reach, and a negative base with a small index is a read
  BEFORE the buffer - the case a per-index check at draw time cannot catch and this one can.
  A SCISSOR OUTSIDE THE VIEWPORT IS REFUSED RATHER THAN CLAMPED: it can only clip fragments that do
  not exist, so refusing names the mistake where clamping hides one. A viewport with no area is the
  same decision.
  AND `draw_instanced` IS `draw` WITH A COUNT rather than a second entry point, because two functions
  whose only difference is a `1` are two places for the validation to diverge.
  NOTHING HERE OWNS A RESOURCE: a `Buffer`, a `Texture` and a `Sampler` are the caller's own
  identifiers, which is what makes the model backend-neutral rather than a description of one
  backend's handles.

- [x] Define asynchronous-capable submission in `render3d` before implementing either backend:
  `prepare -> submit -> Submission { completion, status }`, plus readback tickets using the same
  completion/status contract. Completion may already be ready for synchronous software execution;
  pending results expose a waitable progress source and a typed terminal result. Retain resources
  while work is in flight, bound outstanding submissions and readbacks, define cancellation,
  backend loss and release ordering, and refuse overflow without partial publication.

  Reuse the ordinary-channel completion primitive and ownership-consuming tokens chosen above;
  a renderer's bounded completion storage is reserved when preparing/creating the backend, not
  allocated once per steady-state frame. This is an internal backend API and needs no surface or
  DisplayService. The graphics-app helper waits for renderer completion before publishing WSI
  producer-ready; a render submission and a displayed-frame outcome are different completions.
  `s-3d` freezes the exact ticket and resource-lifetime rules; the software backend and a pending
  test implementation exercise the same public shape without adding a GPU driver or thread pool.

  DONE 2026-09-14 in `render3d::submission`, and BEFORE either backend - which is the point of the
  item. A software backend finishes inside `submit` and a GPU one does not, and an API shaped around
  the first has no place to put the second: the completion becomes a return value, the resources are
  released at the end of the call, and the first pending submission needs a different function.
  Defining it now means the software backend implements the asynchronous contract with an
  already-ready completion, which is a legal value of it rather than a special case.
  `Status` HAS FIVE VALUES AND NOT AN `Option<Result>`: `Pending`, `Complete`, `Cancelled`,
  `BackendLost` and `Failed`. "No answer yet" and "no answer ever" have the same shape in an
  `Option<Result>` and are different things to a caller.
  THE COMPLETION IS OWNERSHIP-CONSUMING - `wait` takes it by value - so "waited for exactly once" is
  a property of the type rather than a convention, and a second wait would answer for a submission
  whose resources are already released. The same shape the 2D profile's presentation completion has.
  RESOURCES ARE RELEASED IN SUBMISSION ORDER AND NOT AS EACH FINISHES, with the fixture that makes it
  visible: the second submission settles first and NOTHING is released, because the first still holds
  its own; when the first settles, both come back in order. A backend may complete two out of order
  where nothing observes it, and releasing out of order would let a caller reuse a buffer an earlier
  submission still holds.
  THE QUEUE IS BOUNDED AND AN OVERFLOW PUBLISHES NOTHING: the submission is not queued, the caller's
  resources are still its own, and the serial is not spent - a queue that half-accepted would leave a
  caller unable to tell which. The bound is also what makes the completion storage a FIXED
  RESERVATION at backend creation rather than an allocation per frame.
  CANCELLATION IS NOT A ROLLBACK and says so; a LOST BACKEND takes every submission with it including
  the ones that had finished, because their results are no longer readable.
  AND A READBACK TICKET USES THE SAME COMPLETION AND STATUS rather than a second contract: a readback
  is a command IN a list and completes with it, so its ticket is the submission's completion with a
  destination attached.

- [x] Complete the RESOURCE model, because naming resource kinds in a profile and naming `Buffer` and
  `Texture` in a command list does not yet say how one is created or what part of it a pass writes.
  Descriptors: `BufferDesc` with size, usage and host visibility; `TextureDesc` with dimension,
  extent, mip levels, array layers, sample count, format and usage; `TextureViewDesc` with aspect,
  base mip and count, base layer and count, and view dimension; and `RenderTargetView` /
  `DepthStencilView` built from views rather than from whole textures - a shadow pass renders to one
  cube face and one mip, and a model without subresource views cannot say that. Operations: create
  and destroy, map and unmap, write buffer, write texture, buffer-to-buffer, texture-to-texture and
  buffer-to-texture copies, blit, resolve, generate mipmaps, clear and readback.

  Hazard behavior follows the single rules frozen in `RENDER3D_PROFILE_1.md`: simultaneous sampling
  and rendering of a subresource, attachment aliasing, visibility between passes, discard/read-after-
  discard and view/resource lifetime. That specification chooses each refusal or defined dependency
  before implementation; neither backend may decide locally. Reading discarded contents is a typed
  error. Tests cover each rule and resource lifetime across pending submissions.

  DONE 2026-09-14 in `render3d::resource`. THE DESCRIPTORS ARE ALL FOUR AND THE VIEWS ARE WHAT A PASS
  RENDERS INTO: `RenderTargetView` and `DepthStencilView` are built from a `TextureViewDesc` and carry
  the subresource they name, because a shadow pass renders to ONE CUBE FACE AND ONE MIP and a model
  whose attachments are whole textures cannot say that.
  `TextureViewDesc::overlaps` IS WHAT EVERY HAZARD RULE IS REALLY ABOUT. Sampling mip 2 while
  rendering into mip 0 of one texture is not a conflict, two cube faces are not a conflict, and depth
  and stencil are different planes - so `refuse_sampled_attachment` is per SUBRESOURCE and admits the
  common case a whole-texture test would refuse.
  THE TWO HAZARDS THE PROFILE REFUSES ARE REFUSED WHERE IT SAYS: a texture sampled while it is an
  attachment of the same pass, at submission, because the read depends on tile order and refusing it
  is the only answer that does not make the picture depend on the backend; and a host write to a
  buffer a submission owns, at the WRITE rather than at the submission, so the report names the thing
  that is wrong.
  READING DISCARDED CONTENTS IS A TYPED ERROR, and `Contents` has THREE states rather than two:
  `Undefined` is a subresource nothing has written and `Discarded` is one a pass threw away. Both
  refuse a read and they say different things, which is what a report needs. `Contents::after`
  carries the rule that LOADING WHAT WAS DISCARDED IS STILL DISCARDED - a pass that keeps contents
  nobody may read has kept nothing.
  AND A DESCRIPTION THAT DESCRIBES NOTHING IS REFUSED WHERE IT IS WRITTEN: a zero extent, a cube map
  that is not square or not six-at-a-time, a mip chain longer than the extent has, a 3D texture with
  layers, a buffer of no bytes, a readback buffer that is not a copy destination. A multisampled
  texture is refused three ways - no mip chain, not sampled, and no way to be written unless it is an
  attachment - because it is a render target rather than a texture.

- [x] Enumerate the BLEND STATE, since "blend state per attachment" cannot be conformance-tested
  without knowing what a state may contain. Factors: `Zero`, `One`, `SrcColor`, `OneMinusSrcColor`,
  `DstColor`, `OneMinusDstColor`, `SrcAlpha`, `OneMinusSrcAlpha`, `DstAlpha`, `OneMinusDstAlpha`,
  `ConstantColor`, `OneMinusConstantColor`, `ConstantAlpha`, `OneMinusConstantAlpha`,
  `SrcAlphaSaturate`. Operations: `Add`, `Subtract`, `ReverseSubtract`, `Min`, `Max`. Separate colour
  and alpha state, a blend constant, per-attachment enable and a colour write mask.

  DONE 2026-09-14 in `render3d::blend`, with every factor and every operation the list names and the
  arithmetic that applies them - so a backend does not write its own answer to what
  `OneMinusDstAlpha` means and the conformance suite compares one.
  IT IS A DIFFERENT TYPE FROM 2D COMPOSITING AND DELIBERATELY SO. `graphics-core` carries the
  Porter-Duff operators and the separable and non-separable blend modes `render2d` draws with; those
  are a painter's model. This is the fixed-function blend a 3D pipeline has: two factors and an
  operation, per channel group, per attachment. Re-using one for the other would make
  `SrcAlphaSaturate` a compositing operator and `Multiply` a blend factor.
  THREE THINGS THE ENUMERATION ALONE WOULD NOT HAVE SETTLED, each with its fixture: `SrcAlphaSaturate`
  IS ASYMMETRIC - the minimum on colour and ONE on alpha - which is what lets accumulated coverage
  reach opaque; `Min` and `Max` IGNORE THE FACTORS, which is what the operations mean and why they
  are not written as `Add` with different ones; and the write mask is applied AFTER the blend, so a
  masked channel keeps the DESTINATION rather than an unwritten blended value - the same answer for
  most states and a different one for `Min`, `Max` and anything reading `DstColor`.
  AND AN INTEGER FORMAT CANNOT BLEND, checked against the profile's own capability table rather than
  a second list: enabling blending on `R32Uint` is `UnsupportedFormat` naming the format and what it
  was used as, while leaving it disabled on the same target is fine - which is the deferred pass that
  writes colour to one attachment and object ids to another.

- [x] Fix the MSAA geometry rather than the sample count alone: the exact 2x and 4x sample positions,
  whether shading runs once per covered pixel or once per sample, the centroid location rule, the
  order in which the sample mask and alpha-to-coverage are applied, the alpha-to-coverage mapping for
  each count, and that resolve is an arithmetic average in linear premultiplied space. "4x MSAA"
  without sample positions is two different coverage results that both satisfy the words.

  DONE 2026-09-14 in `src/user/libs/graphics/render3d/src/msaa.rs`, which READS the frozen positions
  rather than restating them: `MSAA_2X` and `MSAA_4X` are the profile's, and one sample is the pixel
  CENTRE, which is the only position that makes a one-sample target the picture an unmultisampled one
  would be. A fixture holds the indices to being the first `count` integers, because they are the bit
  positions a mask is written in and a table that skipped one would make every mask wrong.
  THE SHADING RATE IS DERIVED AND NOT SET: a pipeline whose shader reads a `sample`-qualified input
  and whose state said per-pixel would be a contradiction with no correct resolution, so
  `shading_rate` takes the shader's answer.
  THE CENTROID IS THE CENTRE OF MASS of the covered samples, with the fixture asserting it is NOT the
  first covered sample's position - an average moves smoothly along an edge where that jumps between
  adjacent pixels, which is a seam in exactly the place antialiasing exists for.
  ALPHA TO COVERAGE IS THE FIRST `round(alpha * count)` BITS IN INDEX ORDER, with the rounding at the
  half asserted and out-of-range and NaN alphas clamped rather than producing a mask of arbitrary
  width. THE ORDER OF THE TWO MASKINGS is its own fixture: both narrow coverage BEFORE the depth
  test, so a masked-out sample is not tested and not written.
  AND THE RESOLVES ARE TWO RULES: the arithmetic mean for colour, in the attachment's own numeric
  space - the signature takes numbers rather than bytes, so the classic edge-darkening resolve in an
  encoded space is a mistake it cannot make - and sample 0 for integer, depth and stencil, because
  there is no average of two object ids that identifies anything.

- [x] Implement each depth/stencil format's mapping from clip depth, rounding, packing, clear,
  comparison, bias and stencil-operation semantics exactly as frozen in `RENDER3D_PROFILE_1.md`.
  `s-3d` selects one deterministic Depth32F storage/comparison rule before implementation; it must
  preserve the plan's bit-exact depth contract rather than allow backend-specific rounding or FMA.
  Depth16/Depth24 and stencil remain bit-exact too. The format table states stencil width/packing
  and depth-bias units. Conformance tests cover every format and comparison with boundary values.

  DONE 2026-09-14 in `render3d::depth`. `Stored` HAS TWO SHAPES AND NOT ONE NUMBER, because a
  normalised format compares INTEGERS and a float format compares FLOATS - a type holding both as
  `f32` would let the comparison be written once and be wrong for one of them.
  THE `Depth32F` ANSWER IS IMPLEMENTED AS WRITTEN: the stored floats are compared and the incoming
  depth is NOT quantised, with a fixture contrasting a value a 24-bit buffer stores as zero against
  the same value ordered correctly in a float one.
  THE BIAS UNIT IS THE FORMAT'S OWN, which is why the format is a parameter: one storage unit for a
  normalised format, `2^(exponent(z) - 23)` for a float one. The fixture asserts the step near zero
  is orders of magnitude finer than the step near one, which is the whole reason one number for both
  is wrong.
  THE STENCIL TEST PRECEDES THE DEPTH TEST and the operation is selected by BOTH outcomes - `on_fail`,
  `on_depth_fail`, `on_pass` - with a fixture per branch, plus one for the read and write masks
  selecting BITS rather than values, and one for all eight stencil operations at the boundary where
  the two that saturate and the two that wrap are told apart.

- [x] Bound the device rather than the frame: `Render3DLimits` states maximum attachments, texture
  extents and bytes, samples, vertex streams, attributes, uniform bytes, bound resources, draws and
  command bytes per list, shader instruction and loop bounds. Every request is checked against the
  implementation's hard maximum, what the raster arithmetic can represent, and the Domain's budget -
  but a failed check REFUSES or NEGOTIATES and never silently clamps: a caller given a quiet
  reduction finds out three frames later in a failure that names neither number. Over the hard
  maximum is `LimitExceeded`; over the Domain budget is `OutOfMemory`; and where less is granted than
  asked, construction returns the ACTUAL limits alongside the device.

  DONE 2026-09-14 in `render3d::limits`. `Render3DLimits::PROFILE_MINIMUM` is the floor, and
  `conforms` is DRIVEN BY THE FROZEN TABLE rather than by a hand-written list of comparisons: a
  minimum added to the profile without a field here is a refusal, which is what a hand-written list
  fails to be the first time the profile grows.
  A TEXTURE IS CHECKED AGAINST THE EXTENT AND THE BYTES, because either can be the one that fails: a
  4096x4096 RGBA32F texture is inside the extent limit and is 256 MB, and a device checking only the
  extent accepts it and fails later with no number attached.
  NEGOTIATION NEVER GRANTS MORE THAN WAS ASKED FOR, which a fixture asserts against a device with
  eight attachments and a caller that asked for four: a request is a statement about what the caller
  will use, and granting more would make the limits it plans against differ from the ones it was
  given. `Granted` carries `exactly_as_requested` so the caller is told ONCE rather than discovering
  it at the first pass that asked for more.

- [x] Typed errors throughout: `InvalidMesh`, `InvalidTransform`, `UnsupportedFormat`,
  `TargetMismatch`, `NonFinite`, `LimitExceeded`, `OutOfMemory`, `InvalidTexture`,
  `InvalidRenderState`, `InvalidShader`, `IncompatiblePipeline` - and `Unsupported`, which within
  Profile 1 is unreachable by construction and exists for extensions beyond it.

  DONE 2026-09-14 as `render3d::Error`, exactly the twelve named and no more, with a fixture that
  builds one value of each and asserts every pair is distinct - written as one value per variant
  rather than as a count, because a count passes when two are added and one removed.
  THREE PAIRS ARE KEPT APART ON PURPOSE AND THE FIXTURE SAYS SO: `LimitExceeded` against
  `OutOfMemory`, because one is "this implementation cannot" and the other is "not right now" and only
  the second is worth retrying; `NonFinite` against `InvalidTransform`, because a NaN in a vertex is
  the caller's DATA and a singular matrix is the caller's MATHS; and `TargetMismatch` against
  `IncompatiblePipeline`, because one is fixed by changing a target and the other by changing a state.
  EACH CARRIES WHAT A CALLER NEEDS TO ACT: `MeshFault` and `AttachmentFault` are closed sets rather
  than strings, and `LimitExceeded` carries the ceiling AND the number asked for, because a refusal
  naming neither is one a report cannot act on.

- [x] 2D and 3D share `graphics-core`'s image/resource vocabulary and need no ad-hoc bridge API.
  A compatible 2D single-sample color target can be an `OwnedImage` that either layer consumes.
  Multisample, HDR or otherwise incompatible attachments undergo explicit resolve or conversion
  whenever sample count, storage, alpha representation or color space differs. The reverse path
  likewise validates texture format and usage instead of promising that every image is sampled
  without conversion. Interop tests cover both the compatible path and required conversions.

  DONE 2026-09-14 in `render3d::interop`, and THERE IS NO BRIDGE TYPE - which is the point. A
  single-sample colour target in the same format, space and alpha representation IS the `OwnedImage`,
  and `bridge_for_attachment` answers `Direct`. What gets built instead, and did not, is a
  `Texture::from_image` that copies.
  WHAT IS NOT COMPATIBLE IS NAMED RATHER THAN CONVERTED SILENTLY, one variant per operation:
  `Resolve`, `ConvertFormat`, `ConvertColourSpace`, `ConvertAlpha`. THE ORDER OF THE CHECKS IS THE
  ORDER THE OPERATIONS WOULD RUN IN - a multisampled attachment is resolved first, and only then is
  its format and its colour meaning a question - so a caller is never told to convert something it
  has to resolve anyway.
  COLOUR SPACE AND ALPHA ARE SEPARATE VARIANTS from the format, because the memory can be identical
  and the meaning different: those are the two conversions that get skipped, and the second is the one
  that produces dark or bright fringes.
  AND THE REVERSE PATH VALIDATES RATHER THAN PROMISING. `admit_as_texture` refuses a format the
  profile does not have, and tells a FILTERED read apart from a point-sampled one - `RGBA32F` is
  sampled and not filterable in the profile's own table, and folding the two answers would make every
  integer texture unusable. A packed-mask framebuffer, which is what firmware hands over, is not a
  profile format and is refused rather than being treated as some format.

- [x] Host tests for this part run WITHOUT a backend: vector, matrix and quaternion identities,
  camera and frustum transforms, pipeline and vertex-layout validation, render-pass attachment
  compatibility, shader IR validation including rejection of unbounded loops, recursion and
  out-of-range access, command-list bounds, submission/readback state transitions, and every typed
  refusal. An asynchronous stand-in and an already-completed software result exercise the same API. Property and fuzz tests feed
  hostile shader IR, malformed vertex layouts and incompatible attachment sets.
  DONE 2026-09-14. 19 fixtures in `render-math`, 61 in `render3d` and 18 in `render-shader`, none of
  which needs a backend, a device or a frame.
  THE TWO BACKENDS GO THROUGH ONE API. A fixture builds an ASYNCHRONOUS stand-in that settles a
  submission when it is told to and an IMMEDIATE one that has finished before `submit` returns, and
  runs both through the same `Queue`, the same `Completion` and the same `ReadbackTicket`. The only
  difference is WHEN `settle` happens - the asynchronous one shows a window in which the retained
  resources belong to the submission and the immediate one never does, which is exactly why a caller
  must not be written against the immediate one.
  THE FUZZING IS DETERMINISTIC, seeded and reproducible: a fixture whose failing case cannot be
  reproduced reports a defect nobody can find. Three generators, each asserting BOTH halves of its
  rule - that hostile input is answered rather than crashing, AND that what is ACCEPTED satisfies the
  rule restated from the definition, because a validator that accepted everything would pass a
  "nothing panics" property without trouble.
  A thousand generated vertex layouts, each held against "an attribute is admitted if and only if it
  names a stream that exists and lies wholly inside its stride". A thousand generated attachment
  sets, each held against "one extent, one sample count, and one resolve slot per colour attachment
  or none", with the fixture failing if fewer than a hundred were refused - a generator that produced
  only legal input would be testing nothing. Ten thousand arbitrary command sequences, REPLAYED
  afterwards rather than trusted: every recorded draw is inside a pass and after a pipeline and a
  viewport, every recorded binding is inside a pass, and a finished list has no open one.
  And two thousand hostile shader modules, each a valid one with up to three mutations applied -
  reading an unassigned value, storing the other stage's output, removing the store the stage exists
  for, a trip count of zero or seventy thousand, a `break` outside a loop, a `discard` in a vertex
  stage, a transcendental spliced onto the position path, an index that cannot be in range. Plus the
  input that turns a recursive validator with no bound into a crash: two hundred nested loops, which
  comes back `LoopDepth` rather than a stack overflow.

## P02M0103f - scene3d, the retained scene layer

The convenience layer applications use when they do not want to build command lists themselves. It
generates `render3d` command lists and contains NO backend-specific path. Depends on `P02M0103e`.

**THE ENGINE FEATURES ARE PART `f-ext`, NOT THIS PART** (2026-08-30). Two rounds ago the header
moved LOD, skinning, morph targets, animation, PBR and shadowed lighting, sorted
transparency, HDR and postprocessing into an optional later profile, and one round ago the `f` Done
clause said the same - while every mandatory item in this section still required them, and so did
the conformance suite in `h`, the demo in `i` and the aggregate 3D gate. A feature that is optional
in three sentences and required in ten items is not optional. They now live in their own separately
activated part below, with their own profile, their own gates and their own dependency on this one.
Nothing in `f` implements or waits for them.

- [x] `scene3d` IMPLEMENTS - not "admits" - hierarchy with parent/child local and world transforms,
  cameras, lights, mesh instances, materials, render queues, bounding spheres and axis-aligned boxes,
  frustum culling, instancing, visibility masks, offscreen targets and a render-pass graph. The
  previous draft required these only to be possible to add later, which is the same deferral the
  seventh pass removed from `soft3d`: a scene layer that cannot express a hierarchy is one every
  application replaces. LOD selection, skinning, morph targets and an animation pose input are
  `f-ext`.
  DONE 2026-09-14. `src/user/libs/graphics/scene3d` is nine modules: `scene` (nodes, cameras,
  drawables, instances, limits, the dirty-flag traversal), `bounds`, `cull`, `queue`, `light`,
  `material`, `pick`, `graph` and `emit`. Registered in the shared-image manifest and in the release
  list as a host suite; 52 fixtures.
  THE DECISIONS ARE THE SCENE PROFILE'S, AND THE FIXTURES HOLD THE IMPLEMENTATION TO THE FROZEN LIST
  RATHER THAN TO ITSELF: one fixture reads every guaranteed minimum out of the registry by NAME and
  fails if the layer enforces one the document does not name or names one the layer does not
  enforce, and another reads the three queues and their depth-write answers the same way.
  The local transform is TRANSLATION * ROTATION * SCALE with the quaternion normalised on
  composition - a fixture distinguishes the order with a non-uniform scale under a rotation, which is
  the only arrangement where the wrong order gives a different answer. World transforms are computed
  lazily, once per frame, per dirty subtree, in one traversal over a TOPOLOGICAL order rebuilt
  depth-first when the shape changes: a node is recomputed when it is dirty or its parent was, so the
  propagation cannot be missed, and a fixture re-parents a node under a HIGHER-indexed one to catch
  an implementation that walks by index. A cycle and a depth past the limit are both refused at the
  parenting, and a refused re-parenting is undone rather than left half-applied.
  A drawable holds a LOCAL box; its per-frame volume is the world sphere derived from it with the
  LARGEST axis scale, and a fixture shows a re-fitted box growing under two eighth-turns while the
  sphere does not. A drawable with no bounds is never culled. Instances are culled ONE AT A TIME and
  compacted in their original order, and the set sorts ONCE by the sphere of the WHOLE set including
  the instances that were culled - so the key does not move as the camera does.
  Everything the layer produces is a `render3d` command: `emit::record` begins one pass, binds and
  draws, and ends it, eliding redundant binds and overriding the pipeline's depth write with the
  QUEUE's, so a transparent surface cannot be recorded writing depth whatever pipeline it carries.

- [x] Give `scene3d` its own closed profile, `Scene3D Core Profile 1`, in the same machine-readable
  registry as the other two. `Render3D Core Profile 1` is a low-level pass and pipeline API and does
  not contain hierarchy, materials, lights, sorted queues or picking - yet those are exactly what "a
  complete 3D library" means to an application, and with them outside every registry they are the one
  part free to drift away from its conformance suite and its documentation. A third
  `enum Scene3DFeature` and its constant closes that gap on the same terms: no `Unsupported` inside
  the profile, a test per feature, and the guaranteed minima in `SCENE3D_PROFILE_1.md`. The Extended
  features get their own closed `Scene3D Extended Profile 1` constant in the same registry, so
  "optional" is a profile a build either implements entirely or does not claim - not a set of
  features that may be half-present.
  DONE 2026-09-14. `graphics-profile::scene3d` now carries `enum Scene3DFeature` and
  `SCENE3D_CORE_PROFILE_1` beside the rules and the minima it already held: 71 entries in ten groups
  - hierarchy, camera, queues, culling, instancing, materials, lighting, picking, passes and limits.
  The granularity is the same one the other two use, "something an implementation can fail to do":
  the three queues are separate entries because a layer can carry the opaque one and get the
  transparent one's direction wrong, and a list that could only say "queues" would call that
  conforming. The same reasoning splits the four light kinds, the four materials and the three
  readbacks.
  `profile-doc` generates the checklist, the conformance matrix, the capability report and the
  canonical for it exactly as for the other two, and CLAIMS ARE ROUTED BY NAME rather than by a
  two-profile special case, so a fourth profile is a row rather than an edit.
  EVERY ONE OF THE 71 FEATURES HAS AT LEAST ONE CONFORMANCE TEST, which the gate now asserts instead
  of reporting "NOT PERFORMED": `@covers:` markers sit on the fixtures that measure each one. The
  five backend-owned entries - the three readbacks, their asynchrony and the offscreen target - have
  no `@handles:` claim yet because no 3D backend exists; the checklist names them and says so.
  THE SPEC DOCUMENT'S SLUG MOVED TO `scene3d-spec`, mirroring `render3d-spec`, so the feature list
  can hold the plain `scene3d` slug the way `render3d` does. The canonical moved from
  `docs/gen/scene3d/` to `docs/gen/scene3d-spec/`; `docs/graphics/SCENE3D_PROFILE_1.md` is unchanged
  and its hash is unchanged, which is what shows the move was a rename and not an edit.

- [x] Ship a STANDARD MATERIAL library over the programmable pipeline, so an ordinary application
  does not write shader IR to draw an ordinary object: `Unlit`, `VertexColor`, `Lambert` and
  `BlinnPhong`. `PbrMetallicRoughness` and its equation set are `f-ext`.
  DONE 2026-09-14. `scene3d::material` holds the four kinds and `shade` evaluates them, each fixture
  against a value worked out by hand: `Unlit` ignores every light even when one is supplied,
  `VertexColor` multiplies the vertex colour by the texture, `Lambert` is the ambient term plus the
  clamped cosine, and `BlinnPhong` adds a highlight about the HALF vector with the specular carrying
  its OWN colour. The exponent is an INTEGER in `1..=1024` evaluated by squaring: a fractional one
  needs `exp` and `ln`, which this stack does not have outside a shader, and the exponent is a loop
  count whose upper bound is what keeps a frame finite.
  WHERE THE EARLIER DRAFT'S EQUATION AND THE FROZEN PROFILE DISAGREE, THE FROZEN PROFILE WINS. The
  attenuation above is the one written here two passes ago, `1 / (kc + kl*d + kq*d*d)`; the
  registry freezes `1 / (1 + d^2/r^2)` with a multiplicative `saturate(1 - (d/range)^4)^2` window,
  which is the one implemented - it reaches EXACTLY zero at the range rather than leaving the edge a
  clamp produces, and it has no term that can divide by zero, so the `EPS` the older form needed has
  nothing to guard. The rest of the older clause is implemented as written: the specular carries its
  own colour, lighting never changes alpha, and the result is clamped to `[0, 1]` in linear light
  BEFORE the transfer function, because the core profile has no high dynamic range for a value above
  one to go to.
  A material's BLENDING decides its queue, its depth write and whether it writes a picking identity,
  so a node's queue changes when its material does and the two can never disagree. The per-draw blend
  state and colour write mask sit beside it - `render3d` carries blending in the pipeline rather than
  in a command - and a material whose blend state contradicts its blending is refused, which is the
  mistake that draws a transparent surface opaque.

  `BlinnPhong` keeps the exact equation earlier passes fixed, now as a LIBRARY material rather than
  as the renderer's only path, so its tests keep their single expected answer:

      base    = material.base * vertex_colour * texture_rgb
      N = normalize(normal),  L = normalize(light_pos - frag_pos)
      V = normalize(eye_pos - frag_pos),  H = normalize(L + V)
      diffuse  = max(dot(N, L), 0)
      specular = if diffuse > 0 { pow(max(dot(N, H), 0), shininess) } else { 0 }
      atten    = 1 / max(kc + kl*d + kq*d*d, EPS)
      out      = clamp(ambient*base + atten*(diffuse*base + specular*spec_colour), 0, 1)

  with `EPS` and the `shininess` range named, specular carrying its OWN colour rather than
  multiplying the base, lighting never changing alpha, and the clamp applied before the
  transfer-function conversion.

- [x] Lighting has no fixed count. Multiple directional, point and spot lights and an ambient term.
  "One directional and one point light" was a demo's requirement written as a design. Environment
  maps, image-based lighting and shadow maps are `f-ext`.
  DONE 2026-09-14. `scene3d::light` holds the four kinds, the two fall-off equations and the
  SELECTION, which is the part an application cannot get right by itself: the lights whose volume
  reaches a drawable's bounding sphere, ordered ambient first, then directional, then point and spot
  by DESCENDING irradiance at the sphere's centre, ties broken by the order they were added, and
  truncated at the per-drawable limit AT THE END so what is dropped is what matters least. The order
  is part of the contract because floating-point addition is not associative: two implementations
  that accumulate the same lights in different orders produce different colours, and the difference
  is invisible until a conformance comparison fails for a reason nobody can find.
  A cone whose inner angle equals its outer is a HARD EDGE and not a division by zero, and a light
  with a zero source radius, a negative intensity, no direction or an inner angle past its outer is
  refused where it enters.
  `Limits` carries the eight fields the profile names, each with its own refusal, and the structural
  checks are beside them: cycle detection in the hierarchy AND in the pass graph, the dirty flag and
  its traversal order. Every refusal names the limit and both numbers.

  No fixed count is NOT no limit, and this layer needs its own `Scene3DLimits` for the same reason
