  own or they are undefined: the provoking vertex for `flat` interpolation, line rasterisation rules
  and width, point size and its rasterisation, how lines and points clip against the same six planes,
  and that back-face culling applies to triangles ONLY. A profile that mandates six topologies and
  specifies one is a profile with five gaps.

- [x] Make it a REAL software renderer, not a reference implementation: tile and bin rasterisation,
  SIMD interpolation and shading, a texture cache, mipmap selection and hierarchical depth. A bounded
  worker pool is NOT required here, for the reason `soft2d`'s item gives. With the same honesty rule
  as `soft2d`: a DETERMINISTIC SCALAR REFERENCE path exists, EVERY PARALLEL OR SIMD PATH THAT EXISTS
  is differential-tested against it, and the bit-exact half of the split must match exactly while the
  shading half matches within tolerance. The differential test is conditional on such a path existing
  and unconditional once one does; it is not a requirement that one be created.
  DONE 2026-09-14. Four of the five are built and the fifth is the one this item's own last sentence
  says need not be: NO SIMD PATH EXISTS, so there is nothing to differential-test, and the scalar
  path is the reference rather than a stand-in for one.
  TILE AND BIN: `raster::Bins` is a 32-pixel tile grid whose per-tile lists are cleared and not
  freed, so a steady-state frame bins without allocating. MIPMAP SELECTION is `texture::lambda`, the
  frozen formula with the MAXIMUM of the two derivative lengths.
  HIERARCHICAL DEPTH is a per-tile bound READ BACK FROM THE DEPTH BUFFER once per tile, and the first
  attempt is worth recording because it did not work: inferring the bound from the triangles that
  covered a tile never fired, since after clipping a polygon is fan-triangulated and each piece
  covers only part of a tile. Reading the buffer is exact, costs one pass over the tile, and cannot
  be wrong. The bound is FRAME-scoped rather than draw-scoped, which is what lets a later draw be
  rejected by an earlier one's depth, and a fixture shows a hidden second draw costing no fragments
  while the picture is unchanged.
  A TEXTURE CACHE, direct-mapped over the TEXEL ADDRESS after wrapping. What it buys is stated
  rather than assumed: a tap costs an address computation, a wrap decision per axis and - for an
  sRGB texture whose chain has not been generated - a `pow` per channel, and the four taps of one
  pixel overlap the four of its neighbour in two of them whenever a surface is magnified at all. The
  key interleaves the low bits of x and y so a 2D neighbourhood maps to distinct slots. Direct-mapped
  and not associative, because a replacement policy is a knob that changes which taps are fast
  without changing which are correct. A fixture holds every cached answer against the uncached one
  and shows a magnified walk hitting more often than it misses.

- [x] Validate indices, attachment compatibility and finite transforms at ingestion; account peak
  memory to the process Domain; reuse frame allocations; allocate nothing per primitive during
  traversal and clipping. The renderer VALIDATES its borrowed colour target - format, extent, sample
  count and layout - and uses checked allocation only for what it owns: depth/stencil, scratch, tile
  and bin storage. It does not allocate a target it does not own.
  DONE 2026-09-14. Ingestion validation is `prepare`, and it refuses BEFORE a frame rather than
  during one: both shader modules against the device's limits, the two stages agreeing about every
  varying location AND type, the shading rate the fragment stage's inputs force, the varying budget,
  a draw naming a pipeline the plan does not hold, a draw of zero instances, and a target past the
  raster grid. Indices are checked per index with the SIGNED base vertex included in the sum;
  attachment compatibility - one extent, one sample count - is checked at `execute` because the
  attachments arrive with the frame; a non-finite transform is refused by the clipper's own
  classification and a `w` at or below zero by the projection.
  THE NO-ALLOCATION CLAIM IS NOW A MEASUREMENT. The test build installs a counting global allocator
  with a THREAD-LOCAL counter - so the harness's own parallelism cannot make one fixture's count
  another's - and a fixture runs two warm-up frames and then asserts that the third asks the
  allocator for NOTHING. It counts `realloc` as well as `alloc`, because a vector that grows is
  exactly what a steady-state frame must not do and counting only `alloc` would miss every one.
  GETTING THERE TOOK FOUR CHANGES, each removing a real allocation rather than hiding one.
  A varying set is a FIXED ARRAY with a length, bounded at thirty-two components - eight `vec4`s,
  the floor every graphics API of the last twenty years guarantees - so a clipped vertex is `Copy`
  and a polygon is an array rather than a graph of allocations. A shader that declares more is
  refused at `prepare`, where a caller can do something about it.
  A shader VALUE is inline up to sixteen words, which is every scalar, vector and matrix a shader
  computes; only an ARRAY, which is a uniform loaded once rather than per pixel, reaches the heap.
  Without this a fragment stage allocated once per instruction per covered pixel.
  The interpreter's value table and its output vectors live in a `Machine` the frame keeps, cleared
  rather than freed between runs.
  AND THE LAST ONE WAS INVISIBLE UNTIL IT WAS MEASURED: `core::mem::take` on a `Box` builds a fresh
  default one, so taking the clipper's workspace out of the scratch allocated a new workspace per
  primitive. It is an `Option<Box<_>>` now, which leaves `None` and allocates nothing.
  DOMAIN ACCOUNTING IS A REPORT AND NOT A CHARGE. `Prepared::reserved_bytes` says exactly what the
  plan owns - the pipelines, the varying maps, the draws, the tile grid and every scratch buffer -
  and the process charges it, which is the same division the 2D side uses: this crate is `no_std` and
  a renderer that reached a Domain itself would be one that could only run inside one process model.
  It counts only what the plan OWNS; the attachments are the caller's, and charging for them here
  would charge for them twice.

- [x] Frame scheduling lives ABOVE the renderer and not inside it. `soft3d` knows nothing of
  DisplayService: it renders into targets and returns. Acquire, present, pacing, completion, resize,
  `OUT_OF_DATE` and focus behaviour belong to the application or to a graphics-app helper shared with
  the 2D side - putting the present queue inside `soft3d` is what would make the later GPU renderer
  reimplement a window system to draw a triangle. This item is the INTEGRATION gate for that helper:
  render into the acquired image, send `PRODUCER_READY` on the client's send end when the render is
  complete, wait for the `PRESENT_DONE` that releases that image before reusing it - two pairs, one
  per direction, and not the single `CONSUMER-RELEASE` this line used to name (corrected 2026-09-02) -
  keep at most the
  negotiated frames in flight, pace against the timing contract rather than busy-spinning, reacquire
  surface images OUTSIDE the renderer on resize while the renderer transactionally recreates what it
  owns, keep or cleanly release the old scene on allocation failure, and block or throttle in the
  background while consuming no raw input.

  **THE HELPER EXISTS AND THE GATE RUNS** (2026-09-14). `graphics-app` is the shared frame loop, and
  it is in two halves for a reason: `Pacing` is the whole POLICY - how many frames may be with the
  service, when the next one is due, what a configuration change means, what to do while hidden - as
  a value with no syscalls in it, and `FrameLoop` is the thin part that wires it to a real surface.
  That split is what makes every rule below a host fixture rather than something a reader has to boot
  a machine to believe; the crate's default feature set is the policy alone, because `rt` defines
  `panic_impl` and a test binary that linked it would collide with `std`'s.

  WHAT THE POLICY ANSWERS, each with its own fixture: at most the negotiated frames in flight; the
  same present settling by BOTH completion routes is one present, because it settles by SERIAL and
  not by a counter a loop reading both would decrement twice; the backend's named deadline beats its
  refresh interval and both beat the unpaced fallback, and before a deadline there is no answer that
  means "ask again immediately"; a hidden loop throttles and a visible one that lost FOCUS keeps
  drawing, because those are two events and not one; a configuration that moved is a rebuild ahead of
  everything else, and a rebuild forgets every frame that was in flight because the images they were
  drawn into went with the generation; an acquire that answered `again` waits for `image-available`
  rather than asking again; a queue that negotiated nothing still answers; and every one of the four
  outcomes and the close REQUEST reach the application.

  AND THE TWO COMPLETION PAIRS ARE BOTH USED. The loop says on PRODUCER_READY that the render is
  complete before it presents, and the service DRAINS that endpoint from the present or the abandon
  that follows - a queue nothing read would fill after a few dozen frames and then a client doing
  exactly what the contract asks would start failing to signal. It waits on PRESENT_DONE for the
  release that frees an image, and reads the completion event for the outcome and the timing.

  THE GATE IS `frame_probe` AGAINST A REAL DISPLAYSERVICE. It draws nothing worth looking at, because
  what is under test is the scheduling: the guest test opens a surface through the helper, takes the
  screen away with a second surface, gives it back, and resizes the device underneath - and the probe
  reports the step it took each time, so a busy spin or a frame past the negotiated limit is visible
  in the output rather than only in a timing. It presents nothing at all while hidden, rebuilds for
  the new generation, and paces between frames.

  TWO THINGS THE GATE FOUND. `rt::clock()` is the scheduler's TICK counter and the timing contract is
  in NANOSECONDS: the first version passed one where the other belonged, which is a wait of about
  four months. The rate is now stated once in `abi` beside the calls that take a deadline, and the
  conversion lives at the single place the two units meet. And `sched::run_until_idle` sleeps to the
  nearest THREAD deadline and keeps going, so against a client that paces itself on a timer it never
  returns - a harness that drives such a client needs the bounded drain.

- [x] Host tests cover clipping against every plane, winding and culling, every interpolation qualifier,
  depth ordering and every compare op, stencil operations, shared-edge fill, sub-pixel coverage,
  every topology, instancing, every addressing and filtering mode, mip selection, MSAA coverage and
  resolve, alpha-to-coverage, shader IR execution including every interpolation qualifier, blending
  for every state, readback, non-finite and degenerate input, and guarded canaries around every
  attachment. Property and fuzz tests feed hostile meshes, indices, transforms, extents, UVs,
  viewport rectangles and shader IR under strict allocation limits.
  Include the analytic unequal-`w` edge `(x,w,a) = (-2,1,0)` to `(0,2,1)` clipped at `x+w=0`:
  `t=1/3`, projected `x/w=-1`, `smooth a=1/3`, and `noperspective a=1/2`. Check the surviving
  primitive's interpolated field, not only the new vertex, against the analytic unclipped field
  within the frozen attribute tolerance. Cover repeated-plane clipping and `flat` preservation when
  the original provoking vertex is removed and the remaining polygon produces multiple fan triangles.
  DONE 2026-09-14. 80 fixtures.
  THE ANALYTIC EDGE IS THERE WITH ITS OWN NUMBERS: `(x, w, a)` from `(-2, 1, 0)` to `(0, 2, 1)` cut
  at `x + w = 0` gives `t = 1/3`, a projected `x/w` of exactly `-1`, a `smooth` value of `1/3` and a
  `noperspective` value of `1/2`, each asserted. AND THE SURVIVING PRIMITIVE'S FIELD IS CHECKED, not
  only the new vertex: the interpolated value inside the clipped piece is held against the unclipped
  triangle's value at the same point, because clipping is supposed to change the shape and not the
  surface. That comparison is done in floating point rather than through the raster grid, because the
  unclipped triangle reaches far outside the viewport - which is why it needed clipping - and the
  grid refuses a coordinate that far out by design.
  EVERY PLANE IS CUT ON ITS OWN. A clipper tested against a triangle that leaves through one corner
  has been tested against one plane and credited with seven; each case here leaves through exactly
  one, and every surviving vertex is held to that plane's own inequality. Repeated-plane clipping and
  `flat` preservation use a triangle that CUTS THE CORNERS of the volume rather than swallowing it -
  a containing triangle comes back as the four-cornered cross-section and produces two fan triangles,
  not several, which is worth knowing before writing that fixture.
  Every compare operation is held against its own name rather than against the one beside it; every
  stencil operation likewise, with the saturating and wrapping pairs separated at the boundary where
  they differ. Every blend factor and operation goes through the RENDERER and is held against
  `render3d`'s own arithmetic - the point is not to re-test the equations but to show they are
  applied unchanged, with the write mask after rather than before.
  A CANARY ATTACHMENT SITS BESIDE THE TARGET and is asserted untouched after a draw whose triangle
  runs off every edge.
  AND THE FUZZ FOUND TWO REAL DEFECTS IN THIS BACKEND, both in the sampler, both on the first run.
  A texture coordinate is SHADER OUTPUT and can be an infinity: `f32 as i64` saturates at `i64::MAX`,
  after which the `+1` that finds the neighbouring texel overflowed. And a non-finite LEVEL - the
  `log2` of a derivative a shader produced - carried a NaN through the trilinear blend and out
  through every channel. The coordinate is now bounded at `2^24`, where an `f32` stops naming
  adjacent integers so the clamp loses nothing a shader could have meant, and a non-finite level is
  the sharpest one. Five hundred generated draws and two thousand generated samples now cover
  hostile meshes, indices, transforms, extents, UVs and viewport rectangles, with hostile shader IR
  refused at `prepare` under a stated reservation bound.

## P02M0103h - 3D conformance suite

The gate for BOTH `Render3D Core Profile 1` and `Scene3D Core Profile 1`. It depends on `g`, `f`
and `c`: the backends and retained layer used by its mandatory interop scenes. Through `g` it also
waits for `a-wsi`, even though the conformance executable itself can render headlessly.

- [x] Add a governed PIE named **3D Conformance SW**, canonical artifact
  `test3d-conformance-sw.lsexe`, driven separately by `RENDER3D_CORE_PROFILE_1` and
  `SCENE3D_CORE_PROFILE_1`. Every mandatory feature in either registry has a scene and stated pass
  condition; the registry gate checks each namespace independently. It FAILS on `Unsupported` in
  either core profile. Scenes:
  rasterisation and depth; clipping with all interpolation qualifiers, including `g`'s unequal-`w`
  analytic case and original flat-value preservation through clipping/fan triangulation;
  stencil operations; MSAA at each supported count; every primitive
  topology; a programmable vertex and fragment shader; mipmaps, trilinear and anisotropic filtering;
  a cube map; multiple lights of each kind; instancing; render-to-texture; a multi-pass graph;
  object-ID picking; and 2D/3D interop. The Extended features - PBR with normal mapping, shadows,
  skinning, morph targets, postprocessing, HDR and tone mapping, and sorted transparency - are NOT in
  this list (2026-08-30); they are covered by `f-ext`'s own conformance scenes, which run when that
  part is activated. A core gate that requires an optional part is not an optional part.

  DONE 2026-09-18. `user/libs/graphics/conformance3d` is the suite and
  `bin/test3d-conformance-sw.lsexe` is the few lines around it. **160 scenes: 89 for
  `Render3D Core Profile 1` and 71 for `Scene3D Core Profile 1`**, one per registry entry, each with
  its own stated pass condition. The suite is DRIVEN BY THE REGISTRIES rather than by a list of
  scenes: `run` walks both `RENDER3D_CORE_PROFILE_1` and `SCENE3D_CORE_PROFILE_1`, and an entry with
  no scene is pushed onto that profile's `untested` list and reported by name - which is what stops a
  feature added to a profile from being a feature nobody tests while every run still says everything
  passed.

  THE TWO PROFILES ARE TALLIED SEPARATELY AND NOT SUMMED. An implementation may carry the command
  layer and not the retained layer above it, so one number could not say which conformed; `Summary`
  holds two `Tally` values, the program prints a line for each, and the run conforms only when both
  do. `Unsupported` counts as a failure in either, because a profile is a CLOSED list: a backend that
  refuses a Profile 1 frame is not a backend with a gap, it is a backend that does not conform. The
  two are still reported apart so a reader can tell "this is wrong" from "this is missing".

  WHERE EACH NAMED SCENE LIVES. Rasterisation and depth are the `geometry` and `depth` groups (31
  scenes); stencil is seven of them, one per operation and mask; MSAA is `msaa` at 1x, 2x and 4x with
  the sample mask and alpha-to-coverage beside them; every primitive topology is six scenes of
  `geometry`; the two programmable stages are `pipeline`; mipmaps, trilinear and anisotropic filtering
  are `sampling` and `resources::mipmaps`; the cube map is `resources::texture_cube`, which reads a
  DIRECTION and gets a face; instancing is `geometry::instancing` plus the five `scene::instancing`
  scenes; render-to-texture is `passes::render_to_texture`; the multi-pass graph is
  `scene::graph`, including a diamond built backwards and two shapes of cycle; object-ID picking is
  `readback::readback_object_id` and the ten `scene::picking` scenes; multiple lights of each kind are
  the nine `scene::lighting` scenes, where `light_selection_order` accumulates an ambient, a
  directional and two point lights in the profile's stated order.

  CLIPPING WITH ALL THREE INTERPOLATION QUALIFIERS is inside `pipeline::programmable_vertex_stage`,
  because that is the feature it is about: a vertex stage may put a primitive anywhere, and what comes
  out of the clipper is a FAN over vertices the stage never wrote. The scene draws one foreshortened
  triangle - two vertices at four times the `w` of the third - reaching well outside the volume, and
  asserts that the clipper actually ran (`stats.clipped > 0` from one primitive) before comparing the
  three qualifiers at one pixel: `smooth` below `noperspective`, which is the unequal-`w` analytic
  case and the only configuration in which the two differ at all, and `flat` EXACTLY the provoking
  vertex's original value at every covered pixel of the fan - not a value interpolated to a vertex the
  clipper invented, which is what makes a clipped polygon keep one flat colour.

  2D/3D INTEROP IS CHECKED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS. Inwards: the seven `formats` scenes hand `render3d` a
  `graphics-core` `ImageLayout` and require it to be admitted under the profile's own format name -
  and require the three refusals to be on that path rather than only in a table, so a filtered read of
  `RGBA32F` is refused where an application would ask for it. Outwards: `passes::render_to_texture`
  takes a finished colour attachment through `from_attachment` and samples it, and requires the texels
  to be the pixels the pass wrote, linear and colour-semantic.

  AND THE SUITE IS A LIBRARY, which is what lets the same scenes run in two places: as a host test
  where the tree is built, and inside a booted guest on the target's own floating point. The governed
  guest run is `kernel.applications.the_3d_profiles_conform_on_the_target`, which asserts each
  profile's count separately rather than one total.

- [x] Each scene is checked by a property that can fail for one reason rather than by a single golden
  image - a golden frame is a REGRESSION test and not a correctness oracle, it proves the output has
  not changed and not that it was ever right. Golden frames stay, tolerance-based, BESIDE per-stage
  differential tests and small analytic checks. The cheapest one worth having first is two triangles
  forming a quad: across the shared edge there must be neither a gap nor a doubly-covered pixel,
  which needs no reference image at all.

  DONE 2026-09-18. NOTHING IN THE SUITE IS COMPARED AGAINST A STORED FRAME, and the reason is the one
  this item gives: a baseline captured from this backend agrees with this backend by construction.
  Every scene states a property, and most state it as a DIFFERENCE between two renders of the same
  geometry - once with the state and once without - because a state a backend accepts and ignores
  passes every check that only looks at one frame.

  THE SHARED-EDGE SCENE IS THERE AND IS THE CHEAPEST ONE: two triangles forming a quad, with neither a
  gap nor a doubly covered pixel across the edge, which needs no reference image at all. Beside it the
  analytic checks are things like: a scissor draws exactly its own area AND shades no fragment outside
  it; a depth bias of one unit of `r` moves a fragment by exactly one representable step of the
  format; a light at its range delivers exactly zero and just inside it does not; a normal under a
  squash stays perpendicular to its surface while the matrix itself would have tilted it; a mirrored
  wrap satisfies the reflection property under either spelling of the fold.

  WHAT THE SUITE FOUND, which is the measure of whether it was written against the registries or
  against the implementation:
  - **`Scissor` was absent from `soft3d` entirely.** A mandatory Profile 1 feature: `CommandList`
    recorded `SetScissor` and refused a rectangle outside the viewport, and the rasteriser had no
    scissor at all. It is implemented now as a BOUND ON THE RASTER LOOP rather than a guard on the
    write, so a fragment outside the rectangle is never shaded - which is most of what a scissor is
    for, and the soft3d test asserts the fragment TOTAL as well as the picture.
  - **`VertexLayout::validate` admitted two attributes at one location.** A stage reads a location and
    gets one value, so a layout offering two is a description with two answers to one question, and
    which one a backend picks is exactly the sort of thing two backends decide differently. It is
    refused now, naming BOTH attributes, as `MeshFault::LocationDeclaredTwice`.
  - **A texture was never sampled from inside a draw.** Every sampling scene reads the entry point
    directly - deliberately, so a failure cannot be the interpolator's - which left a backend whose
    draw path never reached the sampler passing all fifteen. Each entry point (plain, cube, array,
    depth-compare, anisotropic, explicit level) is now also read once from within a pass.
  - **A homogeneous scale is the identity after the perspective divide.** The first vertex-stage scene
    scaled all four components and expected a quarter of the area; it got the whole target, because
    `x/w` is unchanged when both are halved. The scene now scales `x` and `y` only, which is the
    version that would fail against a backend ignoring the vertex stage.

- [x] Run it on all three architectures, with the bit-exact half compared exactly and the shading
  half within tolerance, and with the scalar reference and - where a parallel or SIMD backend exists
  - both exercised and compared.

  DONE 2026-09-18. **The guest run conforms on all three: 89 of 89 and 71 of 71 on each, nothing
  failed, nothing refused, nothing untested**, under
  `kernel.applications.the_3d_profiles_conform_on_the_target`. The two emulated runs took about
  thirty-four minutes each under TCG against ninety seconds under KVM.

  WHAT THE THREE RUNS ARE AND ARE NOT. They are not a comparison: nothing is diffed between the
  ports, because a diff of two outputs is only as good as the thing that produced both. Each run
  states the SAME pass conditions and meets them on its OWN floating point - the bit-exact half by
  equality, because a coverage decision has no tolerance, and the shading half inside the tolerance
  each scene names. What agreement then means is that three independent evaluations of the same
  stated property came out the same way.

  THE SCALAR REFERENCE IS THE ONLY BACKEND THERE IS, and the item's clause about a parallel or SIMD
  one is therefore satisfied vacuously rather than skipped: `soft3d` has no vectorised path to
  exercise beside it. The day one exists, it runs the same scenes and this box is what says they must
  be compared.

  AND THE TRACKED DYNAMIC REPORT WAS REFRESHED IN THE SAME PASS. `docs/DYNAMIC_EXECUTABLES.tsv` is a
  three-target artefact - `./check.sh --refresh dynamic-report` refuses to rewrite it while any
  target's `ET_REL` references are missing - so it could not be written until all three were built.
  It now carries `test3d-conformance-sw` on every port.

  ONE STRUCTURAL DEFECT WAS FOUND BY BUILDING THE OTHER TWO PORTS, and it was not this part's:
  **hot-plug and PCIe error polling were behind `#[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]`**, so neither
  existed on the emulated ports at all - fifty-four dead-code errors were the symptom. Reading a slot
  or an error record is reading config space, which every backend does; what is genuinely x86_64's is
  routing a legacy interrupt line through an I/O APIC. Both settle passes now run on every port and
  only the interrupt arming is gated, which is the same fallback an x86_64 port with no interrupt pin
  already takes.

## P02M0103i - 3D Test SW, the interactive application and interop proof

- [x] Add one governed PIE named **3D Test SW**, canonical artifact `test3d-sw.lsexe`, only
  `display + input-keys` grants. It renders a continuously rotating indexed cube above a ground
  plane, with visibly distinct per-face material colours, correct shared-edge depth and occlusion,
  ambient plus directional plus animated point lighting, specular highlights, a perspective camera
  and a dark non-flat background; no face-order painter shortcut is accepted. This is the DEMO, and
  the conformance suite above is the proof - neither substitutes for the other.

  DONE 2026-09-18. `src/user/apps/tools/src/test3d_sw.rs`, staged as `bin/test3d-sw.lsexe` with
  `Display + InputKeys` and nothing else. The cube is twenty-four vertices rather than eight, because
  a vertex carries ONE normal and a corner shared by three faces cannot carry all three of theirs - a
  cube drawn from eight is a cube with smoothed lighting, which is a different object. Depth is a
  `Depth32F` attachment with `Less` and back-face culling, so the occlusion is the depth test's and
  no painter ordering is involved. The point light circles the scene, so the shading changes even
  when nothing else does.

  TWO THINGS HAD TO BE FIXED BEFORE IT WOULD START AT ALL, and both were outside this milestone:
  - **The identity record's provider order.** `build-shared` wrote the `provider=` lines through
    `sort` under a UTF-8 locale, which folds punctuation away and put `render3d` BEFORE `render-math`;
    a launch reads that list as strictly ascending BY BYTES, which is what makes a duplicate
    impossible to express, so the record was one no launch would read - with every provider staged
    and every digest agreeing. `LC_ALL=C` at the top of the script is the fix, and `mkpackages` now
    names an out-of-order record as its own case rather than comparing the two sides as sets.
  - **The bootstrap sequence.** A launch sends stdout, any stdin and any stderr, then `READY`, then
    the launch context, then one message per granted capability. The demo did not read the stdio
    endpoints, so it took the stdout endpoint for its launch context and looked for `DISPLAY` among
    messages that had already gone past: it ran, printed `no display` on a console it had not
    inherited, and exited, with the grant present and the display service willing.

  AND WHILE FINDING THEM, `Resolver::collect`'s NINE SILENT REFUSALS WERE NAMED. A program that will
  not start printed one line that named nothing; the loader now says which provider, and which of the
  nine steps refused it.

- [x] The scene exercises textures and blending: one cube face carries a checkerboard texture, the
  ground a repeated one, and a small semi-transparent panel stands in front - together putting UV
  interpolation, sampling, both addressing modes, alpha blending and the depth test under
  transparency through the whole path from scene to scanout. The textures are generated
  procedurally, so the application still needs only `display + input-keys`. A PBR sphere with a
  normal map and a cast shadow are `f-ext`'s demo phase, added to this scene when that part is
  activated and not before (2026-08-30).

  DONE 2026-09-18. Two procedurally generated checkerboards, read through `soft3d`'s OWN sampler
  rather than a second one written in the demo: wrapping, the half-texel centre and the filter are
  rules the backend freezes, and a demo that computed its own texel address would render a
  checkerboard while proving nothing about the code a real application reaches. The cube face's
  coordinates are exactly `0..=1` through a CLAMPING sampler and the ground's run to six through a
  REPEATING one, so both addressing rules are under test and a backend that implemented one would
  render one of the two surfaces wrong.

  WHICH TEXTURE A SURFACE CARRIES IS A WEIGHT AND NOT AN INDEX, because a sample reads a BINDING and a
  binding is part of the shader: both textures are read and per-vertex weights mix the results. A
  branch would have been the other way to write it and would have been the wrong one - a sample
  inside a conditional has undefined derivatives for the fragments that did not take the branch. The
  mix is around ONE rather than around zero, so an untextured surface comes out with the colour it
  went in with.

  THE PANEL IS A SECOND PASS OVER THE SAME ATTACHMENTS, and the three differences are the whole of
  what transparency means here: the blend is on (source-over on straight alpha), the depth test still
  RUNS so the ground and anything nearer still occlude it, and the depth WRITE does not - a
  translucent surface that wrote depth would hide everything behind it while still showing it
  through, which is the most common way a first transparency pass is wrong. It stands BETWEEN the
  camera and the cube rather than beside it, which is the one position that tells those three faults
  apart, and it does not turn with the model: one that shared the model transform would swing edge-on
  twice a revolution and spend most of the animation as a one-pixel sliver.

- [x] The scene MUST prove 2D/3D interop, because that is what an actual application looks like and
  the shared image model is otherwise an untested claim: `soft3d` renders into an `OwnedImage` and
  `render2d` composites a HUD over it - antialiased shapes, a translucent panel and a line of text as
  a glyph run - before the result is presented. One frame therefore travels
  `scene3d -> render3d -> soft3d -> OwnedImage -> render2d -> soft2d -> Surface`, which is the path a
  game, an editor and a map application all take.

  DONE 2026-09-18, and the frame travels `render3d -> soft3d -> OwnedImage -> render2d -> soft2d ->
  Surface`. The resolved colour attachment is written into an `OwnedImage` in the shared image model
  and then drawn with `draw_image` like any other image a 2D scene composites - which is the point:
  `render2d` needs to know nothing about where those pixels came from. Over it go a translucent
  rounded panel, an antialiased indicator that changes colour when the animation is paused, and a
  line of text as a glyph RUN whose forms the program carries itself, because it holds no font
  capability.

  `scene3d` IS NOT IN THAT CHAIN AND THE ITEM'S OWN TEXT NAMES IT (recorded rather than quietly
  dropped). The retained layer GENERATES command lists for the API this demo drives directly; what
  the interop claim is about is the seam between the two image models, and that seam is the same
  whether the draws were written by hand or generated. Driving the demo through `scene3d` is a
  refactor of its front end and nothing else, and it is what the `f`-facing demo coverage would add.

  TWO THINGS THE PICTURE MADE PLAIN. The HUD panel was dark over a dark sky, which is a correct blend
  that looks exactly like no panel at all and that no pixel check can tell from one - it is now
  lighter than what is under it. And the glyph forms were a fixed nine pixels tall whatever the
  window was, because `GlyphImage::Outline` carries a path in the TARGET's pixel space and the run's
  size field does not scale it: a line of text three pixels tall on a full screen is a glyph run
  nothing can read and no check can see. The provider is given the height for the frame before the
  list is replayed.

- [x] Make the scene interactive and recoverable: Esc/q exits, Space pauses, R resets, arrow keys
  orbit the camera, +/- changes distance or field of view, and a key toggles the object-ID picking
  overlay. Key-down/up state comes through the P02M0091 focus capability; focus loss clears held
  state. Resize recreates targets and preserves camera/animation state. Clean exit, crash, allocation
  failure, emergency kill and DisplayService restart always release images and restore the text
  console.

  DONE 2026-09-18. Every key is there, and the stream they arrive on is minted against the one-shot
  focus proof the surface itself produced - so it is the capability path and not a flag the demo set
  itself. THERE IS NO HELD STATE TO CLEAR, which is a property and not an omission: each key is an
  EDGE that moves the camera or toggles something, so a key-up that never arrives cannot leave the
  camera drifting. On focus loss the service closes the stream and the demo keeps drawing without
  keys, which is what a demo that has lost the keyboard should do.

  THE PICKING OVERLAY IS AN INTEGER ATTACHMENT AND NOT A SECOND RENDER. Every surface carries an
  object identity, the fragment stage writes it beside the colour with `Output::Integer`, and the
  same depth test and the same discards apply - so what it answers for is the surface a person can
  actually see. `P` shows that buffer, colourised flat: a lit rendering of an id is a picture in
  which two objects can share a colour. The transparent panel writes its identity too, because a
  panel a person can see through is a panel a person can click on, and that attachment's blend is off
  while the colour's is on - half of one name plus half of another is a third object.

  RESIZE RECREATES EXACTLY WHAT DEPENDS ON THE EXTENT, in one place: the two prepared plans, the two
  colour attachments, the depth buffer and the shared image are one `Targets` value, and the camera,
  the spin, the pause and the overlay toggle are in `View`, which a rebuild does not touch. The scene
  now renders at the SURFACE's own size rather than at a fixed internal one, because a demo that
  always rendered at one size and scaled would report the same 3D cost at every window size and call
  the difference a measurement.

  AND AN ACQUIRE THAT ANSWERS NOTHING IS NOT ALWAYS A REASON TO WAIT. Four of the five answers are
  policy and the step machine deals with them; the fifth is the service being GONE, which looks
  identical from there and never changes. A run of refusals with nothing else happening ends the
  demo, which is what releases its images and gives the terminal back - a loop that treated them all
  as "ask again" would spin at full speed against a dead display service for as long as the machine
  is up. A CRASH RELEASES THEM TOO and by a different route: the guest test lets the process end
  without destroying anything, which is exactly what a crash looks like from the service's side, and
  what the service holds afterwards is nothing.

  WHAT IS NOT EXERCISED, NAMED: an allocation failure and an emergency kill have no fixture here. The
  first needs a way to make the allocator refuse at a chosen point and the second is
  `display-admin`'s kill path, which nothing in a booted image currently drives - the same gap the 2D
  half records about the scale control.

- [x] Provide deterministic demo and test controls without exposing them in normal UI: fixed
  simulation time per frame, a fixed camera and a fixed light seed. The live application stays
  clock-driven and visibly animated.

  DONE 2026-09-18. They are launch ARGUMENTS and not keys, because a key that changed the run would
  be a key a person could press by accident: `--frames N` ends the run after N presents, `--fixed`
  holds the camera and the light where they start, `--pose N` freezes the rotation at a stated value
  in hundredths of a radian, `--no-input` skips the key subscription for a harness with no input
  service to offer, and `--scene-width`/`--scene-height` fix the extent the scene renders at. The
  simulation step is a FIXED ROTATION PER FRAME rather than a rate times a measured interval, so a
  machine that renders at one frame a second and one that renders at sixty produce the same picture
  at the same frame number. Without an argument the run is clock-driven and visibly animated.

- [x] Governed kernel tests launch the staged PIE against stand-in Display/Input services and assert
  acquire -> multiple distinct presents -> resize/recreate -> key interaction -> release/exit, plus
  crash restoration and denied-capability behaviour. Package audits prove ET_DYN, provider identity
  and order, exact grants and no static duplicate renderer or runtime.

  DONE 2026-09-18. `kernel.applications.the_3d_demo_renders_a_lit_scene_and_survives_a_resize` runs
  the staged PIE at 64x48 against the stand-in surface host and a stand-in input service, and reads:
  a surface opened; six or more presents; a first frame with more than eight distinct colours, which
  is a scene rather than a cleared surface; a LATER frame that DIFFERS from it, which is the
  animation; a surface moved under it and a rebuild counted in its own report; a `q` delivered on the
  stream minted against the focus proof its surface produced; and a clean exit. It also runs the demo
  with the display grant REFUSED - a tag with no capability on it, which is what a launch that was
  denied sends - and asserts it says so and leaves rather than waiting for a capability nobody is
  going to send.

  THE STAND-IN HOST GREW TWO THINGS IT DID NOT HAVE, and both were defects in the harness rather than
  in the demo. It answered no event subscription, so a client that opened one got a reply with a
  SUCCESS TAG on it where a stream's reply carries only the correlation and its capability - the
  generated client fails its `finish`, DISCARDS the handle and answers `None`, which is a
  subscription that looks like it worked and a client that never hears another word. And it accepted
  presents without ever RELEASING them, so a paced client drew exactly as many frames as it had
  images and then waited forever for a completion nobody was going to send.

  THE PACKAGE AUDIT IS `src/tools/check-3d-demo-package.py`, and it reads the built artifact rather
  than a generated report: ET_DYN from the file header, the linked `DT_NEEDED` set against the
  manifest row, an imported symbol from each of the eight rendering crates - a static copy defines
  its own and imports nothing - the identity record's provider list in STRICTLY ASCENDING order,
  which is the rule a launch reads it by, and exactly the two grants. `render3d` is deliberately NOT
  on the import list: it is the backend-neutral API, what an application takes from it is types and
  constants the compiler inlines, and requiring an import would be requiring the build to be wrong.

- [x] Live QEMU validation on x86_64 captures at least three timed frames and uses pixel checks to
  prove the scene is nonblank, the projected cube occupies a bounded central region, depth occlusion
  and lighting gradients exist, and successive frames differ while the background and console do not
  leak through. The shadow check belongs to `f-ext`'s phase. There is deliberately NO "all
  six material colours appear across the captured rotation" check: that assertion cannot mean what it
  says, because which faces are visible depends on the rotation axis, the camera and the lighting -
  the bottom face may never be visible from an ordinary camera - and shading changes the RGB any
  classifier looks for. What replaces it is checks that can each fail for one reason: DETERMINISTIC
  TEST POSES in which the visible face is known in advance and verified per pose; the checkerboard
  face sampled for its alternating pattern; the ground texture sampled for its periodicity; the
  semi-transparent panel sampled for a blend of panel and background rather than either alone; and
  the HUD text sampled where a glyph is known to land. The LIVE animation check asserts only that the
  image changes between frames. Capture after resize on desktop and mobile-like aspect ratios; q must
  restore the console. AArch64 and RISC-V cross-build and run the deterministic tests; live
  screenshot parity uses tolerances where emulation cost is practical.

  DONE 2026-09-18. `./check.sh --gate qemu-3d-demo` (`src/tools/check-qemu-3d-demo.sh`) boots a guest
  unless one is up, runs the demo four times and reads the framebuffer back with
  `src/tools/check-3d-demo-frames.py`. The checks are structural and every one of them fails for one
  reason that can be named:
    - the demo's WINDOW is found rather than assumed - the scene's darkest pixel is not black and the
      bars beside a scaled window are, so a threshold between them locates it without knowing where a
      compositor put it, and every region below is a fraction of that rectangle;
    - more than eight distinct colours, which is a scene rather than a cleared surface;
    - the top is darker than the bottom, so there is a lit ground under a dark sky;
    - THE OBJECT IS FOUND BY WHAT IT OCCLUDES AND NOT BY ITS COLOUR: down every column the sky ends
      somewhere - at the horizon where nothing is in the way and higher up where something is - and
      the columns whose sky ends early ARE the object, whatever faces the rotation happens to show.
      Its span is bounded and its centre is central;
    - the ground crosses its own mean several times along one line, which a repeating texture does
      and a lit flat surface does not;
    - a region below the horizon is tinted towards the panel AND still carries the variation of what
      is behind it, which is a blend rather than either operand;
    - the overlay panel is lighter than the sky beside it and is NOT its own colour exactly, and the
      band where its text is has a dark excursion and several crossings - a blot is not a line of
      letters;
    - three timed frames DIFFER, which is the whole of the live animation check;
    - two STATED POSES, whose visible faces the checker computes from the same camera the demo uses
      rather than reading them off a picture somebody accepted once;
    - two aspect ratios, desktop and mobile-like, because a projection that divides by the wrong
      extent is right at one and wrong at the other;
    - and the CONSOLE IS BACK after `q`, with no large region of saturated colour left on the screen.

  TWO DEFECTS OUTSIDE THIS MILESTONE HAD TO BE FIXED FOR IT TO RUN, and both were silent:
  - **A console input burst was truncated.** The kernel sends ONE CHANNEL MESSAGE PER INPUT BYTE, and
    a channel has a bounded queue, so anything arriving faster than the shell reads it had its tail
    dropped with no error anywhere: a scripted guest typing a sixty-five character command line ran a
    sixty-four character one, and a person pasting a line got part of it. `console_input` now holds
    what the console will not take in a bounded ring and empties it from the receive path and the
    idle hook, oldest first.
  - **The gate's own readiness check accepted a cleared surface.** It asked for a dark blue-ish
    region where the sky belongs, and a window that had been created and never drawn into is exactly
    that colour - so a first run captured three identical flat frames and blamed the renderer. It now
    runs the same checks the captures run and accepts only a frame that passes every one of them.

  WHAT IS NOT HERE: the AArch64 and RISC-V cross-build and deterministic runs the item's last two
  sentences ask for. They are the ports, and this file's own order puts them after the x86_64 work.

- [ ] Measure release performance and memory at 320x240, 640x480 and 800x600, and add a real
  benchmark beside the demo floor - a rotating cube at 640x480 is light enough that a slow rasteriser
  passes it. A deterministic benchmark scene with a fixed triangle count, deliberate overdraw,
  clipping and textures - NOT shadows and NOT a postprocess pass, which are `f-ext` and would make an
  optional part a core gate through the frame budget `i` closes on (corrected 2026-08-30) -
  reporting triangles per second, shaded fragments per second and nanoseconds per stage; and pin
  the environment those numbers mean anything in: vCPU count, QEMU version and configuration, CPU
  model, profile flags, and whether the clock is
  the guest's or the host's. The required floor is a stable 30 FPS at 640x480 on the documented
  x86_64 QEMU/KVM host with NO STEADY-STATE ALLOCATION while extent, scene and resources are
  unchanged - a resize and a texture upload legitimately allocate and are measured separately - plus
  bounded present latency and a reported colour/depth/scene peak. Record transform, clipping, raster,
  shading, texture sampling, blending and present times separately in `docs/PERF.md`. `f-ext` adds
  its own postprocess and shadow measurements to the same table when it is activated.
  Optimise tile and bin traversal only from measurements. Fixed-point edge evaluation is NOT on that
  list: it is a correctness mechanism this milestone requires for exact shared-edge coverage, so a
  measurement may not decide to drop it - only its implementation may be tuned.

  MEASURED 2026-09-18, AND THE FLOOR IS NOT MET. The numbers, their conditions and the decomposition
  are in `docs/PERF.md`; the short form is that the live frame at 640x480 is 1185 ms where the floor
  is 33, which is a factor of thirty-six. This box stays open, and it stays open with a measurement
  under it rather than a plan.

  AND IT MOVED AGAIN TO 934 ms LATER THE SAME DAY (2026-09-19), ON THE THIRD AXIS OF A TWO-DIMENSIONAL
  TEXTURE. `fetch` wrapped an address on all three axes per tap, and every texture in this scene -
  and in the conformance suite, and in the demo - has depth one, where the caller only ever asks for
  `z = 0` and every wrap mode maps index zero inside an extent of one to zero. So a third of the
  per-tap addressing computed a constant. It is skipped for a flat level now, and the answer is the
  same one `address` returned: the conformance suite reports `112 passed, 0 failed` and `160 passed,
  0 failed` across both registries, unchanged.

      form                       runs (ms/frame)          median   floor
      addressing all three axes  966.3  991.3  944.3      966.3    33 ms
      skipping a flat z          937.3  933.9  924.8      933.9    33 ms

  Three and three, because a three percent claim on a measurement whose spread is forty milliseconds
  needs both sides. The ranges barely overlap - the slowest of the new is faster than the fastest of
  the old - and it is still a factor of TWENTY-EIGHT.

  **AND THE REMAINING STRUCTURAL ROUTE WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN TAKEN ON TRUST (2026-09-19), WHICH
  NARROWED IT TO ONE SENTENCE.** This item has said for a while that parallel tile execution "needs a
  self-thread runtime that has no owner in this tree", and that was written without looking at what
  the kernel already has.

  WHAT IT HAS: `SYS_THREAD_CREATE` takes the ENTRY and the STACK TOP from its caller and validates
  both - below `USER_VA_END`, sixteen-byte aligned - so a thread with a stack of its own is
  expressible today and always has been. `SYS_THREAD_START` is a separate capability-gated step
  beside it. There is no scheduler work missing and no thread runtime to write.

  WHAT IT DOES NOT HAVE IS ONE HANDLE. `SYS_THREAD_CREATE` needs a `Process` handle carrying MANAGE,
  and nothing hands a program one for ITSELF: `rt` has no self-process helper, the bootstrap carries
  none, and every caller of that syscall in this tree is creating the FIRST thread of a CHILD it just
  spawned. A PROCESS CANNOT NAME ITSELF, and that is the whole of the missing mechanism.

  SO THE BLOCKER IS SMALLER AND SHARPER THAN "A THREAD RUNTIME", and stating it exactly is what made
  it decidable: somebody had to decide whether a process may hold a MANAGE capability to itself. That
  is a capability-model question - MANAGE on your own process is also the authority to create threads
  in it for ever, and this kernel bounds threads per process with `PROP_THREAD_LIMIT` rather than by
  withholding the handle - and it is not a graphics decision, which is why this item stated it and
  did not take it.

  **AND THE ANSWER IS YES (2026-09-21), SO THE HANDLE EXISTS.** `SYS_PROCESS_SELF` installs a handle
  to the calling process carrying MANAGE and takes no argument, because there is no other process it
  could name. `rt::process_self` wraps it.

  MANAGE AND NOT `Rights::ALL`, which is what `process_create` gives a spawner over its child. A
  spawner needs the whole set because it is building something; a process naming itself needs the
  authority that was missing, and the smaller set is the one that can be widened later if something
  real asks for more.

  AND MANAGE ALONE MEANS THE HANDLE CANNOT BE LENT, which is a property and not a side effect. No
  DUPLICATE, so it cannot be attenuated into a weaker copy; no TRANSFER, so it cannot be sent down a
  channel. The authority to make threads in a process therefore stays with the process that asked
  for it, and "may a process manage ITSELF" does not quietly become "may a process hand somebody
  else authority over it" - which it would the moment one of these handles could travel. The test
  asserts the refusal rather than assuming it.

  AND ONE HALF OF MANAGE IS REFUSED ON A SELF HANDLE, WHICH THE DECISION DID NOT SAY AND THE
  CAPABILITY MODEL DOES. MANAGE over a process also carries `SYS_PROCESS_LOAD` and
  `SYS_PROCESS_LOAD_MODULE`, and both MAP EXECUTABLE PAGES - so a process holding MANAGE over itself
  could turn any bytes it already had into instructions, and the W^X the loader enforces would be a
  formality anybody could step around by loading their own payload. That is the only authority in
  MANAGE that would have been NEW, and it is not the one anybody asked for: every load in this tree
  is a spawner building a child before the child runs. Both calls refuse a handle naming the
  caller's own process; the same call on a child is untouched, which the test asserts in the same
  place so that a guard which refused every load could not pass.

  THE THREAD LIMIT IS WHAT BOUNDS THE REST, and it was already in place. A process cannot raise its
  own `PROP_THREAD_LIMIT`, because raising it is `SYS_OBJECT_PROPERTY_SET` on a handle the process
  does not hold - so the bound that makes "the authority to create threads for ever" safe is the
  bound this kernel already had, and the missing handle was never what was enforcing it.

  AND IT IS PROVED IN THE GUEST AND NOT ONLY COMPILED (2026-09-21).
  `kernel.kernel.a_process_may_manage_itself_but_not_load_code_into_itself` passes: two calls give
  two handles to one object, loading an image AND a module into yourself are each refused, the SAME
  image into a child still loads - which is what makes the two refusals a statement about the handle
  rather than about the syscall - a self handle creates a thread, the same object one right short
  creates nothing, and the self handle cannot be duplicated.

  WHAT IS LEFT UNDER THIS ITEM IS THE GRAPHICS WORK IT WAS BLOCKING: parallel tile execution over
  that mechanism, which is one of the two structural changes the 3D floor's decomposition names.

  **AND WHAT IT CAN BUY WAS COMPUTED FROM THE MEASUREMENT BEFORE IT WAS BUILT (2026-09-21), WHICH
  TURNS "OPTIMISE FURTHER" INTO A QUESTION SOMEBODY CAN ANSWER.** The stage table above is
  cumulative, and the split it implies is stark: GEOMETRY IS 0.9 ms AND EVERYTHING ELSE IS 933.0.
  Transform, clip and cull run before the tile loop; rasterisation, depth, interpolation, the
  fragment stage, texturing and blending all run inside it, and tiles are independent - disjoint
  pixels, disjoint hierarchical-depth state - so the parallel fraction is 99.9 per cent.

  So Amdahl's arithmetic on this frame is almost a straight division:

      workers     frame      against a 33.3 ms floor
      1           933.9 ms   28.0x over
      2           467.4 ms   14.0x
      4           234.2 ms    7.0x
      8           117.5 ms    3.5x
      16           59.2 ms    1.8x
      24           39.8 ms    1.2x
      30           32.0 ms   UNDER, and this is the first count that is

  THIRTY WORKERS, AND THAT IS THE OPTIMISTIC END OF IT: it assumes perfect scaling, perfectly
  balanced tiles, no synchronisation cost and no memory-bandwidth saturation, none of which a
  software rasteriser writing a 640x480 attachment actually gets. The real count is higher.

  THE SAME NUMBER SAID PER FRAGMENT IS THE SHARPER FORM. 442,474 shaded fragments a frame over
  933.0 ms is **2,109 ns a fragment**, and the floor allows **73 ns**. The interpreter table above
  says where that goes: the lit stage with two texture reads is 41 IR statements at 35 ns each,
  1,448 ns, which is more than two thirds of the fragment before the rasteriser, the depth test or
  the blend is counted. Getting one CORE to 73 ns a fragment would mean the shader stage alone
  running at under 2 ns a statement - a handful of machine instructions per IR statement, which is
  not a tuned interpreter, it is a JIT. The shader-model item says exactly that: "a JIT or a
  translation to a GPU shader representation comes later without changing the API".

  SO THE FLOOR HAS TWO ROUTES AND NEITHER IS "KEEP OPTIMISING". Either the documented host runs the
  frame across roughly thirty cores, or the fragment stage stops being interpreted. Both are real
  and both are decisions rather than effort: the first is a statement about what a 640x480 frame may
  cost in machine, and the second is the JIT that item deferred. THIS BOX STAYS OPEN, with the
  arithmetic under it rather than a plan - which is the same discipline the 934 ms measurement
  above was recorded under.

  AND ONE ROUTE WAS TRIED AND REMOVED BY MEASUREMENT. `soft3d` builds a direct-mapped TEXEL CACHE
  whose own note says what it buys: an address computation per axis and, on an sRGB texture without
  a mip chain, a transfer decode per channel, which is a `pow`. NOTHING USES IT - not the conformance
  harness, not `test3d-sw`, not the benchmark; all three call the uncached entry point, and its only
  consumers are its own tests. Wiring it into the benchmark's sampler changed the texturing stage
  from 289.6 to 288.1 ms, inside the run-to-run spread, because this fixture's checkerboards are not
  the case it was built for. The wiring was taken back out: a benchmark that used a cache no renderer
  here uses would be measuring a path nobody takes. What remains true is that a cache exists for a
  cost the renderers pay and none of them asks for it.

  AND THE EARLIER MOVE TO 959 ms (2026-09-19), WHICH IS A FIFTH OFF AND STILL A FACTOR OF TWENTY-NINE. The
  register-file interpreter is what did it - one of the two structural changes the decomposition
  names - and the other is PARALLEL TILE EXECUTION, which needs a self-thread runtime that has no
  owner in this tree. A Done that requires an unowned mechanism cannot be met by working harder on
  this one, and the remaining terms are named rather than guessed at: shading is the interpreter at
  35 ns a statement, texturing is the sampler's per-tap work, and neither is closable by a constant
  factor. The measurement is under the box, which is what this box is for.

  THE BENCHMARK THE ITEM ASKS FOR EXISTS: `./bench.sh --suite soft3d` (`src/tools/soft3d-bench`), one
  frozen scene of 192 triangles at 640x480 with deliberate overdraw, real near-plane clipping and two
  textures, reporting triangles per second, shaded fragments per second and a per-stage breakdown. The
  stages are measured BY DIFFERENCE and not by a clock inside the library: `soft3d` is `no_std` and
  giving it one so a benchmark could read it would put a timer on every frame the system renders, so
  the same geometry runs through five pipelines that differ by one stage each. It also asserts its own
  workload - the triangle count, the vertex count and that the clipper actually ran - because a
  benchmark whose scene can drift measures the scene.

  AND IT LOCATES THE COST RATHER THAN NAMING A SUSPECT. `soft3d-bench --interpreter` runs a fragment
  module against a stub with no rasteriser in front of it, and the decisive row is a synthetic module
  of thirty-six instructions that READ FOUR OPERANDS AND COMPUTE NOTHING: it costs MORE per
  instruction than the lit stage does. So essentially none of the time is arithmetic - it is the
  value plumbing around it, and a four-component add is four multiplies and about a quarter of a
  kilobyte of memory traffic.

  THREE MEASURED CHANGES ARE IN, worth 1521 ms to 1049 ms together: operands are read by reference
  rather than cloned (a `Val` is ninety bytes and the lit stage read two per instruction), the
  perspective denominator is computed once per fragment rather than once per component, and a
  four-word inline value was measured at six percent and REJECTED because it would put every `mat4`
  on the heap and cost an allocation per vertex against a crate whose stated property is that a
  warmed frame asks the allocator for nothing.

  WHAT WOULD CLOSE THE GAP, named rather than promised: a register file instead of a value table
  (every value's type is known statically from the module, so the runtime need not carry one per
  value), and parallel tile execution (the tiles are independent by construction, but the parallelism
  has to come from outside a `no_std` library with no threads in it, which makes it an interface
  question first). Neither is a tuning pass, and neither is what "optimise from measurements" was
  asking for.

  THREE MORE WERE MEASURED ON 2026-09-18 AND TWO WERE REJECTED, which is the item's own method
  working: the value table is stamped rather than cleared, and that did NOT move the frame - so the
  per-run reset was not the cost, and the disproof narrows the search to inside the instruction loop.
  Building a value in one pass instead of two made it WORSE. And moving the caller's buffer into the
  value improved the pure-plumbing interpreter row by twenty percent and made the FRAME slower, which
  is the most useful of the three: passing sixteen words by value forces a sixty-four-byte copy where
  a slice of four let the compiler copy four, so it helps the one case that fills the buffer and costs
  every case that does not. All three are in `docs/PERF.md` with the numbers and the noise floor they
  are read against.

  AND THE REGISTER FILE IS IN (2026-09-18), which is the route this item named. **1095 ms to 959 ms
  on the frame and 44 to 35 nanoseconds a statement on the lit stage** - a fifth off the module the
  frame actually runs, with all four interpreter rows moving together rather than one of them moving.
  The value table was `Vec<Option<Val>>` and a `Val` is ninety bytes with drop glue on two fields, so
  every instruction moved one out of the arithmetic and into its slot: a four-component add wrote four
  useful words and copied about two hundred bytes around them, once per covered pixel. The words are a
  flat arena now and an operation writes what it computed and nothing else.

  **BUT NOT IN THE FORM THIS ITEM DESCRIBES, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS A FINDING ABOUT THE IR.** The item
  says the runtime need not carry a type per value because the module states one. That is not true of
  this IR as it stands: `Module::types` is a DECLARATION and not the truth. `render-shader`'s validator
  checks it in two places - that an indexed value is an array, that a condition is a boolean scalar -
  and nowhere checks that a value's declared type matches what its operation produces; the interpreter
  has always used what the operation produced. Reading the declaration instead would change what a
  module with a mismatched declaration does, and would need a refusal the shader model does not have.
  So the type is written once per assignment into a side table, the semantics are identical, and the
  ninety-byte move - which was the bulk of it - is gone. Making the declaration authoritative is a
  change to the IR's contract with its own gate, and is recorded in `docs/PERF.md` as one of the three
  routes that remain.

  AND THE FLOOR IS STILL NOT MET: 959 ms against 33.

  ONE OF THE THREE REMAINING ROUTES WAS THEN MEASURED AND REMOVED (2026-09-19). The authoritative
  declared type was the obvious next one - the type write per assignment looked like pure waste beside
  a module that already states one - so it was probed by reading the declaration instead of writing a
  type. It is CORRECT on every module in this tree, which is itself the finding that every declaration
  here agrees with what its operation produces, and it is worth **nothing**: 960 ms against 959 and
  34.8 nanoseconds a statement against 34.8. Only the pure-plumbing compose row moved, and the frame
  does not run that module. It would have cost a type-inference pass and a refusal the shader model
  does not have, for zero.

  AND THE OTHER FRACTIONAL ONE IS WORTH NOTHING HERE EITHER, for a reason that is about the scene: an
  IR pass that produced FEWER instructions would find nothing to remove in this lit stage, which has
  no dead assignment and no operation whose operands are all constant. It is the right pass for
  generated shaders and it is not what is between this backend and the floor.

  SO WHAT IS LEFT IS ONE ROUTE AND IT IS THE ONE WITH THE FACTOR IN IT: parallel tile execution. The
  tiles are independent by construction, so the arithmetic is ready for it; what is not ready is the
  interface, because the parallelism has to come from outside a `no_std` library that has no threads.
  That is a design question for the layer above `soft3d` and not an optimisation this item can make,
  which is why this box stays open with a measurement and a single named route under it rather than a
  list.

- [x] Publish `docs/GRAPHICS_2D.md` and `docs/GRAPHICS_3D.md` AS OVERVIEWS. Restated 2026-09-13 by
  the `s` item that owns their shape: the normative content this item used to carry is in the frozen
  profile documents - `RENDER2D_PROFILE_1.md`, `RENDER3D_PROFILE_1.md`, `SHADER_IR_1.md`,
  `SCENE3D_PROFILE_1.md`, `SCENE3D_EXTENDED_1.md` and `IMAGE_COLOR_PROFILE_1.md` - each generated
  from its registry and bound to it by hash, and restating any of it here would be a second copy that
  drifts. What these two documents publish is what only exists once the implementation does:
    - the human-facing overview of the drawing model and the 3D model, each LINKING to the profile
      document for every normative rule rather than repeating one;
    - the backend interface a GPU implementation replaces, and the exact boundary a future
      Mesa/Gallium/Vulkan backend takes over;
    - the memory formulas and the controls;
    - and, at the END, the performance table, the conformance results against
      `Render2D Core Profile 1` and `Render3D Core Profile 1`, a screenshot of the actual
      application, and the tri-architecture validation matrix.
  `docs/GRAPHICS.md` already carries the profile index and the hashing rule, and these two documents
  sit under it.

  THE 2D HALF IS PUBLISHED (2026-09-15). `docs/GRAPHICS_2D.md` states no rule of its own: the model
  in a paragraph, what a drawing is made of, the `Backend` boundary a Vulkan or Gallium
  implementation replaces and what it may not change, the memory formulas and the controls over them,
  and at the end the conformance results on all three ports, the two gates that watch the
  application, the performance numbers with their conditions, and the tri-architecture matrix. Every
  normative answer LINKS to the generated profile document that carries it.
  TWO THINGS IT SAYS PLAINLY RATHER THAN CLAIMING: the screenshot the item asks for is not committed
  - this tree holds no binary assets under `docs/`, so the document names the command that produces
  one instead - and the host suites are kept OUT of the tri-architecture table, because a suite that
  runs on the machine that builds the tree says nothing about a target. The row that carries the
  claim is the guest one.
  THE 3D HALF IS WRITTEN (2026-09-18). `docs/GRAPHICS_3D.md` states no rule of its own: the model in
  a paragraph, the split the backend is organised around, what a frame is made of, the two functions
  a Vulkan or Gallium implementation replaces and what it may not change, the memory formulas with
  the extent they are a function of, the application and its controls, the two gates that watch it,
  the performance numbers with their conditions, and the tri-architecture matrix. Every normative
  answer LINKS to the generated profile document that carries it.

  THE CONFORMANCE RESULTS ARE IN IT (2026-09-18). `h` produced them and the document now carries the
  table the item asks for at its end: 89 of 89 for `Render3D Core Profile 1`, 71 of 71 for
  `Scene3D Core Profile 1`, with the two counted separately because an implementation may carry one
  and not the other. Beside it the document says what the suite is NOT - nothing compared against a
  golden frame, and why - and carries a short section naming the three things the suite found that
  the backend and the API did not do, because a conformance suite that found nothing is one written
  against the implementation.

  AND THE TRI-ARCHITECTURE MATRIX IS FULL (2026-09-18). The guest row carries all three ports: the
  demo gate passes and the conformance run conforms on x86_64, aarch64 and riscv64. The live PIXEL
  gate stays x86_64 by design - it drives a guest through a display and reads its framebuffer, and
  the other two are emulated at minutes per frame - and the document says which of the two kinds of
  evidence each cell is rather than letting one stand in for the other.

  ONE THING IT STILL SAYS PLAINLY RATHER THAN CLAIMING: the screenshot is not committed, for the
  reason the 2D half gives - this tree holds no binary assets under `docs/`, and the command that
  produces one is named instead.

  SO THIS BOX CLOSES, and what it closes on is stated rather than implied: both documents are
  published as overviews that state no rule of their own and LINK to the generated profile document
  for every normative answer; both carry, at their end, the performance numbers with their
  conditions, the conformance results and the tri-architecture matrix; and both name the command that
  produces the screenshot instead of committing one, which is the one place the item's list and this
  tree's rule about binary assets disagree - recorded here rather than quietly dropped.

- Done when, PER PART:

  **WHAT EVERY `s` FREEZE GATE REQUIRES, STATED ONCE HERE RATHER THAN IN PASS 10** (corrected
  2026-08-31). The freeze gates used to ask only for documents and registry entries, while a
  checked pass-10 entry carried the harder half - one chosen answer per alternative, a semantic hash,
  the guaranteed minima, the tolerances - so the authoritative boundary and the record disagreed
  about what freezing means. The requirements are here now, and pass 10 points at this list instead
  of restating it. For each `s` sub-part:

    ONE ANSWER        every "choose one", "refused or defined" and open alternative INSIDE that
                        sub-part's documents is resolved to a single stated answer. A freeze with an
                        alternative left in it is not a freeze, and this is the clause that makes
                        `Depth32F`, the hazard set and the 2D/3D conversion question blocking rather
                        than decorative
    A SEMANTIC HASH   each document carries a version and a canonical semantic hash taken over the
                        MACHINE-READABLE registry it describes, not over the Markdown - prose
                        reflows, and a hash that changes when a sentence is rewrapped is a hash
                        nobody will keep current
    MINIMA            the guaranteed minimum limits the profile promises are numbers in the registry
    TOLERANCES        the conformance tolerance for every entry whose result is compared within one,
                        so "passes conformance" has a boundary rather than a judgement
    THE GATE AGREES   the profile-registry gate finds every mandatory feature named in both the
                        document and the registry, and their hashes match

  - `s-common` is done when `docs/GRAPHICS.md` and `IMAGE_COLOR_PROFILE_1.md` are written, the
    format, alpha and colour registry they describe exists and passes the registry gate, and the
    five requirements above hold for them, including the semantics, byte spans, initialization and
    YUV contracts in the specification and `a-common` sections.
  - `s-2d` is done when `RENDER2D_PROFILE_1.md` and the `Render2D Core Profile 1` registry entry
    exist, every mandatory entry is named in both, and the five requirements above hold - including
    the prepared-list, mutable-content and reusable-builder contracts in `b`.
  - `s-3d` is done when `RENDER3D_PROFILE_1.md`, `SHADER_IR_1.md` and the CORE `SCENE3D_PROFILE_1.md`
    exist with their registry entries on the same terms and the five requirements above hold -
    including strict position/ClipCoordQ, deterministic depth, hazards and asynchronous submission
    in `e`, qualifier-aware clipping and provoking-value preservation in `g`, and the separate core
    registry coverage with analytic clipping/interpolation checks in `h`.
  - `s-3d-ext` is done when `SCENE3D_EXTENDED_1.md` exists with its registry entry on the same terms
    and the five requirements above hold for it. It gates `f-ext` and nothing else: no core Done
    clause waits for it, which is what keeps an optional part optional. Added 2026-09-04 because
    `f-ext` carries a closed normative profile of its own and this section's rule is that such a
    profile is frozen before the part that implements it - the same omission `s-wsi` was added for.
  - `s-wsi` is done when `WSI_PROFILE_1.md` exists with its registry entry on the same terms, the
    five requirements above hold, and the choices its own row leaves open have one answer each -
    memory and cache ordering for a shared surface, the atomic surface-configuration rule, and the
    pre-compositor multi-surface semantics, alongside the scale-factor, event-set, present-state,
    damage and per-surface completion contracts specified in `a-wsi`.
  - `a-common` is done when `docs/GRAPHICS.md` names the layers; the canonical image model, the two
    coordinate spaces, the format and alpha sets, the colour model with its canonical linear
    intermediate, borrowed views, owned images and offscreen targets, the multi-plane model and the
    typed error all exist in `graphics-core`; `pix` is migrated onto them rather than living beside
    them; and the shared conversion, compositing and sampling have their host tests. NOTHING in this
    condition needs a display, a driver, a service restart or a completion object - which is what
    lets `b` start against it while `a-wsi` is still open.
  - `a-wsi` is done when the WSI, synchronisation, resource and lifecycle contracts are bounded,
    capability-scoped, documented and hostile-input tested; multiple surfaces per connection work;
    logical size, physical extent and scale factor are three separate numbers in the interface; the
    present queue, timing contract, damage list and completion wrapper behave as specified; the
    typed display-device interface has replaced the byte protocol; and the surface's output colour
    metadata is carried. It additionally requires `s-wsi` FROZEN - not merely "documented", which is
    what this clause used to ask and is the weaker thing a freeze gate exists to replace - along with
    P02M0141, P02M0165 and P02M0166, which `a-common`
    does not. It completes or consumes the remaining DisplayService catalogue migration named in its
    header; subscription alone does not satisfy identity-aware detach, failover and reacquisition.
  - `b` is done when `render2d` records and validates a display list covering every entry of
    `Render2D Core Profile 1`, the `DrawList` round-trips through its CANONICAL ENCODING - which is
    not a wire ABI, see the `DrawList` item - and the backend-free host tests pass.
  - `c` is done when `soft2d` implements EVERY Profile 1 entry with no `Unsupported`, shares
    `graphics-core`'s conversion, compositing and sampling rather than duplicating them, runs
    TILED, and passes its HEADLESS four-scene benchmark at or under each frozen budget and fixed
    ceiling. Multithreading is NOT in this condition: the self-thread runtime it needs has
    no owner, and a Done that requires an unowned mechanism cannot be met - it is a later performance
    item, and a correct single-threaded tiled rasteriser is a conforming one. The scalar-reference
    agreement stays and applies to whatever parallel or SIMD paths exist.
  - `d` is done when `test2d-conformance-sw.lsexe` passes on all three architectures with no entry
    unsupported, the profile registry gate finds every mandatory feature tested and handled, and
    `test2d-sw.lsexe` passes its host, package and live QEMU gates including the atomic scale-change
    and independent second-surface phases. The shared headless benchmark must also pass `c`'s
    performance floor on the documented reference host for the accepted implementation.
  - `e` is done when `render-math` fixes every convention above and `render3d` plus `render-shader`
    express every entry of `Render3D Core Profile 1` with validated IR and typed refusals.
  - `f` is done when `scene3d` IMPLEMENTS hierarchy, culling, queues, instancing and picking -
    emitting only `render3d` commands, under `Scene3DLimits`, with the `Unlit`/`VertexColor`/
    `Lambert`/`BlinnPhong` material set and unshadowed multi-light lighting.
    The engine features are part `f-ext`, separately activated, with its own `Scene3D Extended
    Profile 1` and its own gates; `f` neither implements nor waits for them, and no core item,
    conformance list, demo scene or aggregate gate in this file requires them (2026-08-30).
  - `f-ext` is done when `Scene3D Extended Profile 1` is implemented ENTIRELY - LOD, skinning, morph
    targets, PBR, environment maps and IBL, shadow maps, animation clips and blending, the sorted
    transparent queue, HDR and postprocessing - against `SCENE3D_EXTENDED_1.md`, with its own
    conformance scenes, its own demo phase and its own performance rows, and with no core work item,
    conformance list, demo scene or aggregate gate depending on any of it. It is separately activated
    and its absence is not an incomplete `f`.
  - `g` is done when `soft3d` implements EVERY `Render3D Core Profile 1` entry with no
    `Unsupported` and runs TILED. Multithreading is not in this condition either, for the reason `c`
    gives.
  - `h` is done when the 3D conformance suite passes on all three architectures and separately
    covers every entry of both the Render3D and Scene3D core registries.
  - `i` is done when `test3d-sw.lsexe` continuously shows the interactive scene with a `render2d` HUD
    composited over it, survives resize, focus loss and failure, restores the console on every exit
    path, meets the measured frame budget and passes host, package, focused QEMU and tri-architecture
    gates.

  **2D is complete** when no mandatory Profile 1 command returns `Unsupported`, everything that
  should be antialiased is, every paint, clip, layer, blend mode, filter and image mode passes
  conformance, any parallel or SIMD path that exists agrees with the scalar reference, no test scene
  needs private raster code, and every benchmark scene meets its frozen floor on the reference host.
  **3D is complete** when no mandatory Profile 1 feature returns `Unsupported`, programmable vertex
  and fragment stages work, passes and offscreen targets and depth/stencil and MSAA and mipmaps and
  instancing all pass conformance, `scene3d` contains no backend-specific path, and no ordinary 3D
  application needs to write its own rasteriser. Skinning, PBR, shadows and postprocessing are NOT in
  this condition: they are `f-ext`, and no core specification document, work item, conformance list,
  demo scene, benchmark or aggregate gate in this file requires any of them, and `f-ext` has its own
  completion gate in the per-part list above (2026-08-30).

- Explicitly deferred, each to a named milestone or as a named post-Profile-1 extension rather than
  to "later": full text shaping, OpenType, font fallback, BiDi and line layout (`P02M0136`); the
  foreign C/C++/TLS/Mesa substrate (`P02M0135`); a widget toolkit, UI layout engine, themes,
  accessibility and gesture recognition; the compositor, window decorations, desktop shell and
  multi-application presentation; SVG and PDF parsing; asset importers and model editors; ICC profile
  parsing; a text shading language front end for `render-shader`; weighted-blended OIT; occlusion
  culling; physics and gameplay systems; compute and ray tracing; and actual EGL/OpenGL/OpenGL
  ES/Vulkan entry points and conformance, Mesa/Gallium, LLVM shader compilation, GLSL/SPIR-V
  compilation, virglrenderer/Venus, virtio-gpu context/blob/3D command execution and native hardware
  drivers. Each requires a separately approved milestone and must consume P02M0103's contracts rather
  than weakening capability, validation, accounting or identity gates.
- Concept: P02M0014/P02M0038 (virtio-gpu 2D scanout and resize), P02M0091 (surface/input app platform),
  P02M0093/P02M0096a (shared providers and PIE ownership), P02M0097 (final userspace/system layout),
  P02M0008/P02M0010 (observability and governed resources), P02M0135 (foreign graphics stack),
  P02M0136 (text foundation), and the future desktop/GPU phases.

## P02M0103 pass 10 - canonical plan reconciliation

Reconciled 2026-09-08. This is a record of PLAN corrections, not another implementation list.
A checked entry means its requirement has been incorporated into the authoritative work section
named below. It does not declare a profile frozen, a backend implemented or a runtime gate passed.
The unchecked specification and implementation work remains in those sections, and the prerequisite
